- `lib/messaging/messages.types.ts` — the timer transitions became **mutations**: `TIMER_STARTED`/`TIMER_PAUSED` payloads changed from `{ sessionId }`/`{}` to `Record<string, never>` (the background mints the session id — it owns the timer — and a caller-supplied id would be untrusted anyway, M6.T1), a new `TIMER_RESET` variant was added, and `MUTATION_TYPES` now lists all ten mutation types. `MutationMessage` is now **derived** from `MUTATION_TYPES` (`Extract<RuntimeMessage, { type: (typeof MUTATION_TYPES)[number] }>`) instead of a `BLOCKLIST_*`/`TAG_*` template-literal pattern, so the runtime list and the type can no longer drift apart and `TIMER_TICK` (a passive notification, not a state change) can never leak into the mutation path.
- `lib/messaging/apply-mutation.ts` (new) — the mutation dispatcher extracted from `entrypoints/background.ts` (which was 499 lines, over the §1.2 ≤200 guideline, and now spans two domains). `createMutationHandler(store)` routes the timer commands to the background's **own** timer slice (`startTimer`/`pauseTimer`/`resetTimer`, M2.T1) — which is what schedules/clears the alarm on the background's provider — and keeps the blocklist/tag variants on the existing pure `applyBlocklistMutation` with its same-reference no-op discipline. A timer command whose payload is not empty is rejected without mutating (untrusted input, forward-compatible with M6.T1).
- `lib/messaging/mutation-sender.ts` (new) — the `MessagingDependencies` interface (`sendMutation`) extracted so the timer slice (M3.T3b) and the blocklist slice (M1.T10) share one declaration instead of duplicating it.
- `store/timer.slice.ts` — three new actions `requestStartTimer`/`requestPauseTimer`/`requestResetTimer` that **only** send the corresponding mutation and never touch the local state: the popup's store is read-only (M1.T9), so running `startTimer` locally would mutate only its in-memory copy and schedule the alarm on its own provider. `TimerDependencies` now extends `MessagingDependencies`.
- `components/timer/TimerControls.tsx` — the buttons invoke the `request*` actions instead of `startTimer`/`pauseTimer`/`resetTimer`; the disabled state still derives from the synced status, which is the authoritative one.
- `lib/timer/tick-emitter.ts` (new) — `createTickEmitter({ send, intervalMs, setIntervalFn, clearIntervalFn })` → `{ start, stop, isRunning }`, a dumb periodic clock with the scheduler injected (pure w.r.t. its dependencies, §1.4). `TICK_INTERVAL_MS` reuses `GROWTH_TICK_MS` (2s) so the cadence has a single definition. It makes no decision about whether a tick counts: the background's growth wiring (M2.T15) already ignores a tick that arrives while the session is not `running`.
- `components/timer/use-timer-tick-emitter.ts` (new) — the React hook that starts the emitter while `status === 'running'` and stops it on status change/unmount, tying it to the popup's lifetime. The tick payload carries the last known `remainingSeconds` for observability only; the background measures the elapsed focus time itself and never trusts it.
- `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx` — calls `useTimerTickEmitter()` (M3.T4 keeps the call when it replaces the starter UI).
- `entrypoints/background.ts` — `MESSAGE_TYPES` dropped `TIMER_STARTED`/`TIMER_PAUSED` (they are mutations now, so each type still has exactly one listener) and the local `applyMutation`/`handleMutation` were replaced by `createMutationHandler`.
- Files created/modified: `lib/messaging/messages.types.ts`, `lib/messaging/apply-mutation.ts` (new), `lib/messaging/mutation-sender.ts` (new), `lib/timer/tick-emitter.ts` (new), `components/timer/use-timer-tick-emitter.ts` (new), `components/timer/TimerControls.tsx`, `store/timer.slice.ts`, `store/blocklist.slice.ts`, `store/index.ts`, `entrypoints/background.ts`, `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx`.
- Dependencies added: none (reuses the M1.T6 bus, the M1.T10 mutation transport, the M2.T1 slice and the M2.T15 growth wiring).
- Tests: `tests/integration/messaging/timer-mutation-bus.test.ts` (8 tests, integration with `fakeBrowser` + `FakeAlarmProvider`) — `TIMER_STARTED` moves the background store to `running` and schedules the alarm on the **background's** provider; `TIMER_PAUSED` clears it; `TIMER_RESET` returns to idle with no pending alarm; a duplicate command does not open a second session and the persisted state matches; a command with a non-empty payload mutates nothing and persists nothing; a read-only popup store converges through the M1.T8 bridge; a command + simulated tick sequence grows the city by the expected character count; a tick after a pause command does not grow. `tests/unit/lib/timer/tick-emitter.test.ts` (7 tests, unit with a manual scheduler) — default cadence equals `GROWTH_TICK_MS`; one tick per firing; nothing before `start`; `start` is idempotent; `stop` is idempotent and silences the emitter; restart works; `isRunning` tracks the state. `tests/unit/components/timer/use-timer-tick-emitter.test.tsx` (4 tests, component with fake timers) — no emission while idle; three ticks in 6s while running with the expected payload; emission stops on pause; emission stops on unmount. Updated: `tests/unit/components/timer/TimerControls.test.tsx` (the three click tests now spy on the `request*` actions), `tests/integration/background/background.test.ts` (`MESSAGE_TYPES` 6 → 4, `MUTATION_TYPES` 7 → 10, the `TIMER_STARTED` payload and the new `TIMER_RESET` variant).
- Relevant notes/decisions: **the popup owns the tick emitter** (the acceptance criterion's chosen side). The §1 note allows either side "while the context is active"; the popup is the context that is active exactly when the user watches the city, and it is the only one that can be woken on a 2s cadence at all — an MV3 service worker is suspended between events and `browser.alarms` cannot tick faster than 60s (M1.T5). The known consequence — **the city only grows while the popup is open** — is accepted here and recorded as open issue #9's remaining limitation; the countdown itself is unaffected because it is persisted by the background's alarm (M2.T2). The "why" is documented in the hook's doc comment (§1.4). **The popup-side estimated countdown (`ui.liveRemainingSeconds`) was deferred here and is now implemented by the I001 fix** (see "Bug fixes" below): the field and its selector existed but nothing wrote them, so `TimerDisplay` showed the session's full duration for the whole session. The deferral's recorded justification — that the display "keeps showing the last value the background persisted (updated on each alarm tick / sync)" — was **incorrect**: the session alarm is one-shot, so there is no per-tick write to update from.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — (1) `TIMER_STARTED`/`TIMER_PAUSED`/`TIMER_RESET` are part of `MutationMessage` and handled by the background's mutation path, so the popup's commands change the **background's** timer state and schedule/clear the alarm on the background's provider (closes issue #6); (2) the popup emits `TIMER_TICK` on the 2s cadence while `running` and the background's existing growth/malus wiring consumes it unchanged (closes issue #9); (3) the emitter is tied to the popup's lifetime and stops on unmount, and a tick while `status !== 'running'` neither grows the city nor accrues malus (M2.T15 preserved); (4) the popup-side countdown is explicitly deferred, with the decision recorded here; (5) the integration tests cover the command round-trip and the tick-driven growth.

### M3.T3c — Session completion (`SESSION_ENDED` → history + population)
- `store/store.types.ts` — `TimerStatus` gains `'completed'`. A finished session is deliberately distinct from `paused` (which would look resumable, and `remainingSeconds` is zero so resuming would re-arm a full session) and from `idle` (indistinguishable from "never started", which would leave M3.T4 unable to tell that a session just ended — the card's "`ExportCityButton` visible at session end" criterion).
- `lib/blocking/store-snapshot.ts` — `isTimerStatus` accepts `'completed'`. **Required, not cosmetic**: the content script parses the persisted envelope through this guard, so without it a completed session would make `parseStoreSnapshot` return `{ ok: false }` and the overlay would silently stop working.
- `store/timer.slice.ts` — new `completeTimer()` action: no-op unless `status === 'running'` (a stale alarm firing after a pause/reset must not resurrect a finished state), otherwise it captures the `sessionId` **before** the transition (the other actions clear it), sets `status: 'completed'` with `remainingSeconds: 0`, and clears the pending alarm so the "no pending alarm outside a running session" invariant (M2.T2) stays true.
- `entrypoints/background.ts` — `createGrowthTick` (M2.T15) now also accumulates the running-only elapsed time as `focusedMs` and exposes `getFocusedMs()`. It reuses the **same** baseline transitions as growth (reset on pause, set on resume), so paused gaps are excluded exactly as they are for the city. Sharing the measure is deliberate: the population then always agrees with the city that was actually built, instead of being derived from a second, differently-clocked quantity.
- `entrypoints/background.ts` — new `createSessionCompletion({ store, getFocusedMs, now, send? })` replacing the empty `handlePlaceholder` on `alarmProvider.onFire` (the placeholder function is deleted). On a firing it: (1) ignores it unless the timer is `running` **and** the name equals `timerAlarmName(timer.sessionId)` — the alarm name is the only thing the firing carries, so a stale alarm from a previous session must not complete the current one; (2) computes `population = calculatePopulation(getFocusedMs() / 60_000)` (M2.T18); (3) writes it with `setPopulation` and calls `completeTimer()`; (4) builds the `SessionSummary` (`sessionId`, `score: store.getState().score.level`, `population`, `endedAt: now()`) and records it with `recordSession` (M2.T19); (5) emits `SESSION_ENDED` with that summary through the existing bus. The `send` sink is injectable so the handler is testable without the bus.
- Files created/modified: `store/store.types.ts`, `lib/blocking/store-snapshot.ts`, `store/timer.slice.ts`, `entrypoints/background.ts`, `tests/integration/background/session-completion.test.ts` (new), `tests/unit/store/timer-slice.test.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/blocking/store-snapshot.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none (reuses `calculatePopulation` M2.T18, `recordSession` M2.T19, the M2.T1 slice, the M2.T15 tracker and the M1.T6 bus).
- Tests: `tests/integration/background/session-completion.test.ts` (10 tests, integration with `fakeBrowser` + `FakeAlarmProvider`) — a session run to completion records exactly one history entry with the expected population (derived from the ticks actually elapsed) and `endedAt`, ends in `'completed'` with `remainingSeconds: 0`, and writes the population to the score slice; `SESSION_ENDED` is emitted with the recorded summary; a re-emitted summary for the same `sessionId` replaces the entry instead of growing the history; a paused session records none; a reset session records none; a stale alarm name does not complete the running session; the pending alarm is cleared and the DNR rules are emptied (blocking is on exclusively while running, M2.T7); the completed session is persisted (history + `completed` status survive a restart); a second firing is a no-op; a normal completion logs nothing. `tests/unit/store/timer-slice.test.ts` (+4 tests, unit with `FakeAlarmProvider`) — `completeTimer` transitions/zeroes/clears while keeping the session id; no-op while idle; no-op while paused; no-op while already completed (same reference). `tests/unit/lib/blocking/store-snapshot.test.ts` (+1 test) — the `'completed'` status is accepted by the snapshot parser.
- Relevant notes/decisions: **focus-time source (decision)** — the population is derived from the growth tracker's accumulated `focusedMs` rather than from a new persisted field or from `sessionStartedAt`. This needs no schema change and keeps population and city consistent by construction; the accepted consequence is that a session whose popup was never open has `focusedMs = 0` and therefore population `0` — consistent with the empty city it built (the M3.T3b emitter limitation, issue #9). **Status after completion (decision)** — `'completed'` was added to `TimerStatus`; every existing consumer except the snapshot validator already treats anything `!== 'running'` correctly (DNR rules, the overlay, the tick emitter, `restoreTimer`), so the change is additive. **Score** — the summary carries whatever `score.level` the slice holds; the `distractionRatio` formula remains the roadmap §4 open point (issue #1), so a completed session currently records `'excellent'` unless something set the ratio. **Not completed on restart** — a session that ends while the worker is asleep is still reverted to `paused` by `restoreTimer` (M2.T2) and records nothing; that is existing behaviour, unchanged here. `SESSION_ENDED` is emitted but has no consumer yet: M3.T4 is the intended one (the variant stays registered in `MESSAGE_TYPES`).
- Acceptance criteria: verified — (1) the alarm firing transitions the timer out of `running` and emits `SESSION_ENDED` with a `SessionSummary` (`sessionId`, `score`, `population`, `endedAt`), closing issue #13; (2) the background records it through `recordSession` (newest first, dedup by `sessionId`, capped at 5) and computes/writes the population through `calculatePopulation` + `setPopulation`, closing issue #12; (3) a re-emitted `SESSION_ENDED` for the same `sessionId` replaces the entry instead of growing the history; (4) the integration tests cover a completed session (exactly one entry, expected score/population) and a paused/reset session (none).

### M3.T4 — Popup `App.tsx` — full UI composition
- `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx` — the WXT starter is replaced by the real popup: a tab bar over four sections, one visible at a time. Each section is a small local component that only composes the components owned by the earlier tasks (`TimerSection` → `TimerDisplay` + `TimerControls`; `CitySection` → `CityCanvas` + `ExportCityButton`; `ScoreSection` → `ScoreBadge` + `PopulationCounter` + `SessionHistory`; `BlocklistSection` → `PresetPicker` + `TagEditor` + `SiteList` ×2). A `SECTIONS` record keyed by `PopupTab` maps the active tab to its section, so adding a tab is a compile error until it is wired. The `useTimerTickEmitter()` call (M3.T3b) is kept.
  - **`ExportCityButton` gating**: rendered only when `status === 'completed'`, i.e. after the session has ended and not while it runs — the signal M3.T3c introduced for exactly this criterion.
  - **Two `SiteList` mounts**: the component is generic over the list it edits (M3.T3), so the blocklist and the allowlist are two instances rather than a monolithic two-column widget.
- `components/common/PopupTabs.tsx` (new) — the tab bar, extracted from `App.tsx` to respect the §1.2 size guideline. Presentational: it reads `selectActiveTab` and calls `setActiveTab`. It renders `role="tablist"` with one `role="tab"` per section, `aria-selected`, `aria-controls` and a roving `tabIndex` (only the active tab is tabbable), and implements arrow-key navigation plus Home/End — a `tablist` without it is not keyboard-navigable at all. `TAB_ORDER`, `TAB_LABEL_KEYS`, `tabPanelId` and `tabButtonId` are exported so `App.tsx` wires the `tabpanel` to its tab without duplicating the id convention.
- `store/index.ts` — new `setActiveTab(tab)` action on `UiSlice`, writing the volatile `ui.activeTab` field. It is safe on the read-only popup store (M1.T9) because `ui` is never persisted, so the single-writer rule is not involved. `createAppState` now takes the standard `(set, get, ...rest)` signature so the action can read the current `ui` before writing. The dead `ui.malusAlertVisible` field is **dropped** (the malus alert is the content-script overlay, M2.T8, not a popup element), as the card decided.
- `entrypoints/popup/App.css` + `entrypoints/popup/style.css` — the starter styles are replaced by the shell only (the components carry their own CRT/monospace styles): the reset, the dark `#050805` background and the tab-bar/section layout. The starter's `#root { max-width: 1280px }`, `body { min-height: 100vh }` and `body { display: flex; place-items: center }` are gone, because a fixed viewport would force a scrollbar on the city section (M3.T4 sizing decision).
- `entrypoints/popup/index.html` — the starter `<title>Default Popup Title</title>` becomes `BuildIt — Focus Timer` (it also feeds the manifest's `action.default_title`).
- `locales/en.yml` + `locales/it.yml` — a new `tabs` group (`ariaLabel`, `timer`, `city`, `blocklist`, `score`) added to **both** files, so the M3.T10 drift test stays green. Every string in `App.tsx`/`PopupTabs.tsx` resolves through `i18n.t(...)`.
- `tests/integration/background/dnr-rules.test.ts` — the "a volatile UI change must not re-evaluate the ruleset" assertion used `malusAlertVisible` as its arbitrary volatile field; it now uses `activeTab`, the field that actually exists.
- Files created/modified: `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx`, `components/common/PopupTabs.tsx` (new), `store/index.ts`, `entrypoints/popup/App.css`, `entrypoints/popup/style.css`, `entrypoints/popup/index.html`, `locales/en.yml`, `locales/it.yml`, `tests/integration/background/dnr-rules.test.ts`, `tests/unit/components/common/PopupTabs.test.tsx` (new), `tests/integration/popup/app-composition.test.tsx` (new).
- Dependencies added: none (composes the existing components; no new package).
- Tests: `tests/unit/components/common/PopupTabs.test.tsx` (8 tests, component with Testing Library + `user-event`) — one tab per section with the `tablist`/`tab` roles; only the active tab is `aria-selected`; clicking switches the store's `activeTab`; every tab's `aria-controls` matches its panel id; arrow keys move the selection; the selection wraps at both ends; Home/End jump to the ends; the roving `tabindex` leaves exactly one tabbable. `tests/integration/popup/app-composition.test.tsx` (8 tests, integration with `fakeBrowser` + the real in-process background) — the tab bar and the timer section render by default; one section at a time behind the tab bar; every section is reachable from the single entry point (city canvas, score badge + history, presets + both site lists); clicking Start drives the **background** and the display updates through the M1.T8 sync bridge; a simulated `TIMER_TICK` grows the city; the export button is absent during the session and appears once the session completes; a completed session shows up in the history rendered by the score section; the popup returns to the default tab after a remount (the field is volatile).
- Relevant notes/decisions: **test seam (option A, as decided)** — the integration test starts the real background in-process on the module-level `useAppStore` singleton and renders `<App />` against it, so no injectable-store refactor of the components was needed. Because the singleton uses the *real* alarm provider, the session alarm is fired through `fakeBrowser.alarms.onAlarm.trigger(...)` rather than through `FakeAlarmProvider.advanceBy` (which only drives an injected fake). **Layout verified in a real browser**: the built extension was loaded into Chromium and each tab measured — the intrinsic (max-content) width is 322px for Timer/City/Score and 337px for Blocklist, the 40ch city canvas fits its panel without overflow, and no horizontal scrollbar appears at a 420px viewport. **`App.tsx` is now translated**, which closes issue #17 and resolves the "not modified, M3.T4 owns it" deferrals recorded by M3.T7/M3.T8/M3.T9/M3.T10.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — (1) all sections are reachable and functional from the single entry point (component + integration tests, plus the real-browser check); (2) the tabs layout is driven by `ui.activeTab` through the new `setActiveTab`, the field is volatile so the popup opens on the default tab, and the tab bar is keyboard-navigable with `tablist`/`tab`/`tabpanel` roles (M4.T4 polishes further); (3) the popup is content-sized and does not scroll (measured: 322–337px intrinsic, no horizontal scroll at 420px); (4) the CRT look is kept and the starter's `max-width`/`min-height` are dropped; (5) `ExportCityButton` is visible at session end, not during the session; (6) every `App.tsx` string goes through `i18n.t(...)` with the keys in both locales (drift test green); (7) the component-level integration test starts a timer from the UI and observes both the display and the city state after a simulated tick.

### M3.T5 — `framer-motion` animations on building growth/deletion
- `components/city/CityCell.tsx` — the cell is now wrapped in `framer-motion`: the occupied character renders as a `motion.span` that fades/scales in when the cell becomes occupied (growing → building) and fades/scales out when it is destroyed, under an `AnimatePresence initial={false} mode="popLayout"`.
  - `initial={false}` prevents the whole city from replaying every building's growth on first paint (e.g. after a service-worker restart); only *transitions* (mount/unmount of the occupied character) animate.
  - `mode="popLayout"` takes the exiting character out of the inline flow, so the monospace grid does not shift while the destroyed character fades away, and a blank `.city-cell--empty` placeholder keeps the column width.
  - The character is keyed by a constant (`"building"`), so a character swap (decoration) does not remount the cell: growth and destruction are the only transitions.
- `tests/setup.ts` — `framer-motion` is mocked globally for tests (the roadmap §3.3 explicitly allows mocking/disabling it): `AnimatePresence` becomes a passthrough rendering only the current state and `motion.span` becomes a plain `<span>` with the animation-only props stripped. This makes the rendered DOM deterministic regardless of animation timing, so assertions always observe the settled state. Production still uses the real `framer-motion`.
- Files created/modified: `components/city/CityCell.tsx`, `tests/setup.ts`, `tests/unit/components/city/CityCell.test.tsx` (new).
- Dependencies added: `framer-motion` `^14.0.0` (building growth/destruction animations, roadmap stack; the exact version is the one resolved by `bun add`).
- Tests: `tests/unit/components/city/CityCell.test.tsx` (4 tests, component with Testing Library) — a cell that just became occupied renders character + color and is not `--empty`; empty → occupied settles on the new character (growth); occupied → empty settles on the empty placeholder and no longer exposes the character (destruction); exactly one cell is rendered per state (grid alignment). No test asserts on real animation timing (roadmap §3.3). The M2.T14 tests in `tests/unit/components/city/CityCanvas.test.tsx` remain green (10 tests) — no regression.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the transition duration (`0.25s`) and the fade/scale keyframes live as module constants in `CityCell.tsx`, so the animation is presentational and does not touch the grid state (Separation of Concerns, §1.1). The pre-existing open issue (framer-motion not installed) is resolved by this task: it is now a runtime dependency and is exercised by the real component, not only by the mocked test.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — (1) transitions do not block rendering in tests (framer-motion is mocked to a passthrough, so the settled DOM is observed synchronously); (2) no regression in the M2.T14 tests.

### M3.T6 — Multi-browser persistence: Firefox/Chrome build verification
- Both WXT targets now build as **Manifest V3**. WXT defaults Firefox to MV2, but `declarativeNetRequest` (the in-session blocklist engine, M2.T6/M2.T7) is MV3-only on Firefox, so `manifestVersion: 3` is set globally in `wxt.config.ts`. This keeps blocking, `browser.alarms` and the Shadow-DOM overlay (M2.T8) behaving identically on Chrome and Firefox. WXT then emits `background.service_worker` for Chrome and `background.scripts` (a non-persistent event page) for Firefox, because Firefox has no service-worker background.
- `wxt.config.ts` — `manifest` is now a function of the build environment (`({ browser }) => …`) instead of a static object. It keeps the existing `permissions` (`storage`, `alarms`, `declarativeNetRequest`, `tabs`) and `host_permissions` (`<all_urls>`) and adds, **for every target**, an empty but enabled static DNR ruleset (`declarative_net_request.rule_resources` → `rules/empty-rule-set.json`): Firefox ≤ 132 can silently drop dynamic DNR rules after a browser restart unless at least one static ruleset is declared (Mozilla bug 1921353), so this preserves the M2.T7 in-session ruleset across restarts on Firefox as well as Chrome. For Firefox only it adds `browser_specific_settings.gecko`: an explicit add-on `id` (required for MV3), `strict_min_version: '113.0'` (dynamic DNR rules need Firefox 113+), and `data_collection_permissions: { required: ['none'] }` (the extension collects nothing; declared for AMO).
- `public/rules/empty-rule-set.json` (new) — the empty static ruleset (`[]`) referenced by both manifests; WXT copies it from `public/` into each output.
- `README.md` — new "Multi-browser builds" section: why both targets are MV3, how the two manifests differ (background form + the Firefox-only `browser_specific_settings` block), the Firefox ≤ 132 dynamic-rules workaround, and how to load each build manually (`chrome://extensions` / `about:debugging`); notes that the automated Firefox e2e regression is M4.T5.
- Files created/modified: `wxt.config.ts`, `public/rules/empty-rule-set.json` (new), `README.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: none — the roadmap §3.3 lists M3.T6 as "Manual + preparation for M4.T5" (no automated tests). Verified instead by running `bun run build` (`.output/chrome-mv3`: MV3, `background.service_worker`, `declarativeNetRequest` + static ruleset) and `bun run build:firefox` (`.output/firefox-mv3`: MV3, `background.scripts`, the ruleset and the Firefox `browser_specific_settings` block), plus a manual inspection of both generated manifests. The full suite (484 tests across 55 files), `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` stay green.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the card's dependency is M3.T4 (popup composition), which is still open; that dependency is a sequencing one for the finished UI, not a prerequisite for the build-target work — the manifest/DNR/alarms/overlay verification does not read `App.tsx`. Forcing Firefox to MV3 moves its output directory from the stale `.output/firefox-mv2` to `.output/firefox-mv3` (the `build:firefox` script is unchanged, WXT derives the name). The static-ruleset entry is additive and also valid on Chrome (an empty array is a legal ruleset), so it does not change the Chrome behaviour.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — `wxt build -b chrome` and `wxt build -b firefox` both succeed and emit an MV3 manifest carrying `declarativeNetRequest` (with the static ruleset), `alarms`, `storage`, `tabs`, the `<all_urls>` host permission/content script, and the correct background form per browser (Chrome `service_worker`, Firefox `scripts`).

### M3.T7 — `SessionHistory` (5-session history UI)
- `components/score/SessionHistory.tsx` (new) — presentational history of the last 5 completed sessions. It reads the slice through the granular `selectSessionHistory` selector (M2.T19) and computes nothing (Separation of Concerns, §1.1; coupling rule §1.3): the list is rendered in the stored order and never re-sorted or re-sliced, so the cap (max 5) and the newest-first order remain the slice's contract rather than being re-implemented in the component.
  - **Empty state**: with no sessions it renders an explicit `No completed sessions yet.` message and no list (`role`-less `<p>`), instead of an empty `<ol>` or a crash.
  - **Score rendered, not recomputed**: the level comes straight from `SessionSummary.score`; the component never derives it from a ratio. The display label reuses the exported `SCORE_BADGE_LABELS` map (M2.T17, DRY), widened to `Record<string, string>` so a stale/unknown persisted level degrades to its raw string instead of rendering `undefined` at the untrusted storage boundary (§1.4).
  - Each row exposes `data-session-id`/`data-score`/`data-population` (test hooks) and a per-level modifier class for its color; a non-finite persisted population renders as `0` rather than `NaN` (§1.4). It carries its own monospace CRT stylesheet (`text-shadow: 0 0 4px currentColor`, consistent with M2.T14/M2.T17/M2.T18).
- `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx` — **not modified**: the card lists it as "(composition, M3.T4)", and M3.T4 (full popup composition) is still open, so mounting the component is deliberately left to that task (same approach as `ScoreBadge`/`PopulationCounter`, M2.T17/M2.T18).
- Files created/modified: `components/score/SessionHistory.tsx` (new), `tests/unit/components/score/SessionHistory.test.tsx` (new).
- Dependencies added: none (reuses the existing store selector, `SCORE_BADGE_LABELS` and the React stack).
- Tests: `tests/unit/components/score/SessionHistory.test.tsx` (7 tests, component with Testing Library + `happy-dom`) — the explicit empty state (no list, no rows); a full 5-entry history rendered in the stored newest-first order (order preserved, not re-sorted); the stored score level rendered without recalculation (a stored `bad` is shown as `Bad` even though the current `distractionRatio` is `0`); score label, population and per-level class per row; the transition when a session is recorded between two consecutive renders (empty → one entry, then a newer one lands at the front); a non-finite population renders `0`, never `NaN`; the CRT-glow stylesheet and the per-level colors are present.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the component is a leaf reader of `sessionHistory`, whose runtime producer is still missing (open issue #13: nothing calls `recordSession` at session completion yet), so in the running app the list shows the empty state until that upstream wiring lands — exactly the "each layer complete at its own level" sequencing used for M2.T12/M2.T18/M2.T19. Strings are hardcoded in EN here and move to `i18n.t(...)` in M3.T9, like the other components.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — (1) the component is presentational, reads through the M2.T19 selector and computes nothing, rendering the stored score level rather than recalculating it; (2) it renders correctly with an empty history (explicit empty state, no crash) and with a full 5-entry history, newest first, preserving the slice's order; (3) the displayed order and cap match the slice's contract (the component does not re-sort or re-slice); (4) the test covers the empty history, the 5-entry history and the transition when a new session is recorded.

### M3.T8 — `ExportCityButton` — export city as JPEG/PNG at end of focus
- `lib/city/export-image.ts` (new) — the deterministic **serialization** of the current city to an image, kept out of the component (acceptance criterion 2). `buildCityImage(layers, theme, options?)` renders a `CityLayers` + `Theme` to an SVG string plus its pixel dimensions, mirroring exactly what `CityCanvas` (M2.T14) displays: the theme's `backgroundColor`, one deterministic palette color per layer via `getBuildingColor` (M2.T13), the per-cell color override, the `background 0.4 / middleground 0.75 / foreground 1` parallax opacity and the CRT monospace font. Empty (`null`/`''`) cells emit no `<text>` but still occupy their column/row, so the grid stays aligned and matches the rendered DOM. The function is **pure** (no DOM access) and its geometry is configurable (`charWidth`/`charHeight`/`padding`/`fontSize`), with non-finite/non-positive options defensively falling back to the defaults (§1.4). Glyphs that would break the markup (`& < > " '`) are XML-escaped at this untrusted boundary. `exportCityImage(layers, theme, format)` is the high-level entry the component calls: it serializes then delegates rasterization.
- `lib/city/rasterize-image.ts` (new) — the **DOM/canvas boundary** of the export, split out from the pure serializer to keep both files within the §1.2 size guideline. `rasterizeCityImage(svg, width, height, format)` draws the SVG onto an offscreen `<canvas>` via an `Image` and returns `canvas.toDataURL(mimeType, …)`; `ImageFormat` is `'png' | 'jpeg'` with `IMAGE_MIME_TYPES` and a lossless-PNG default, so **both outputs are available** (acceptance criterion 3). `svgToDataUrl` encodes the SVG. A missing 2D context, an image that fails to load or any canvas error resolves as `{ ok: false }` instead of throwing (§1.4).
- `components/city/ExportCityButton.tsx` (new) — presentational trigger (acceptance criteria 2/4): it reads `selectCityLayers` and `selectCityThemeId` (granular selectors), resolves the active `Theme` with `getThemeById` (falling back to the exported `FALLBACK_THEME` from `CityCanvas`, M2.T14, so the resolution cannot drift), and offers **Export PNG** / **Export JPEG** buttons. It delegates serialization to `exportCityImage` and only handles the download (an `<a download>` on the returned data URL) and the error state: a failed or rejected export shows a `role="alert"` message and leaves the popup usable, never throwing. The rasterizer is an **injectable** collaborator (`CityImageExporter`, principle D/L) so a test can assert the component handed it the current grid and theme without touching a canvas. It carries its own CRT/monospace stylesheet.
- `components/city/CityCanvas.tsx` — one-line change: `FALLBACK_THEME` is now **exported** so the export button resolves an unknown/null theme id through the exact same renderable fallback as the canvas (DRY, no duplicated theme object). Behaviour is unchanged.
- `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx` — **not modified**: the card lists it as "(composition, M3.T4)", and M3.T4 (full popup composition) is still open, so mounting the button is deliberately left to that task (same approach as `ScoreBadge`/`PopulationCounter`/`SessionHistory`).
- Files created/modified: `lib/city/export-image.ts` (new), `lib/city/rasterize-image.ts` (new), `components/city/ExportCityButton.tsx` (new), `components/city/CityCanvas.tsx` (export-only).
- Dependencies added: none (reuses the M2.T13/M2.T13b palette+theme and the existing React/store stack; the image is produced with the platform canvas API, no new package).
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/city/export-image.test.ts` (10 tests, unit) — deterministic dimensions for a known grid; the theme `backgroundColor`; the per-layer palette color; a per-cell color override; no `<text>` for empty cells while the full-grid dimensions are kept; the painter's order of the three layers; XML escaping; custom geometry options and defensive fallback for non-finite ones; determinism (same input → deep-equal output). `tests/unit/lib/city/rasterize-image.test.ts` (3 tests, unit) — `svgToDataUrl` encodes the markup; both PNG and JPEG MIME types exist; `rasterizeCityImage` returns `{ ok: false }` (an `Error`) instead of throwing when no 2D context is available. `tests/unit/components/city/ExportCityButton.test.tsx` (6 tests, component with Testing Library + `user-event`) — both export buttons + the accessible group; clicking **Export PNG** calls the injected exporter once with the **current layers**, the active theme (`phosphor`, its `backgroundColor`) and format `'png'`; the JPEG button passes `'jpeg'`; a failed `Result` shows the alert without throwing; a rejected exporter is caught at the boundary; the CRT stylesheet is present.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the SVG option (instead of rasterizing the live DOM) keeps the `CityCanvas` markup untouched and gives a **pure, deterministic** serialization that can be unit-tested against a known grid, as the card requires; the canvas is only the final SVG→raster step, isolated in its own module and returning a `Result`. Splitting `export-image.ts`/`rasterize-image.ts` is a separation-of-concerns choice that also respects the ≤200-line guideline; the card's single-file wording is preserved in spirit (all serialization lives under `lib/city/`). The export is available whenever the button is mounted (the card's "at end of focus" is the objective context, not an enforced gate, and no session-completion state exists yet — open issues #13); strings are hardcoded in EN for now and move to `i18n.t(...)` in M3.T9. `FALLBACK_THEME` being exported is the only touch to a module owned by another task; it is an additive export with no behaviour change.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — (1) `buildCityImage` renders the three layers with the active theme's palette and `backgroundColor`, matching `CityCanvas` (unit tests assert the layer colors, opacity ordering and background); (2) the serialization lives in `lib/city/export-image.ts` and the component only triggers it and handles the download/errors; (3) both JPEG and PNG are offered and mapped to their MIME types; (4) a failed/rejected export is handled at the boundary and shows an alert without breaking the popup; (5) the serializer is unit-tested against a known grid (deterministic dimensions/content) and the component test asserts the export is invoked with the current grid and theme.

### M3.T9 — Internationalization (i18n) — WXT `@wxt-dev/i18n` module setup (EN default)
- `wxt.config.ts` — registered the official `'@wxt-dev/i18n/module'` alongside `@wxt-dev/module-react`, and set `manifest.default_locale: 'en'` (without it the module disables itself). The module compiles `locales/<lang>.yml` into the browser-standard `_locales/<lang>/messages.json` at build time, auto-imports the shared type-safe `i18n` instance from `#i18n`, and generates the key types on `wxt prepare`.
- `locales/en.yml` (new) — the default EN strings, grouped by area (`timer`, `city`, `score`, `blocklist`, `overlay`, `common`) with nested keys and `$1`..`$9` positional substitutions. It is the single source of truth: the `_locales/en/messages.json` shipped in both builds and the EN-fallback of every other locale derive from it, so a new language only needs a sibling `<lang>.yml` with the same tree (M3.T10) — no component or config change.
- Every visible string of the popup components and the overlay now goes through `i18n.t(...)` (explicitly imported from `#i18n`): `components/timer/TimerDisplay.tsx` (countdown aria-label) and `TimerControls.tsx` (Start/Pause/Reset); `components/score/ScoreBadge.tsx` (per-level label + `Focus score: $1` aria-label), `PopulationCounter.tsx` (`population`, `Population: $1`) and `SessionHistory.tsx` (title, empty state, per-session score/population); `components/blocklist/PresetPicker.tsx` (`Presets`, `Apply $1 preset`), `TagEditor.tsx` (form, chips, assignment/filter/delete labels) and `SiteList.tsx` (titles, add/remove, live preview, duplicate/moved/added feedback, the `Enter a valid URL` error and the `example.com` placeholder); `components/city/CityCanvas.tsx` (canvas aria-label) and `ExportCityButton.tsx` (group, PNG/JPEG buttons, failure message); `components/common/BlockedOverlay.tsx` (title, Yes/No, encouragement, and the question split into `questionPrefix`/`questionSuffix` so the domain stays a highlighted `<span>` while the sentence remains one translatable unit).
  - `ScoreBadge` now exports `SCORE_LEVEL_LABEL_KEYS` (`ScoreLevel` → key, as data — Open/Closed) and `scoreLevelLabel(level)`, replacing the old hardcoded `SCORE_BADGE_LABELS` map; `SessionHistory` reuses it (DRY) and still degrades an unknown persisted level to its raw string. `SiteList` maps the pure `normalizeEntry` failure to the localized `blocklist.invalidUrl` key instead of reading the EN literal out of `Error.message` (the pure `lib/` layer keeps no i18n dependency).
- `tests/helpers/i18n.ts` (new) — a test-only `browser.i18n.getMessage` backend. Because `fakeBrowser` leaves `i18n.getMessage` unimplemented, the helper parses the **real** `locales/*.yml` sources (via `@wxt-dev/i18n/build`'s `parseMessagesFile`/`generateChromeMessages`) and reproduces the browser's lookup (including `$1` substitutions and the `default_locale` EN fallback). This keeps tests honest: a component renders a string only if the key exists in the locale file. `installI18nBackend()` is called from `tests/setup.ts`; `setTestLocale`/`resetTestLocale` are the seam that simulates a browser language, ready for M3.T10.
- Files created/modified: `wxt.config.ts`, `locales/en.yml` (new), `tests/helpers/i18n.ts` (new), `tests/setup.ts`, `tests/unit/i18n/i18n.test.tsx` (new), all the components listed above, `tests/unit/components/score/ScoreBadge.test.tsx`, `package.json`/`bun.lock`.
- Dependencies added: `@wxt-dev/i18n` `^0.2.7` (runtime; resolved to `0.2.8`) — the official WXT module, a synchronous type-safe wrapper around `browser.i18n` (roadmap M3.T9).
- Tests: `tests/unit/i18n/i18n.test.tsx` (3 tests, component + integration with Testing Library) — the shared `#i18n` instance resolves EN strings and positional substitutions from `locales/en.yml`; `TimerControls` renders its visible strings through `i18n.t`; `BlockedOverlay` renders the question from its translatable parts around the highlighted domain and the Yes/No choices. The existing component suites (ScoreBadge, SessionHistory, TimerControls, PopulationCounter, PresetPicker, TagEditor, SiteList, ExportCityButton, BlockedOverlay) now assert the same EN text resolved through the real locale file, so a missing/renamed key fails them; `ScoreBadge.test.tsx` was updated for the `scoreLevelLabel`/`SCORE_LEVEL_LABEL_KEYS` refactor. Full suite: 512 tests across 60 files.
- Relevant notes/decisions: **the card's dependency M3.T4 (popup `App.tsx` composition) is still open**, so `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx` remains the WXT starter and is deliberately not translated here — its placeholder copy will be replaced by M3.T4, and that task must route its composition strings through `i18n.t` (same "not modified, M3.T4 owns it" approach already recorded for `ScoreBadge`/`PopulationCounter`/`SessionHistory`/`ExportCityButton`). There is intentionally **no** language selector and no `language` field in any slice: the language follows the browser's own via the native API. The shared `i18n` instance is the same `#i18n` import in both the popup components and the content-script overlay (no second instance). The IT-rendering half of the roadmap criterion belongs to **M3.T10**, which adds `locales/it.yml`; the test backend already exposes the `setTestLocale` seam for it.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — (1) `wxt.config.ts` registers `@wxt-dev/i18n/module` and sets `manifest.default_locale: 'en'` (confirmed in both generated manifests and `_locales/en/messages.json`); (2) all visible popup/overlay strings in the implemented components resolve through `i18n.t(...)` — none is a hardcoded literal except the not-yet-composed `App.tsx` starter (M3.T4); (3) no language selector nor persisted language preference exists; (4) translations load synchronously (native `browser.i18n`, no `await`/fetch); (5) the popup and the content script share the single `#i18n` instance; (6) adding a language only requires `locales/<lang>.yml`; (7) the EN test renders the expected strings through `i18n.t` (the IT half is covered by M3.T10). `bun run compile`, `bun run lint`, `bun run test` (512) and both builds are green.

### M3.T10 — IT translation (language auto-detected from browser)
- `locales/it.yml` (new) — the complete Italian translation of `locales/en.yml`, key-for-key and with the identical nested tree (`timer`/`city`/`score`/`blocklist`/`overlay`/`common`, same `$1`..`$9` positional substitutions). The build compiles it into `_locales/it/messages.json` for both Chrome and Firefox (verified in `.output/chrome-mv3` and `.output/firefox-mv3`). No component, config or store change was needed: language selection is already automatic via the native `browser.i18n` API (`manifest.default_locale: 'en'`), which serves Italian when the browser's UI language is Italian and falls back to English otherwise. There is no selector and no `language` field in any slice — matching the acceptance criterion.
- `tests/unit/i18n/locales.test.ts` (new) — the **drift-detection** unit test required by the roadmap §3.3. It parses the real `locales/en.yml`/`locales/it.yml` with the same `@wxt-dev/i18n/build` parser the build and the test backend use (`parseMessagesText`, M3.T9) and compares the key paths (the parser-appended standard `@@` keys are filtered out): every EN key has an IT counterpart, the two key **sets** are identical (no stray IT-only key), and every IT value is non-empty. It also asserts the "no manual language preference" criterion: no `language`/`locale` property exists on the store state or on any slice (`timer`/`city`/`blocklist`/`score`/`sessionHistory`/`ui`).
- `tests/unit/i18n/i18n.test.tsx` — extended with an `IT locale rendering (M3.T10)` block that forces the locale through the M3.T9 `setTestLocale('it')` seam (the `browser.i18n.getMessage` mock installed in `tests/setup.ts`) and asserts, through the shared `#i18n` instance: `i18n.t` resolves the IT strings and positional substitutions; `TimerControls` renders `Avvia`/`Pausa`/`Reimposta`; and `BlockedOverlay` renders the Italian question around the highlighted domain plus `Sì`/`No` — the same component the content script mounts, so the content script receives Italian without any additional logic.
- Files created/modified: `locales/it.yml` (new), `tests/unit/i18n/locales.test.ts` (new), `tests/unit/i18n/i18n.test.tsx`.
- Dependencies added: none (the `@wxt-dev/i18n` runtime dependency and the `setTestLocale` test seam already existed from M3.T9; adding a locale is only a new YAML file).
- Tests: `tests/unit/i18n/locales.test.ts` (4 tests, unit) — every EN key translated in IT; identical key tree in both locales; every IT value non-empty; no language property on the store state or slices. `tests/unit/i18n/i18n.test.tsx` (+3 tests, component + integration) — IT strings/substitutions via `i18n.t`; `TimerControls` in Italian; `BlockedOverlay` in Italian through the shared instance. Full suite: 519 tests across 61 files.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the drift test compares the **key trees of the source files**, not the compiled output, so a key added to `en.yml` fails CI until `it.yml` is updated — the untranslated key would otherwise silently fall back to English at runtime (the `default_locale` behaviour the M3.T9 helper already mirrors). The IT `blocklist` list names are phrased in the feminine (`lista dei bloccati`/`lista dei consentiti`) so the `alreadyInList`/`movedFromList`/`addTo` substitutions read naturally (`nella lista dei bloccati`). As with M3.T9, `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx` is still the WXT starter (M3.T4 open) and is not translated here.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — (1) every key in `locales/en.yml` has an IT counterpart in `locales/it.yml` with the same tree, guarded by the drift test; (2) language selection is automatic only (no selector, no persisted preference, no dedicated store action), asserted by the store-shape test and by the absence of any config/component change; (3) the content-script overlay renders Italian when the browser is set to Italian via the same shared `#i18n` instance (component test); (4) EN/IT key coverage is verified by the tree-comparison test that fails if an IT key is missing. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint`, `bun run test` (519) and both builds are green; both generated `_locales/it/messages.json` are present.

## Milestone 4 — Polish and Nice-to-have

### M4.T1 — *(Nice to have)* Malus proportional to time spent on the blocked site
- `lib/score/apply-malus.ts` — the M2.T10 module is extended with a **time-proportional** malus on top of the existing one-character-per-tick deletion:
  - `malusForDistractionTicks(ticksOfDistraction): number` (new, pure) — the *additional* characters owed for the whole distraction ticks spent on a blocked site (on top of the single character removed the moment the user chose to proceed), so the malus is a function of the time spent and not just of the single event. The function is **tick-based and cadence-agnostic**: one tick is the session cadence, which follows the selected focus duration (I005 — e.g. 1 s at 10 min, 3 s at the 30 min default, 12 s at 120 min), so the same tick count is always the same number of characters at every preset (duration-neutral, like growth). It is cumulative and **monotonic non-decreasing** (every tick contributes a non-negative cost), total and pure (non-finite/negative input normalises to `0`, mirroring `applyMalus`).
  - `MALUS_ESCALATION_SCHEDULE` / `MalusEscalationRule` (new, exported data — Open/Closed §1.1): each rule means "from this many whole distraction ticks onwards, every further tick costs this many characters" (tick = session cadence, as above). The shipped schedule is `1`/`1`, `6`/`2`, `12`/`3` — the first stage preserves the M2.T10 base cadence, so short stays behave exactly as before and only longer stays become harsher. Making the penalty harder/softer is an array edit, never a change to the calculation.
  - `applyMalus(city, ticksOfDistraction)` is **unchanged**: it remains the pure deletion primitive (one character per tick) with its M2.T10 signature and tests intact, so the new escalation is additive rather than a rewrite of the primitive.
- `entrypoints/background.ts` — the M2.T16 malus pump (`createMalusWiring`) now derives the cumulative characters owed from `1 + malusForDistractionTicks(elapsedTicks)` instead of the linear `1 + elapsedTicks`, so the running app actually escalates the punishment with time while the immediate character on "proceed" is unchanged. The elapsed time is converted to ticks at the **session cadence** (`tickMsForDurationSeconds(store.getState().timer.durationSeconds)`), the same clock growth uses, so reward and penalty stay in step at every preset. All M2.T16 behaviour is preserved for short stays (the first five ticks cost one character each), and the existing M2.T16 unit/integration tests remain green.
- Files created/modified: `lib/score/apply-malus.ts` (extension), `entrypoints/background.ts` (uses the new pure function), `tests/unit/lib/score/apply-malus.test.ts`, `tests/unit/background/malus-wiring.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/score/apply-malus.test.ts` (+8 tests, unit) — no additional malus before a tick elapses (`0`); grows strictly once time is spent; **monotonic non-decreasing over an increasing case table** (`0,1,2,3,5,6,7,11,12,20,60,300`) — the acceptance criterion's monotonic property; no dip over a dense range `0..240` (more time never yields a smaller malus); escalation is real and not merely linear (known values `11 → 17`, `17 → 35`; marginal cost increases across the schedule stages); total for `NaN`/negative/`Infinity`/fractional input; determinism; the schedule is ordered with positive stages. `tests/unit/background/malus-wiring.test.ts` (+1 test, unit with a stub tracker) — through the real wiring, 12 elapsed ticks remove `malusForDistractionTicks(12)` characters in one update, i.e. more than the linear 12 (end-to-end proof that the app escalates).
- Relevant notes/decisions: the card lists only `lib/score/apply-malus.ts`, but the malus is already wired (M2.T16), so the extension is also connected to that pump — otherwise the new curve would be dead code and the app would keep the linear behaviour. This is additive: `applyMalus`'s contract and every existing test are untouched. The escalation is driven by the tracker's whole distraction ticks measured at the **session cadence**, not by a fixed 2 s constant — I005 removed `MALUS_TICK_MS` entirely and the tracker now reports accumulated milliseconds, with the background converting them at `tickMsForDurationSeconds(...)`; "time spent" is therefore still the accumulated blocked-tab time, but a tick is 0.5–12 s depending on the chosen preset (0.5 s at 5 min, 3 s at the 30 min default, 12 s at 120 min). The escalation begins after the first five ticks, i.e. after 2.5 s at 5 min, **15 s at the 30 min default**, 60 s at 120 min — so the M2.T16 acceptance criteria still hold for the durations their tests exercise (they assert per-tick behaviour, never a wall-clock threshold).
- Commit: `5d665bb feat(M4.T1): scale malus with time spent on blocked sites`; `de0ffe8` (I005) adapted the surrounding pump to the session cadence without changing this module's contract.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — the monotonic property is tested both directly (`malusForDistractionTicks`) and through the real malus wiring; more time spent on the blocked site produces a greater-or-equal malus, never a smaller one. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint`, `bun run test` (528 at the time of writing; **642** after I005) and both builds were green; re-checked when this entry was updated: `tests/unit/lib/score/apply-malus.test.ts` + `tests/unit/background/malus-wiring.test.ts` → **28 tests passing**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, working tree clean at `de0ffe8` before this documentation-only update.

### M4.T2 — *(Nice to have)* TexturePacker / building sprite sheet integration — skipped
- **Decision**: the task is skipped for now. It is an optional nice-to-have and is not needed at this time; the city keeps its current ASCII renderer unchanged.

### M4.T3a — Centralized logger foundation
- `utils/logger.ts` (new) — the shared logging primitive consumed by the rest of M4.T3:
  - `createLogger(scope, { sink?, minLevel? })` exposes `debug`/`info`/`warn`/`error` and `child(scope)`. Records are `{ level, scope, message, details }`; the default sink writes `[buildit:<scope>] <message>` to the matching console method, while tests inject a sink and assert the record directly (no exact-console-text coupling).
  - Level filtering is real: the default threshold is `debug` in development and `warn` in production, and `minLevel` overrides it. `child('dnr')` appends the segment (`background:dnr`) and inherits the sink and threshold; an empty child scope leaves the scope unchanged.
  - `error(message, cause?, context?)` accepts `unknown`, preserves an existing `Error` (and its stack), turns a non-empty string into an `Error`, wraps any other value with the original as `cause`, and appends the technical context after it. Context is deliberately limited to technical fields (message type, storage key, export format) — no URLs, domains or payloads — and there is no remote transport or persistence.
  - A throwing sink is swallowed: logging is best-effort and must never turn a handled error into a new failure for the caller.
- `tests/unit/utils/logger.test.ts` (new) — 10 tests (unit): scoped record + context; threshold filtering; child scope/sink/threshold inheritance; empty child scope; `Error` identity + context order; string and object cause normalization; empty details; throwing sink; default console prefix.
- Files created/modified: `utils/logger.ts` (new), `tests/unit/utils/logger.test.ts` (new).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `bun run test` → **652 passing tests** across 71 files (baseline 642/70; +10 tests/+1 file). `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` are clean; `bun run build` (Chrome) and `bun run build:firefox` (Firefox) both succeed.
- Relevant notes/decisions: no call site was changed in this subtask, so the existing three `console.error` calls in `entrypoints/background.ts` remain until M4.T3b. For the same reason `no-console` is deferred to M4.T3e; enabling it now would fail immediately.
- Commit: `c95f193 feat(logger): implement centralized logging with scoped context and configurable levels`.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — the logger has levels, scope/child context and an injectable sink; the threshold is configurable; the error cause is preserved/normalized; context carries no user data; logging cannot break the caller.

### M4.T3b — Background and message-bus error boundaries
- `entrypoints/background.ts` — the three scattered `console.error` boundaries now use the centralized logger, and the worker has a synchronous bootstrap guard:
  - `BackgroundDependencies` gains an optional `logger` (default `createLogger('background')`), so tests assert records/sinks instead of exact console text. The DNR sync, tab-distraction-tracker startup and startup reconciliation boundaries keep their original messages and causes and add technical context: `{ boundary: 'dnr' }`, `{ boundary: 'tab-tracker' }` and `{ boundary: 'startup' }`.
  - `bootstrapBackground()` wraps the synchronous `startBackground()` wiring: a failure is logged as `background failed to start` with `{ boundary: 'bootstrap' }` and returns `null`, so the WXT callback cannot throw and the service worker stays alive. The default entrypoint now calls the wrapper; asynchronous startup failures remain contained by `BackgroundHandle.ready`.
  - Message handlers are registered with `log.child('bus')`, so a handler failure in the background is scoped `background:bus` rather than losing its origin.
- `lib/messaging/bus.ts` — `onMessage` accepts an optional logger (default scope `messaging`) and contains handler failures at the boundary:
  - A synchronous throw is caught around handler invocation; an async handler's rejection is consumed with `.catch`. Both log `message handler failed` with `{ type }` and the original cause.
  - The listener still returns `void`, so the browser's `sendMessage` contract is unchanged; a rejecting handler can no longer become an unhandled rejection that takes down the context. Full payload validation/provenance remains M6.T1.
- Files created/modified: `entrypoints/background.ts`, `lib/messaging/bus.ts`, `tests/integration/background/background.test.ts`, `tests/integration/background/dnr-rules.test.ts`, `tests/integration/messaging/bus.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/integration/background/background.test.ts` (+3 tests) — synchronous bootstrap failure → log + `null`; failed tab-tracker startup → log + worker still idle; failed rehydration/startup reconciliation → log + `ready` resolves. `tests/integration/messaging/bus.test.ts` (+2 tests) — synchronous handler throw and async handler rejection are logged with `{ type }` while `sendMessage` still resolves. `tests/integration/background/dnr-rules.test.ts` — the failing-DNR test now injects a logger/sink and asserts scope, message, cause and `{ boundary: 'dnr' }` instead of spying on console text. Full suite: **657 passing tests** across 71 files (baseline 652/71; +5 tests). `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` are clean; both Chrome and Firefox builds succeed.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the logger is injected at the two public seams (`startBackground`/`bootstrapBackground` and `onMessage`) rather than mocked globally, which keeps the tests independent of console formatting. The existing `session-completion.test.ts` guard still asserts that a normal completion logs nothing. `no-console` remains deferred to M4.T3e; the only remaining `console.*` calls are inside `utils/logger.ts` itself.
- Commit: `bbe209b feat(logging): implement centralized logging in background and messaging components`.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — a simulated DNR failure is logged with context while the store remains usable; synchronous start failures and async handler rejections are contained; all three previous `console.error` boundaries now go through the centralized logger.

### M4.T3c — Persistence read/rehydration diagnostics (CI-3)
- `store/storage-adapter.ts` — `createBrowserStorage(logger?)` now accepts an optional logger (default scope `storage`) and makes the CI-3 fallback observable without changing it:
  - A missing/`undefined` key still returns `null` silently: first hydration is normal and `fakeBrowser` returns the requested key with an `undefined` value rather than an empty object.
  - A *present* value that is not a string logs `warn` as `ignoring non-string persisted value` with `{ key }` and still returns `null`. The rejected value is never passed to the logger, so it cannot leak a domain or other user data.
  - `browserStorage` keeps using the default logger; tests inject a sink through the new parameter.
- `store/index.ts` — rehydration failures are no longer silent:
  - `CreateAppStoreOptions` gains an optional `logger` (default scope `store`), threaded into `buildStore`.
  - `onRehydrateStorage` logs `error` as `store rehydration failed` with `{ key: STORE_NAME }` when zustand reports a parse/migration/hydration failure. Zustand still keeps the store defaults and resolves `rehydrate()`; only the diagnostic is new. A `SyntaxError` from `JSON.parse` is replaced with a generic `persisted store JSON is malformed` error because V8 may quote a prefix of the persisted string in its message; non-parse errors keep their original cause/stack. The raw persisted string and parsed state are never logged.
- Files created/modified: `store/storage-adapter.ts`, `store/index.ts`, `tests/integration/store/storage-adapter.test.ts`, `tests/integration/store/store.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/integration/store/storage-adapter.test.ts` — the absent-key case now injects a logger and asserts no records, and the non-string case asserts one `warn` with the key and no leaked value/domain. `tests/integration/store/store.test.ts` (+2 tests) — corrupt JSON → `error` with `{ key }` + default state; a valid persisted payload restores its data and logs nothing. Full suite: **659 passing tests** across 71 files (baseline 657/71; +2 tests). `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` are clean; both Chrome and Firefox builds succeed.
- Relevant notes/decisions: CI-3 is closed by keeping the defensive fallback exactly as it was and adding diagnostics at the two read layers (adapter shape check, persist rehydration callback). `undefined` is deliberately treated as "absent" rather than "non-string" because that is what both Chrome's `storage.local.get` and WXT's fake return for a missing key. Parse errors are sanitized as described above; non-parse errors retain their stack, and no raw payload/state/domain is attached as context.
- Commit: `d45b6db` — committed together with M4.T3d.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — a present non-string value warns with the key and returns `null`; an absent key logs nothing; corrupt JSON logs `error` and leaves the defaults; a valid payload logs nothing.

### M4.T3d — Popup React error boundary
- `components/common/ErrorBoundary.tsx` (new) — a class-based boundary around the popup, because React only catches render/lifecycle failures with `getDerivedStateFromError`/`componentDidCatch`:
  - `getDerivedStateFromError` sets `hasError`; `componentDidCatch` logs `popup component failed` through an injectable logger (default scope `popup:error-boundary`) with the original error and `{ componentStack }`. The optional `onError(error, info)` hook receives the same pair for tests/integration.
  - The fallback is a self-contained CRT-styled `role="alert"` panel with `aria-labelledby`/`aria-describedby`; its title, body and button resolve from `common.errorTitle`, `common.errorBody` and `common.reload`. No store or other popup module is imported, so the boundary can render even when a component failed.
  - The recovery button calls `onReset()` and clears the error state when the hook is provided (the test/future reset strategy); otherwise it calls `window.location.reload()`. Calling `onReset` first lets a child that stops throwing render on the same update.
  - Scope limit documented in the component: event-handler and async/promise errors are not caught here; they belong to the message bus (M4.T3b), store (M4.T3c) and content/export paths (M4.T3e).
- `entrypoints/popup/main.tsx` — `<App />` is wrapped in `<ErrorBoundary>` inside `React.StrictMode`; the normal popup composition is unchanged.
- `locales/en.yml` / `locales/it.yml` — added `common.errorTitle`, `common.errorBody`, `common.reload` in both locales. The existing EN/IT drift test remains green.
- Files created/modified: `components/common/ErrorBoundary.tsx` (new), `entrypoints/popup/main.tsx`, `locales/en.yml`, `locales/it.yml`, `tests/unit/components/common/ErrorBoundary.test.tsx` (new).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/components/common/ErrorBoundary.test.tsx` (4 tests) — a throwing child is contained, the fallback shows the EN text, `onError` receives the original error/component stack and the logger records scope/cause/context; the fallback renders in IT through the shared i18n instance; `onReset` recovers when the child stops throwing; without `onReset`, the button calls `window.location.reload()`. Full suite: **663 passing tests** across 72 files (baseline 659/71; +4 tests/+1 file). `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` are clean; both Chrome and Firefox builds succeed.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the logger/error/reset hooks follow the M4.T3b injection pattern and keep the test independent of console formatting. React's own development `console.error` for caught render errors is silenced only inside the test. The fallback styles travel with the component (no new CSS file), matching the existing self-contained common components. No user data is logged: the context is limited to the component stack.
- Commit: `d45b6db feat(ErrorBoundary): implement error boundary for popup with logging and recovery options`.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — the boundary logs the error/component stack with a scoped logger and shows a localized, accessible recovery screen; the reload button reloads by default and the reset hook recovers deterministically; `<App />` is wrapped in the real entrypoint; the normal popup composition and all existing tests are unchanged.

### M4.T3e — Content/export boundaries + logging guardrails
- `entrypoints/blocked-overlay.content.tsx` — the last silent content-script boundaries now report through the scoped logger:
  - `createStoreReader(logger?)` accepts an optional logger (default scope `content:store-reader`). A missing key remains silent (normal first run); a *present* value that `parseStoreSnapshot` rejects logs `error` as `unreadable persisted snapshot` with `{ key: STORE_STORAGE_KEY }` and falls back to `undefined`. The parse cause is deliberately not attached: V8's `SyntaxError` message can quote the persisted JSON, which contains the user's domains.
  - `startBlockedOverlay` accepts an optional `logger` (default scope `content:blocked-overlay`) and gives the default reader a `store-reader` child scope. The initial refresh and every subscription refresh go through one helper that contains both a rejected promise and a synchronously throwing reader; a failure logs `store refresh failed` with `{ key }` and leaves the page and overlay lifecycle alive.
  - `sendAttempt` no longer produces an unhandled rejection: a shared `reportAttempt` helper contains both rejection and synchronous throw, logging `blocked attempt could not be sent` with `{ choice }` only. The default sender now turns a failed `sendMessage` `Result` into a rejection, so a missing/restarted background is logged through the same boundary instead of staying silent. The domain is never attached, so the diagnostic cannot leak the visited site. The overlay still unmounts on Yes and shows the encouragement on No; the page remains usable when the background is absent.
- `components/city/ExportCityButton.tsx` — accepts an optional `logger` (default scope `popup:export-city`):
  - A failed `Result` logs `city export failed` with the original error and `{ format }`.
  - A rejected exporter promise, or a synchronous throw before the promise is returned, logs `city export rejected` with the original cause and `{ format }`.
  - The localized `city.exportError` message and the download path are unchanged, and the boundary never rethrows into the popup. No layers, theme or user data are logged.
- `eslint.config.js` — the guardrails are now explicit: `no-empty` is an error with `allowEmptyCatch: false`, and `no-console` is an error with only two exceptions: `utils/logger.ts` (the console sink) and `tests/**/*.{ts,tsx}` (spies/silencing). The effective config was inspected with `eslint --print-config` for a source file, the logger and a test file.
  - The intentional `lib/blocking/apply-rules.ts` guard `queue = run.catch(() => undefined)` is untouched, together with its comment: the internal queue survives a rejection while the promise returned to the caller still rejects.
- Files created/modified: `entrypoints/blocked-overlay.content.tsx`, `components/city/ExportCityButton.tsx`, `eslint.config.js`, `tests/integration/content/blocked-overlay.test.ts`, `tests/unit/components/city/ExportCityButton.test.tsx`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/integration/content/blocked-overlay.test.ts` (+4 tests, 1 extended) — a rejected `refresh()` logs scope/cause/key and leaves the page valid with no overlay; a corrupt persisted snapshot logs `unreadable persisted snapshot` with only the key and does not leak the domain embedded in the malformed JSON; an absent store logs nothing; a rejected `sendAttempt` logs `{ choice }`, still closes the overlay on Yes and leaves the content context valid; the existing no-listener scenario now asserts that the default `sendMessage` failure is also logged while the page stays alive. `tests/unit/components/city/ExportCityButton.test.tsx` (2 tests extended, +1) — a failed `Result` logs the cause and `{ format: 'png' }` while keeping the localized error; a rejected exporter logs `{ format: 'jpeg' }`; a synchronously throwing exporter is contained and logged. Full suite: **668 passing tests** across 72 files (baseline 663/72; +5 tests). `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` are clean; `bun run build` (Chrome) and `bun run build:firefox` (Firefox) both succeed.
- Relevant notes/decisions: logging stays local and technical — no persistence, no remote transport, no URL/domain/layer/theme payload. The manual empty-catch search over `entrypoints components lib store utils` returned **0 matches** (executed with the equivalent `grep -RInE` because `rg` is not installed in this environment). `no-empty` accepts the logger's commented best-effort catch and still rejects a genuinely empty catch. The `apply-rules.ts` queue guard is intentionally not caught as empty because it is an arrow-function rejection handler with a return value, not an empty block.
- Commit: `2a74b6b feat(logging): enhance logging in content and export boundaries with detailed error handling`.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — rejected refreshes, unreadable persisted snapshots and rejected attempts are logged with scope and no user data; the overlay and page survive background failures; failed/failed-promise exports log the cause and format while keeping the localized message; ESLint enforces both rules with only the documented exceptions; compile/lint/tests/both builds are green.
- **Umbrella closure**: M4.T3a–e are complete. Every known boundary now logs with technical context and containment, the simulated background failures no longer crash a worker/page/popup, and the empty-catch/no-console rules prevent the next feature from regressing the hardening.
