## Milestone 5 — Sound cues

### M5.T1 — Sound types and the four sound themes (data)
- `lib/sound/sound.types.ts` (type-only) defines `SoundCue`, `SoundThemeId`, `Waveform`, `SoundNote` (with optional `glideToHz`), `SoundEnvelope`, `SoundFilter`, `OscillatorVoice` (one oscillator per detune value per note), `NoiseVoice`, `SoundEffects` (`delay`, `bitcrush`), `SoundRecipe` and `SoundTheme`; every field is `readonly`.
- `lib/sound/sound-themes.ts` exports `SOUND_THEMES` (neon, synthwave, glitch, terminal, each with a `start` and an `end` recipe), `DEFAULT_SOUND_THEME_ID = 'glitch'`, `isSoundThemeId` and `findSoundTheme`. Labels are not here: i18n belongs to the UI (M5.T16).
- Files created: `lib/sound/sound.types.ts`, `lib/sound/sound-themes.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/sound/sound-themes.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/sound/sound-themes.test.ts` (65 tests, unit) — four themes in order with unique ids; the default exists; `findSoundTheme` for every id; `isSoundThemeId` accepts the four ids and rejects `'NEON'`, `''`, `1`, `null`, `undefined`, `{}`; for each of the 8 recipes: at least one voice, frequencies (including `glideToHz` and filter cutoffs) within 20–20 000 Hz, gains in (0, 1] summing to at most 1, every note/burst ending within 1.5 s release included, positive attack/release, `bits` an integer in 2–8, `feedback` < 0.8 and `mix` in [0, 1].
- Relevant notes/decisions: the card's note values are starting points, to be re-tuned by ear once the renderer exists (the tests check ranges, so re-tuning will not break them). `sound-themes.ts` is 233 lines, over the 200-line guideline, because it is data; the repeated parts are small builders (`neonVoices`, `synthwaveArpeggio`, `terminalBeeps`). `findSoundTheme` throws only for an id outside `SoundThemeId`, which the type forbids (impossible state, §1.4); untrusted input goes through `isSoundThemeId` first.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile` and `eslint lib/sound tests/unit/lib/sound` are clean; the module imports nothing from `browser`, React, the store or Web Audio.

### M5.T2 — `lib/sound/playback-plan.ts` — recipe + volume → timed tones (pure)
- `calculatePlaybackPlan(recipe, volume)` flattens a `SoundRecipe` into a `PlaybackPlan { tones, masterGain, effects, durationSec }`: one `oscillator` tone per note × detune value and one `noise` tone per burst, each carrying its own timing, `peakGain` (the voice gain), envelope, `glideToHz` and `filter`. Times are relative to 0; the renderer (M5.T4) adds its own clock.
- `calculateVolumeGain(volume)` = `(clamp(volume, 0, 100) / 100) ** 2`; a non-finite volume gives 0. A volume ≤ 0 or `NaN` gives `tones: []`, `durationSec: 0`.
- `durationSec` is the latest `startSec + durationSec + releaseSec`, plus `4 × delay.timeSec` when the recipe has a delay (the echo tail). The recipe's `effects` are passed through untouched.
- Files created: `lib/sound/playback-plan.ts` (110 lines), `tests/unit/lib/sound/playback-plan.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/sound/playback-plan.test.ts` (18 tests, unit) — volume table (0, 50, 100, 150, −5, `NaN`, `Infinity`); tone count and field mapping on a hand-written recipe (so re-tuning a theme never breaks them); master gain; empty plan at volumes 0, −1, `NaN`; end time with and without a delay tail; effects passthrough; purity on a deep-frozen recipe; every real theme recipe at volume 100 gives a non-empty plan of at most 2.5 s.
- Relevant notes/decisions: optional fields (`glideToHz`, `filter`) are omitted rather than set to `undefined`, so the plan stays structurally equal to the card's shape. The module imports only types from `sound.types.ts`.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint` and the full suite are clean.

### M5.T3 — `lib/sound/waveshapes.ts` — noise samples and bit-crush curve (pure)
- `calculateNoiseSamples(length, seed)` returns a `Float32Array` of deterministic white noise in [−1, 1], drawn from `createRng(seed)` (`lib/city/plan/rng.ts`), never `Math.random`. A negative or non-finite length counts as 0 and a fractional one is floored.
- `calculateBitcrushCurve(bits, size = 4096)` returns the `WaveShaperNode.curve` that snaps [−1, 1] onto `2 ** bits` evenly spaced levels: first value −1, last value 1, never decreasing, exactly `2 ** bits` distinct values. `bits` is rounded and clamped to 2–8; a non-finite `bits` keeps the finest setting (8), the least audible degradation. `size` is floored and at least 2.
- Files created: `lib/sound/waveshapes.ts` (40 lines), `tests/unit/lib/sound/waveshapes.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/sound/waveshapes.test.ts` (26 tests, unit) — noise length, range, not silent, same seed → same array, different seeds differ, lengths 0/−5/`NaN`/10.9; curve size, endpoints, custom size, 2 ** bits distinct levels for bits 2–8, monotonic, even spacing, clamping of 1/0/−3/12, non-finite bits.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the card did not say what a non-finite `bits` does; the finest setting was chosen so a bad value never makes the sound harsher. Pure: imports only `createRng`; no Web Audio, `browser` or store.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint` and the full suite are clean.

### M5.T4 — Web Audio renderer behind an injected `AudioContext`
- `createWebAudioRenderer(createContext, logger?)` returns a `SoundRenderer { play(plan), stopAll(), dispose() }` that only schedules a `PlaybackPlan` on a Web Audio context; it makes no decisions of its own.
- Signal path: each tone → its `BiquadFilter` (if it has a filter) → its own envelope `Gain` → one master `Gain` (`plan.masterGain`) → a `WaveShaper` using `calculateBitcrushCurve` (only with `bitcrush`) → destination; with `delay`, a parallel branch `Delay` ⇄ feedback `Gain`, mixed in through a wet `Gain` set to `mix`. Every time is `context.currentTime + 0.02 s + tone.startSec`.
- Envelope: gain 0 at the start, a linear ramp to `peakGain` after the attack, a hold until `max(attack, duration)`, then an exponential ramp to 0.0001 at `start + duration + release`, where the source stops. The filter cutoff ramps exponentially from `fromHz` to `toHz` over the tone's duration; `glideToHz` is an exponential frequency ramp over the duration. Noise tones share one looped 1 s buffer per context (`calculateNoiseSamples`, fixed seed).
- The context is created lazily and reused; a `suspended` context is `resume()`d before any source starts. An empty plan resolves `ok` without creating a context. A throwing factory or a failed `resume()` resolves `err(...)` and the next `play` retries; `play` never throws. `stopAll()` calls `stop(0)` on every source that has not ended yet; `dispose()` stops everything, closes the context once and resets, so a later `play` makes a fresh context. A rejected `close()` is logged as a `warn`, not thrown.
- Files created: `lib/sound/web-audio-renderer.ts` (180 lines), `lib/sound/audio-context.types.ts` (type-only: `AudioContextLike` and the node interfaces), `tests/helpers/fake-audio-context.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/sound/web-audio-renderer.test.ts`. `docs/roadmap-en.md`: the M5.T4 card's **Files** line now lists `audio-context.types.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/sound/web-audio-renderer.test.ts` (24 tests, unit) — source counts, start/stop times, envelope calls, filter and glide ramps, master gain, wiring, shared looped noise buffer; no bit-crush/delay node unless requested, shaper and delay wiring; one context across two plays, resume before any `start`, empty plan, throwing factory, retry after failure, failed `resume`; `stopAll`, an ended source forgotten, `dispose` closes once, dispose before play, replay after dispose, a failing `close` logged. A type-level test checks that the real `AudioContext` satisfies `AudioContextLike`.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the interfaces went into their own file to keep the renderer under the 200-line guideline (the card had planned them inside it; the renderer re-exports `AudioContextLike`). `FakeAudioContext` records created nodes, connections and every `AudioParam` call, plus an ordered `log` of `resume` and source starts. `stopAll()` does not cut a delay tail that is already playing (about 0.7 s at most). The optional `logger` argument is an addition to the card's signature.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint` and the full suite are clean.

### M5.T5 — Sound preferences model and validation (pure)
- `lib/sound/sound-preferences.ts` owns the shape, the defaults and the validation of the sound preferences, shared later by the store, the mutation handler and the cues: `SoundPreferences { enabled, themeId, volume }` (volume an integer 0–100), `SoundPreferencesPatch = Partial<SoundPreferences>`, `DEFAULT_SOUND_PREFERENCES = { enabled: true, themeId: 'glitch', volume: 50 }`.
- `sanitizeSoundPreferences(value: unknown)` is lenient, for persisted data: each bad or missing field falls back to its own default, an in-range fractional volume is rounded and an out-of-range one clamped; a non-object (including `null` and arrays) gives the defaults; unknown keys are dropped.
- `applySoundPreferencesPatch(current, patch: unknown): Result<SoundPreferences>` is strict, for incoming messages: it returns `err` for a non-object, an array, `{}`, an unknown key, a non-boolean `enabled`, an unknown `themeId`, or a `volume` that is not an integer from 0 to 100 (a key present with value `undefined` also counts as invalid); a valid one-, two- or three-key patch returns the merged preferences and never mutates `current`.
- `isSoundAudible(preferences)` = `enabled && volume > 0`.
- Files created: `lib/sound/sound-preferences.ts` (91 lines), `tests/unit/lib/sound/sound-preferences.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/sound/sound-preferences.test.ts` (42 tests, unit) — defaults; sanitize with non-objects, a valid value, per-field fallback, unknown theme, non-boolean enabled, volume rounding/clamping (49.6, −3, 140, 0, 100), non-numeric/`NaN`/`Infinity` volume, unknown keys; patch rejection table (13 cases), one-, two- and three-key patches, volume bounds, a deep-frozen `current` left untouched; `isSoundAudible` (on, off, volume 0).
- Relevant notes/decisions: nothing here imports the store, React, `browser` or Web Audio. The lenient/strict split is on purpose: a corrupt persisted field must not reset the user's other choices, while a malformed message from another context must be rejected whole.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint` and the full suite are clean.

- `store/preferences.slice.ts`: `PreferencesSlice { preferences: { sound: SoundPreferences }; setSoundPreferences(patch: unknown): void }`, created by `createPreferencesSlice()` (no dependencies, same factory shape as the other slices) and composed in `createAppState`. The initial state is `DEFAULT_SOUND_PREFERENCES`.

Nothing is persisted yet: `partialize` and `merge` are untouched, so a restart still resets the preferences until M5.T7. The invalid-patch case is silent here; the background handler (M5.T8) is where a rejection can be logged.

### M5.T7 — Persist the sound preferences (no version bump)
- `PersistedState` gains `preferences: PreferencesState` and `partialize` writes it, so `preferences.sound` survives a browser restart; `ui` is still not persisted.
- The slice-aware `merge` gives `{ sound: sanitizeSoundPreferences(saved.preferences.sound) }` when `saved.preferences` is present (each corrupt field falls back to its own default, M5.T5) and keeps the current defaults when it is absent, which is every payload saved before this task. There is no `migrate` branch and the persist `version` stays **4**; a comment next to the version notes explain why (the merge already covers a missing field, CI-2).

The existing `partialize` test now expects `preferences` among the persisted keys.

### M5.T10 — `SoundPlayer` port, direct player and the `PLAY_SOUND` message
- `lib/sound/sound-player.ts`: `SoundPlayRequest { themeId, cue, volume }`; `isSoundPlayRequest(value: unknown)` (an object with a known theme, the cue `'start'` or `'end'`, and a whole volume from 0 to 100); the `SoundPlayer { play(request): Promise<Result<void>> }` port, which every implementation and fake honours without throwing.
- `createDirectSoundPlayer(renderer)` validates the request, then runs `findSoundTheme` → the recipe for the cue → `calculatePlaybackPlan(recipe, volume)` → `renderer.play(plan)`. An invalid request returns `err` without touching the renderer; volume 0 gives an empty plan, so the renderer is not called and the result is `ok`; a renderer that returns `err`, rejects or throws makes `play` resolve `err`.
- `createSilentSoundPlayer()` always returns `ok` and plays nothing (for contexts with no audio, M5.T13).
- New `RuntimeMessage` variant `{ type: 'PLAY_SOUND'; payload: SoundPlayRequest }`. It is neither in `MUTATION_TYPES` nor in the background's `MESSAGE_TYPES`: the background sends it to the offscreen page (M5.T11/T12), it does not listen for it.

- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint` and the full suite are clean.

### M5.T11 — Offscreen document page that plays sounds (Chrome)
- `entrypoints/offscreen/index.html` is a minimal WXT unlisted page, built as `/offscreen.html`, that loads `main.ts`. `<meta name="manifest.include" content="['chrome']">` limits it to the Chrome build (WXT parses the `manifest.` meta tags into the entrypoint options, `include: ['chrome']`).
- `lib/sound/play-sound-listener.ts`: `listenForPlaySound(player: SoundPlayer, logger: Logger): () => void` registers `onMessage('PLAY_SOUND', …)` from the bus and returns the disposer. The payload is validated with `isSoundPlayRequest`: an invalid one is dropped with a `warn` carrying `{ type }` only; a valid one goes to `player.play`, and an `err` result is logged as `error` with the cause and `{ type }`.
- `entrypoints/offscreen/main.ts` only wires `listenForPlaySound(createDirectSoundPlayer(createWebAudioRenderer(() => new AudioContext())), createLogger('offscreen'))`: no state, no store, no other logic.
- Files created: `entrypoints/offscreen/index.html`, `entrypoints/offscreen/main.ts`, `lib/sound/play-sound-listener.ts` (30 lines), `tests/integration/sound/play-sound-listener.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/integration/sound/play-sound-listener.test.ts` (9 tests, integration, `fakeBrowser` + a fake player) — a valid `PLAY_SOUND` reaches `player.play` once with its payload; an unknown theme, the cue `'pause'`, a volume of 500 and a `null` payload never reach the player and log exactly one warning whose only detail is `{ type: 'PLAY_SOUND' }`; a player returning `err` is logged once as `error` with that cause and the send still resolves `ok`; a rejecting player is contained and logged; other message types are ignored with no logs; after the disposer runs, messages are ignored.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the page is not loaded by anything yet; M5.T12 creates it on demand from the background. Because the fake `sendMessage` does not wait for an async handler, the failure tests use `vi.waitFor` on the log records. The `wxt.config.ts` `offscreen` permission belongs to M5.T12.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint` and the full suite are clean. `bun run build` emits `.output/chrome-mv3/offscreen.html` (and its chunk); `bun run build:firefox` emits no `offscreen.html`.

### M5.T12 — Background offscreen player + `offscreen` permission (Chrome)
- `lib/sound/offscreen-sound-player.ts`: the `OffscreenApi { hasDocument(), createDocument(), send(message) }` port, `createOffscreenSoundPlayer(api): SoundPlayer` and `createBrowserOffscreenApi()`, the only code in the project that touches `browser.offscreen`. `hasDocument` is `browser.runtime.getContexts({ contextTypes: ['OFFSCREEN_DOCUMENT'] })` returning at least one context; `createDocument` is `browser.offscreen.createDocument({ url: getURL('/offscreen.html'), reasons: ['AUDIO_PLAYBACK'], justification })`; `send` is the bus's `sendMessage`.
- `play` validates the request with `isSoundPlayRequest`, makes sure the document exists, then sends one `{ type: 'PLAY_SOUND', payload }`. Plays made while the document is being created share one in-flight promise, so `createDocument` runs once (a second call would throw: only one document may exist). The promise is cleared in a `finally`, so after a failed `hasDocument` or `createDocument` the next `play` retries. A failed `send` (an `err`) and any throw become `err`; `play` never rejects.
- `wxt.config.ts` adds the `offscreen` permission **only for the Chrome build** (`browser !== 'firefox'`), with a comment saying why: Firefox has no `offscreen` API and warns on an unknown permission.
- Files created/modified: `lib/sound/offscreen-sound-player.ts` (new, 78 lines), `wxt.config.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/sound/offscreen-sound-player.test.ts` (new).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/sound/offscreen-sound-player.test.ts` (14 tests, unit, a fake `OffscreenApi`) — create, then send, in that order; no creation when the document exists; the document created by one play is reused by the next; two plays started without awaiting create it once and both send; four invalid requests return `err` without touching the API; `createDocument` rejecting, then retried on the next play; `hasDocument` rejecting, then retried; a failing `send` returns its error; a throwing `send` becomes `err`; and for `createBrowserOffscreenApi` (with `browser.offscreen` and `runtime.getContexts` stubbed on `fakeBrowser`): it asks for an `OFFSCREEN_DOCUMENT` context, and creates `/offscreen.html` with the reason `AUDIO_PLAYBACK` and a justification.
- Relevant notes/decisions: Chrome closes an `AUDIO_PLAYBACK` document after about 30 s of silence, so the document is checked on every play and re-created on demand, never kept alive. `createDocument` resolves once the page has loaded, which is after its module script registered the `PLAY_SOUND` listener (M5.T11), so the first message is not lost. The permission itself has no unit test (it lives in the build config); it was checked on the built manifests.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint` and the full suite are clean. The built `.output/chrome-mv3/manifest.json` lists `offscreen` in `permissions`; `.output/firefox-mv3/manifest.json` does not.

- `lib/sound/platform-sound-player.ts`: `selectSoundPlayer(environment: { hasOffscreenApi, createAudioContext? }, factories = DEFAULT_PLAYER_FACTORIES)` returns the offscreen player when `hasOffscreenApi` (Chrome), otherwise the direct player over `createWebAudioRenderer(createAudioContext)` when an audio context can be made (Firefox event page), otherwise the silent player. `PlayerFactories { offscreen(), direct(createAudioContext), silent() }` is injectable.

The card's manual Firefox autoplay check could not be done in that session; it was recorded as open point 23 and passed later (see there).

### M5.T14 — Sound cues: start/end decisions and the 60 s lateness guard (pure)
- `lib/sound/sound-cues.ts`: `END_SOUND_MAX_LATENESS_MS = 60_000`; `shouldPlayEndSound(endsAt: number | null, now: number)` is `true` when `endsAt !== null` and `now - endsAt <= 60 000` (a completion slightly before `endsAt` counts as on time); `createSoundCues({ getPreferences, player, now, logger })` returns `{ sessionStarted(), sessionCompleted(endsAt) }`.
- Nothing plays when `isSoundAudible(preferences)` is false (sounds off, or volume 0). `sessionStarted` plays `{ themeId, cue: 'start', volume }`; `sessionCompleted(endsAt)` plays `cue: 'end'` only when `shouldPlayEndSound(endsAt, now())`. The preferences are read when the method is called, not when the cues are created, so a change applies to the next cue.
- Both methods return `void` and are fire-and-forget. A player `err`, a rejecting player and a player that throws synchronously are all logged as `warn` with `{ cue }` only; nothing is thrown to the caller.
- Files created: `lib/sound/sound-cues.ts` (67 lines), `tests/unit/lib/sound/sound-cues.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/sound/sound-cues.test.ts` (27 tests, unit) — the constant; `shouldPlayEndSound` on time, 3 s, exactly 60 s, 60.001 s, 20 min, 18 h late, a little early and `null`; start cue with the theme and volume from the preferences, preferences read at call time, nothing played when disabled or at volume 0 (for both cues); end cue lateness table (0, 3 s and 60 s play; 60.001 s, 20 min and 18 h stay silent), theme and volume, `endsAt: null` silent, the injected clock judges the lateness, `Date.now` is never read; the three failure modes log one `warn` with `{ cue }` and never throw; a successful play logs nothing.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the guard is the whole answer to the late-completion case: the session still completes and records its score through the existing path, only the chime is skipped. The 60 s value was agreed in planning (it covers alarm jitter and worker wake-up, and stays silent after a sleep or a browser restart). The `catch` block logs, so it is not an empty catch (§1.4).
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint` and the full suite are clean.

### M5.T15 — Background wiring of the start and end sounds
- `BackgroundDependencies` gains `soundPlayer?: SoundPlayer`, defaulting to `createPlatformSoundPlayer()`. `startBackground` builds `createSoundCues({ getPreferences: () => store.getState().preferences.sound, player, now, logger: log.child('sound') })` right after the store.
- **Start sound**: in `applyMutation`, a `TIMER_STARTED` received while `timer.status === 'idle'` that leaves the timer `running` calls `soundCues.sessionStarted()` once (`startTimer` sets `running` synchronously, before its first `await`). A resume (`paused` → `running`) and a `TIMER_STARTED` while already `running` play nothing.
- **End sound**: `SessionCompletionDependencies` gains an optional `onCompleted?: (endsAt: number | null) => void`; the background passes `(endsAt) => soundCues.sessionCompleted(endsAt)`. `handleAlarm` calls it last, with `timer.endsAt` from the snapshot taken before `completeTimer()` cleared it. Both completion paths (the alarm firing and the startup catch-up) go through `handleAlarm`, whose `status !== 'running'` guard limits it to one call per session.
- Pause, reset, duration changes, blocklist mutations and preferences changes never call the player.
- Files modified: `entrypoints/background.ts` (+33 lines, wiring only; every decision stays in `lib/sound/`), `tests/integration/background/sound-cues.test.ts` (new).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/integration/background/sound-cues.test.ts` (14 tests, integration; `startBackground` with a fake player, `FakeAlarmProvider` and an injected clock) — a fresh start plays one `start` with the default theme and volume; pause then resume and a start while running play nothing; a reset then a new start plays again; an on-time alarm plays one `end` and the session is `completed`; an alarm 2 min late plays no `end` but the session still completes, one history entry is recorded and `SESSION_ENDED` is sent once; exactly 60 s late plays, 60.001 s late is silent; the startup catch-up (worker restarted with the alarm still pending) plays one `end` when the deadline passed 30 s ago and none after 5 min, in both cases completing the session; sounds disabled → no call at all and the session still completes; the theme and volume are read from the store at each cue; a `PREFERENCES_SOUND_SET` sent over the bus changes the next cue; duration change, blocklist add, preferences change, pause and reset play nothing.
- Relevant notes/decisions: `entrypoints/background.ts` was already over the 200-line guideline (696 lines before, 729 now); only the wiring above was added. The start condition reads the status before `handleMutation` and the result after it, so no new state is needed. Existing background tests inject no `soundPlayer`: they get the platform player, which is silent in the test environment (no `offscreen`, no `AudioContext`), and none of them changed. Both builds succeed.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint` and the full suite are clean; `bun run build` and `bun run build:firefox` succeed. Open point 23 (manual Firefox autoplay check) could be done from here on, since the sounds are wired; it was done afterwards and passed.

- Tests: `tests/unit/i18n/settings-locales.test.ts` (15 tests, unit) — each of the 12 keys has exactly the agreed English and Italian text; the keys under `settings.sound.themes` match the ids in `SOUND_THEMES` one to one, in both languages (a theme added to the data without a name, or a stray name, fails); the preview label keeps its `$1` placeholder in both languages. The existing EN/IT drift test (`tests/unit/i18n/locales.test.ts`) still passes.

The compiled `.output/chrome-mv3/_locales/{en,it}/messages.json` contain all 12 keys with the right text and placeholders. The card did not list a test; one was added because its criterion that the theme keys match `SoundThemeId` one to one needs an automatic check. The copy follows `PRODUCT.md` (neutral, minimal).

### M5.T17 — Settings tab with a gear icon
- `PopupTab` gains `'settings'`, last in `TAB_ORDER`, with `TAB_LABEL_KEYS.settings = 'tabs.settings'`. The settings tab shows an inline SVG gear instead of text: an original eight-tooth outline plus a hub (`aria-hidden="true"`, `currentColor`, 14 px, no new dependency and no asset file); its accessible name comes from `aria-label={i18n.t('tabs.settings')}`. The four text tabs are unchanged. The gear carries a `popup-tabs__tab--icon` class (`margin-left: auto`), so it sits at the end of the row.
- Same ARIA and keyboard behaviour as the other tabs: roving tabindex, `aria-controls`/`tabButtonId`/`tabPanelId`, arrow keys with wrap-around, Home and End (End now selects Settings).
- `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx`: `SECTIONS.settings` renders a `SettingsSection` that holds only the `settings.title` heading (content arrives with M5.T19). Its heading style (monospace, small, spaced capitals, like the other section titles) is embedded in the section, as the tab bar's styles are, to keep the task to the four files it names.
- Files modified: `store/index.ts` (the `PopupTab` union), `components/common/PopupTabs.tsx` (now 160 lines), `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx`, `tests/unit/components/common/PopupTabs.test.tsx`.
- Dependencies added: none.

The existing wrap-around and Home/End tests now expect Settings as the last tab.

Screenshots of the row and of the Settings panel were looked at: the selected gear is highlighted like the other tabs, and the heading matches the other section titles. The existing e2e suite still passes 9/9.

The heading style could not live in `App.css` without exceeding the four-file limit, so it is embedded; M5.T19 can move it. The panel test renders `<App />` in `PopupTabs.test.tsx` without a background, which works because the Timer tab only needs the read-only store.

### M5.T18 — `use-sound-preview` hook (popup preview playback)
- `components/settings/use-sound-preview.ts`: `useSoundPreview(options?: { createRenderer?, logger? }): { preview: (themeId, volume) => void; previewingThemeId: SoundThemeId | null }`. No component touches Web Audio: the hook builds a `createDirectSoundPlayer` over a renderer (by default `createWebAudioRenderer(() => new AudioContext())`).
- The renderer is created lazily on the first `preview`, which always follows a click, so autoplay is allowed; it is disposed on unmount (and nothing is disposed if no preview ever played).
- `preview(themeId, volume)` plays the theme's `start` sound, then its `end` sound after the start plan's `durationSec` + 0.15 s (timers in whole milliseconds). A new call first runs `stopAll()` and cancels the pending end sound, so previews never overlap. `previewingThemeId` holds the theme while it plays and goes back to `null` when the end sound has finished or the preview is cut off. A volume of 0 (or below, or non-finite) plays nothing, stops a running preview and creates no renderer.
- Errors never reach React: a failed or rejected playback is logged as `warn` with `{ cue }` only; a renderer that cannot be created is logged as `error` and the preview does nothing.
- `preview` keeps a stable identity across renders (it depends only on the injected `createRenderer` and `logger`, whose defaults are module constants).
- Files created: `components/settings/use-sound-preview.ts` (118 lines), `tests/unit/components/settings/use-sound-preview.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/components/settings/use-sound-preview.test.ts` (15 tests, hook, fake renderer and `vi.useFakeTimers`) — no renderer before the first call; the start plan plays at once and the renderer is created once; the end plan plays exactly after the start duration + 0.15 s (checked one millisecond before and at the edge), in start-then-end order; the previewing theme clears at the end of the end sound; a second preview calls `stopAll`, drops the first preview's end sound and takes over; volumes 0, −5 and `NaN` play nothing and create no renderer; dragging to 0 cuts a running preview; unmount disposes the renderer and drops the pending end sound; no dispose without a preview; a stable `preview`; a failed play, a rejecting renderer and a renderer that cannot be created are all contained and logged.
- Relevant notes/decisions: `preview` is declared as a function property (not a method) in the returned type so the settings panel can pass it around without the `unbound-method` lint error. The renderer's `stopAll()` does not cut an echo tail that is already playing (about 0.7 s at most, noted in M5.T4); for the preview this can overlap slightly with the next theme's first sound. The hook never reads the store: the panel (M5.T19) passes the theme and the slider value in.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint` and the full suite are clean.

### M5.T19 — `SoundSettings` panel (toggle, theme list, volume slider)
- `components/settings/SoundSettings.tsx` is the content of the Settings tab, rendered by `SettingsSection` in `App.tsx`. It reads only `selectSoundEnabled`, `selectSoundThemeId` and `selectSoundVolume` and writes only through `requestSetSoundPreferences`: never `setSoundPreferences`, never a browser API, and no timer status (it is not locked during a session).
- **Toggle**: a labelled checkbox (`settings.sound.enabled`); a change sends `{ enabled }`. **Themes**: a `fieldset` with the legend `settings.sound.theme` and one radio per theme in `SOUND_THEMES` order, labelled `settings.sound.themes.<id>`; selecting one sends `{ themeId }`; each row has a preview button (`aria-label` = `settings.sound.preview` filled with the theme name) that calls `preview(themeId, currentSliderValue)` from `useSoundPreview` (M5.T18). **Volume**: `<input type="range" min="0" max="100" step="5">` labelled `settings.sound.volume`, its value shown (and set as `aria-valuetext`) through `settings.sound.volumeValue`.
- The dragged volume lives in local state and is sent once, as `{ volume }`, on `pointerup` or `keyup`, and only if it differs from the stored value; never one message per `input` event. The draft is tied to the stored value it started from (a change made elsewhere wins) and carries a `sent` flag, so a pointer release followed by a key release does not ask twice before the store has caught up.
- When sounds are off, the radios, the preview buttons and the slider are `disabled` (the toggle is not).
- Files created/modified: `components/settings/SoundSettings.tsx` (143 lines; the theme row is an unexported presentational sub-component in the same file), `components/settings/sound-settings.styles.ts` (new, the panel's styles), `tests/unit/components/settings/SoundSettings.test.tsx` (new), `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx` (renders `<SoundSettings />`; the comment about the heading style was updated). `docs/roadmap-en.md`: the card's **Files** line now lists the styles file.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/components/settings/SoundSettings.test.tsx` (23 tests, component; `fakeBrowser.runtime.sendMessage` spied, `useSoundPreview` mocked) — structure (heading, checked toggle, theme group, four radios in data order with the stored one checked, slider range/step/`aria-valuetext`, one named preview button per theme); each control sends exactly its mutation (`{ enabled: false }`, `{ themeId: 'glitch' }`) and never writes the local store; a whole drag sends one `{ volume }` on release and nothing before; the dragged value is shown first; key release sends too; no message when released at the stored value; no second message for the same drag; the slider follows a stored value changed elsewhere; previews use the stored volume, or the dragged one, and send no message; with sounds off the themes, previews and slider are disabled and the toggle is not; it works while the timer is running, paused and completed; the texts are translated in Italian.
- Real-browser checks (built Chrome extension, Playwright with Chromium, not committed): the panel fits the 512 px popup in English and Italian (no horizontal overflow, 340 px tall); end to end, clicking `Glitch` goes through the background and comes back as the checked radio, and switching sounds off disables every radio and the slider. Screenshots were looked at: the first showed the browser's default blue on the checkbox and radios, which broke the design's single phosphor accent, so `accent-color: #7dfba0` was added to the panel's inputs. The existing e2e suite still passes 9/9.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the card allowed a fourth file for the theme row if the component grew past about 150 lines; the row stayed inside the file and the fourth file is the styles file instead, which keeps the component at 143 lines. The test caught a real bug in the first version (the same volume sent twice, once per release event), which is why the `sent` flag exists.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint`, the full suite, `bun run build` and `bun run e2e` are clean.

### M5.T20 — E2E: sound settings and offscreen playback
- `tests/e2e/sound.spec.ts` (3 tests, Playwright on the built Chrome extension). Each test opens its own browser from `launchExtensionContext()`, so no setting and no offscreen document leaks from one test to the next; the spec is skipped when `.output/chrome-mv3` is missing (build first: `bun run build`).
- **Settings persist**: open the popup, the gear tab, pick `Glitch`, set the volume to 20 (the slider is released with a `pointerup`, so the value is sent once) and switch sounds off; the stored `preferences.sound` (read from `chrome.storage.local`) becomes `{ enabled: false, themeId: 'glitch', volume: 20 }`; the popup is closed and reopened and shows the same three values.
- **Sounds on**: before the start there is no offscreen document; after starting a session `chrome.runtime.getContexts({ contextTypes: ['OFFSCREEN_DOCUMENT'] })` returns exactly one within 2 s.
- **Sounds off**: after switching sounds off and starting a session, and waiting 1.5 s, there is none.
- No assertion on audio output (Playwright cannot hear); the end sound is covered by the M5.T15 integration tests.
- Files created/modified: `tests/e2e/sound.spec.ts` (new). `docs/roadmap-en.md`: the card now says the documents are counted from an extension page.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: 3 e2e tests, not counted in the unit-suite total. `bun run e2e` → **12 passing tests** (the 3 new, `popup.spec.ts` and the 8 of the 3D city suite), run three times in a row with the same result.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the documents are counted from the popup page rather than from the service worker (the card said the service worker): both can call `getContexts`, but a service worker can go to sleep, which would let the "none" test pass for the wrong reason. A first version used `check()` and `uncheck()` and failed in the full run: Playwright verifies the state right after the click, but these controls follow the *stored* preference, which reaches the popup a few milliseconds later through the background and the storage. The spec now clicks and then waits for the stored value, and says why in a comment. The same single-writer round trip is why the checkbox updates a moment after a click.
- **Manual verification (not committed)**: with a throwaway script attached to the offscreen page through Chrome's debugging protocol, after a session start the page's `AudioContext` was created, reached `running` (autoplay does not block it) and built exactly the nodes of the Neon start sound (7 oscillators, 6 filters, 10 gains, 1 delay), with no console errors. This settles the M5.T12 point: the first `PLAY_SOUND` message is not lost when the page has just been created. The Firefox side was checked by hand afterwards and passed (open point 23).
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run compile`, `bun run lint`, the unit suite (1896 tests, 137 files), `bun run build` and `bun run e2e` (12 tests, three runs) are clean.

---

## Milestone 6 — Security hardening

### M6.T2 — Hardening of `lib/url/domain.ts`: scheme restriction, canonicalization, and IDN
- `getRegistrableDomain` now reads the host with the browser's own WHATWG `URL` parser before `tldts`, so an entry and a navigation are read exactly as the browser reads them: userinfo dropped (`https://facebook.com@evil.com` → `evil.com`), `\` treated as `/`, case folded, IDN converted to punycode (UTS-46, e.g. `münchen.de` → `xn--mnchen-3ya.de`, Cyrillic `раypal.com` → `xn--ypal-43d9g.com`, full-width letters and `。` mapped to ASCII), IPv4 spellings like `0x7f.1` recognized as IPs.
  - Only `http`/`https`: any explicit other scheme is `{ ok: false }` (`ftp:`, `file:`, `javascript:`, `data:`, `chrome:`, `mailto:`, `about:`, `blob:`, `view-source:`, `ws:` …). Scheme-less input is read as `http`; `host:port` (`facebook.com:8080`) is told apart from a scheme by the bare port. Before this task `ftp://facebook.com`, `chrome://facebook.com` and `mailto:someone@facebook.com` all yielded `facebook.com`.
  - Input containing a control character (`\p{Cc}`) is rejected rather than cleaned up the WHATWG way (`java\tscript:` would become `javascript:`).
  - One trailing dot is the fully qualified spelling and is dropped; an empty label (`facebook.com..`) is rejected.
  - The result is always canonical, `^[a-z0-9-]+(\.[a-z0-9-]+)+$`, checked before returning.
- Files created/modified: `lib/url/domain.ts` (`parseWebUrl` private helper, canonical-form check), `lib/blocking/normalize-entry.ts` (doc comment only). `normalizeEntry`, the background navigation check (`createDistractionPredicate`) and the overlay gate already call the same function, so no other production file changed.
- Dependencies added: none (punycode comes from the built-in `URL`; verified in happy-dom before relying on it).
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/url/domain.test.ts` (+54, unit) — hostile corpus: 15 non-http(s) schemes, http(s) and scheme-less acceptance, trailing dot/case, userinfo and backslash, 7 IDN cases, homograph vs Latin, 16 malformed inputs (control characters, `*`/`|`/`^`, IPv6, hex IPv4, `localhost`, percent-encoded `@`), non-string input, and a no-throw/determinism/charset sweep including a 100 000-character label. `tests/unit/lib/blocking/normalize-entry.test.ts` (+4, unit) — a typed entry and the browser's URL of the same site normalize identically (IDN, case, port); the DRY agreement corpus gained hostile inputs. `tests/integration/background/navigation-normalization.test.ts` (+3, integration) — a Unicode entry blocks the punycode URL the browser reports, a homograph never matches the Latin domain in either direction, and a non-http(s) URL of a blocked domain is not a visit.
- Relevant notes/decisions:
  - **Real bug fixed**: before this task a site typed in Unicode was stored in Unicode while browsers report punycode, and `isDomainBlocked` compares exact strings, so such a site was never blocked.
  - **Display (agreed 2026-11-26, option A)**: the stored and displayed form is the punycode one (`xn--…`); there is no conversion back to Unicode for display. No migration of saved entries (the extension has no released users).
  - `classifyEntryError` (`lib/blocking/entry-error.ts`, UI.T6) still asks `tldts` about the raw input: a newly rejected input (e.g. `ftp://facebook.com`) is classified `invalid`, which shows the generic message. Left unchanged, out of the card's scope.
  - M6.T3 still owns the explicit guard in `lib/blocking/rules.ts`; after this task every domain reaching it is already `[a-z0-9.-]` (Open issues #5 updated).
- Acceptance criteria: verified — only `http`/`https`; trailing dot, uppercase and userinfo canonicalized; IDN/homograph converted to punycode; hostile corpus deterministic with no exceptions; the same canonical form at entry and at the navigation check. `bun run test` (1963 tests, 138 files), `bun run compile`, `bun run lint`, `bun run build`, `bun run build:firefox` and `bun run e2e` (13 tests) are clean.

### M6.T3 — Escaping/validation of `urlFilter` in DNR rule generation
- `buildDnrRules` now interpolates a domain into `urlFilter` only after an explicit `isCanonicalDomain` check (`[a-z0-9-]` labels joined by dots) on top of the `normalizeEntry` canonicalization it already did; an entry that fails either step is dropped, not escaped. Dropping was chosen over escaping because DNR `urlFilter` has no escape character for `*`, `|` or `^`, so there is no safe spelling of such an entry.
  - `*`, `||`, `|` and `^` therefore never produce a wildcard or change the anchoring: every emitted filter matches `^\|\|[a-z0-9.-]+\^$`.
  - `isCanonicalDomain(value)` is exported from `lib/url/domain.ts` and is the same regex `getRegistrableDomain` uses for its own result (DRY).
- Files created/modified: `lib/url/domain.ts` (`isCanonicalDomain` export), `lib/blocking/rules.ts` (guard in `toCanonicalDomain`, comments). `lib/blocking/normalize-entry.ts` needed no change: it already rejects these inputs since M6.T2.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/blocking/rules.test.ts` (+18, unit) — 11 hostile entries never yield an unsafe filter, 5 unrecoverable entries produce no rule, a good entry survives next to hostile ones, IDN/homograph/userinfo inputs only emit `[a-z0-9.-]`. `tests/unit/lib/url/domain.test.ts` (+19, unit) — `isCanonicalDomain` accepts 4 canonical forms and rejects 15 others (uppercase, wildcards, anchors, trailing dot, empty label, Unicode, whitespace, trailing newline). The rules tests were already green before the guard (M6.T2 made `normalizeEntry` strict); the new guard is verified through `isCanonicalDomain`'s red-first tests and is defence in depth.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the "allowlist always wins" criterion no longer exists (superseded by CR1.T4: the blocklist is the only input). The module stays pure, no `browser.declarativeNetRequest` call.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run test` (2000 tests, 138 files), `bun run compile`, `bun run lint`, `bun run build`, `bun run build:firefox` and `bun run e2e` (13 tests) are clean.

### M6.T1 — Message bus hardening: runtime validation and sender provenance
- `lib/messaging/message-guards.ts` (new): `parseRuntimeMessage(raw): Result<RuntimeMessage>` rebuilds every message field by field instead of casting. The result is a **fresh object holding only the fields the variant declares** (a forged `tabId`, at the top level or in the payload, never gets past it). A table typed `{ [T in MessageType]: … }` makes a new `RuntimeMessage` variant fail to compile until it has a guard. Own properties only (a polluted prototype is not a field), total (a throwing getter or proxy is an `err`), and the error names the type at most, never a payload value.
  - Timer commands (`TIMER_STARTED/PAUSED/RESET`) keep their strict empty payload: any field rejects the message. Every other variant ignores extra payload fields. `PREFERENCES_SOUND_SET` keeps only `enabled`/`themeId`/`volume` and rejects an empty or invalid patch. Only the *shape* is checked; domain rules (does the site normalize, is the duration an offered preset, is the timer idle) stay with the consumers.
- `lib/messaging/bus.ts`: `onMessage` now, for **every receiver** (background, offscreen sound listener), (1) skips a message of another type silently, (2) rejects one whose `sender.id !== browser.runtime.id` (`warn`, type only), (3) rejects one that fails the guard (`warn`, type only) and (4) calls the handler with the fresh message **and the `sender`** (`MessageHandler` gained a second argument). The old `as MessageOf<T>` cast is gone.
- `lib/messaging/blocked-attempt-sender.ts` (new): `bindAttemptToSender` rebuilds `SITE_BLOCKED_ATTEMPT` from the sender. No `sender.tab.url` → rejected; the `domain` is taken from the tab URL through `getRegistrableDomain` and the payload's own `domain` is discarded. The background's `handlePassiveMessage` uses it before `malus.handleAttempt`.
- `entrypoints/background.ts`: the no-op `MALUS_APPLIED` and `SESSION_ENDED` listeners are removed (`MESSAGE_TYPES` is now `TIMER_TICK` and `SITE_BLOCKED_ATTEMPT`); the background only sends them, and nothing receives them.
- Files created/modified: the three new `lib/messaging` files above, `lib/messaging/bus.ts`, `lib/messaging/messages.types.ts` (comment only), `entrypoints/background.ts`. `apply-mutation.ts`, `blocklist-payload.ts` and `play-sound-listener.ts` keep their own checks as a second layer (their unit tests call them directly). `entrypoints/blocked-overlay.content.tsx` was not touched: it still sends a `domain`, which the background now ignores.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/messaging/message-guards.test.ts` (+80, unit) — every variant accepted and returned as a fresh equal object, unknown fields dropped, 15 non-message inputs, 27 malformed payloads, strict timer commands, inherited fields, hostile getter/proxy. `tests/integration/messaging/message-hardening.test.ts` (+21, integration, `fakeBrowser`) — foreign id / no sender / no id / spoofed shape rejected for timer commands, blocklist mutations and the offscreen receiver; 11 forged messages cause no crash and no mutation; an injected `tabId` never reaches a handler; handlers get a fresh object; the log carries the type only. `tests/integration/background/malus.test.ts` (+4, integration) — the malus reports the sender tab's domain, not the forged one; no tab, a non-web tab and a foreign extension are ignored.
  - Test infrastructure: `tests/helpers/fake-extension-sender.ts` (new). `fakeBrowser.runtime.sendMessage` delivers no `sender`, so `tests/setup.ts` installs a replacement that delivers as the extension itself and still rejects when nobody listens; `deliverAs` and `sendFromTab` let a test pick the sender. Existing tests changed accordingly: `bus.test.ts` expects the handler's second argument, the CR4.T4/CR5.T2 malformed-payload tests now expect the bus's `warn` `ignored a malformed message` (it fires before the lock and the payload check), `background.test.ts` expects two passive types, `malus.test.ts` answers from a tab.
- Relevant notes/decisions (agreed 2026-11-26): the sender check covers every receiver; extra payload fields are ignored and the guard returns a fresh object (timer commands stay strict); `SITE_BLOCKED_ATTEMPT` needs `sender.tab` and takes its domain from the tab URL; the `tabId` criterion was already met by M2.T8 and no variant carries one. M7.T1 will remove `SITE_BLOCKED_ATTEMPT`, `MALUS_APPLIED` and `SESSION_ENDED` altogether, including their guards and `bindAttemptToSender`.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — unknown type, missing fields or wrong types are discarded without exceptions or mutation; `sender.id` is checked on every receiver and `sender.tab` is required where the payload presupposes it; no `tabId` is read from a payload. `bun run test` (2105 tests, 140 files), `bun run compile`, `bun run lint`, `bun run build`, `bun run build:firefox` and `bun run e2e` (13 tests) are clean.

### M6.T4 — Content-script overlay hardening against the host page
- **Only real clicks answer the overlay.** `startBlockedOverlay` takes an injectable `isTrustedEvent(event)` (default `event.isTrusted`); both buttons check it on the native event and return without unmounting, sending `SITE_BLOCKED_ATTEMPT` or navigating when it is `false`. A page's `shadowRoot.querySelector('button').click()` or a dispatched `MouseEvent` is untrusted; a keyboard activation of the focused button is trusted. `BlockedOverlay`'s `onProceed`/`onGoBack` now receive the click event (type change only; the component itself has no trust logic).
- **The overlay comes back.** While it is mounted, a `MutationObserver` on `document.documentElement` (`childList`, `subtree`) notices that the host element is gone (removed, or `document.body` replaced), drops the orphaned React root and calls `evaluate()` again, which remounts only if the session is still running, the domain is still blocked and the user has not answered. The observer exists only while the overlay is mounted, so an unblocked page pays nothing, and it is disconnected on unmount and on teardown.
- **Nothing is read from the page.** No code change was needed: the script registers no `message` listener and reads only `location.href`; the domain is rendered as a React text node. These are now locked by tests.
- Files created/modified: `entrypoints/blocked-overlay.content.tsx`, `components/common/BlockedOverlay.tsx` (prop types only).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/integration/content/blocked-overlay-hardening.test.ts` (new, +13, integration, happy-dom) — a script-fired and a dispatched click on each button send nothing and leave the overlay in place; the injected check can trust a click; host removed, `document.body` replaced and three removals in a row end with exactly one overlay; no remount after "Open anyway", after teardown or once the session is no longer running; no `message` listener on `window`/`document`; the domain is not taken from page content. `tests/unit/components/common/BlockedOverlay.test.tsx` (+1) — a domain containing HTML renders as text. The existing 23 `startBlockedOverlay` calls in `blocked-overlay.test.ts` go through a local helper that injects `isTrustedEvent: () => true`, because happy-dom's `click()` is untrusted.
- Relevant notes/decisions: out of scope as the card says: a page covering the overlay with its own elements or hiding it with inline styles, and the operator tampering with their own browser. A page that removes the host in a tight loop can keep the tab busy remounting it; that costs the page its own responsiveness, not the score (the background measures distraction from the tab URL). The e2e suite does not click the overlay, so the real-browser trusted-click path is covered by the `isTrusted` contract, not by a Playwright test.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. `bun run test` (2119 tests, 141 files), `bun run compile`, `bun run lint`, `bun run build`, `bun run build:firefox` and `bun run e2e` (13 tests) are clean.

## Bug fixes (post-roadmap)

### I001 — Countdown never decremented (timer display frozen at `00:25:00`)
- **Problem**: `TimerDisplay` showed the session's full duration for the whole session and only changed at completion. `timer.remainingSeconds` had no decrementer: the background never rewrites it while the countdown runs (the session alarm is **one-shot** — `browser.alarms.create(name, { when })`, no `periodInMinutes`), so the display had no update mechanism at all. The M3.T3b deferral note had justified leaving the popup-side countdown out on the grounds that the display "keeps showing the last value the background persisted (updated on each alarm tick / sync)" — there is no such tick, so that justification was factually wrong.
- `store/store.types.ts` — `TimerState` gains `endsAt: number | null`: the absolute epoch ms at which the running countdown reaches zero, or `null` when no countdown is in flight. It is a projection of the background's alarm, which remains the source of truth.
- `lib/timer/countdown.ts` (new) — pure `estimateRemainingSeconds(timer, nowMs)`: projects the countdown from `endsAt` with **ceil** semantics (the last second stays visible until the deadline actually passes), returns the persisted value for `paused`/`idle`/`completed` and for a missing/non-finite `endsAt`, and clamps at zero. The clock is a parameter, never read internally (§1.4).
- `store/timer.slice.ts` — `startTimer` sets `endsAt` on both the fresh-start and the resume path (the same value it schedules the alarm with, so the two cannot drift); `pauseTimer` clears it **and persists the actual remaining time** derived from it; `resetTimer`/`completeTimer` clear it. Persisting the real value on pause is what stops the frozen display from jumping back to `00:25:00`.
- `store/index.ts` — `UiSlice` gains the `setLiveRemainingSeconds(seconds)` action, which finally gives the previously unused `ui.liveRemainingSeconds` field a producer. It normalises non-finite input and skips the write when the value is unchanged (same identity discipline as the other slices).
- `components/timer/use-countdown-estimate.ts` (new) — publishes the estimate once per second while the session is `running`, recomputing from the store's `endsAt` on every tick so a background reconciliation (pause, resume, restart) self-corrects instead of drifting. The interval only exists while running and is cleared on unmount.
- `components/timer/TimerDisplay.tsx` — reads `selectLiveRemainingSeconds` (the estimate) instead of `selectRemainingSeconds`; the component still computes nothing.
- `lib/timer/restore-timer.ts` — re-derives `endsAt` from the still-pending alarm on a service-worker restart, so the estimate resumes from the correct instant; the "no change" identity check now also compares `endsAt`.
- `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx` — calls `useCountdownEstimate()` alongside the existing tick emitter.
- Files created/modified: `store/store.types.ts`, `lib/timer/countdown.ts` (new), `store/timer.slice.ts`, `store/index.ts`, `components/timer/use-countdown-estimate.ts` (new), `components/timer/TimerDisplay.tsx`, `lib/timer/restore-timer.ts`, `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx`, plus test fixtures that construct a `TimerState`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/timer/countdown.test.ts` (9 tests, unit) — projection from the end time; one-second cadence; ceil semantics on the final second; clamping at zero past the deadline; the persisted value for paused/idle/completed; fallback when `endsAt` is missing; non-finite input degrades to the persisted value; purity. `tests/unit/components/timer/use-countdown-estimate.test.tsx` (6 tests, component with fake timers) — publishes while idle; counts down once per second while running; freezes on pause; stops the interval on unmount; re-derives from the store so a reconciliation self-corrects; reaches zero and never goes negative. `tests/unit/store/timer-slice.test.ts` (+4 tests, updated) — `endsAt` matches the alarm on start; pause persists the elapsed countdown instead of the full duration; resume continues from the frozen value and re-projects `endsAt`; `endsAt` is cleared on pause/complete. `tests/unit/lib/timer/restore-timer.test.ts` (+2 tests, updated) — the projected end time is re-derived from the pending alarm; it is cleared when reverting to paused. `tests/integration/popup/app-composition.test.tsx` (+1 test) — the displayed timer follows the session's end time through the real composition.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the fix implements the option the M3.T3b card explicitly allowed (`ui.liveRemainingSeconds`) rather than inventing a new mechanism. The estimate is derived from the **store's** `endsAt` on every tick instead of from a locally captured deadline, so there is no drift to reconcile: any background write (pause, resume, restart) is picked up on the next tick. `pauseTimer` persisting the real remaining time is a small correctness fix that the estimate made necessary — without it the frozen display would show the session's full duration. The M3.T3b changelog note's incorrect justification is corrected by this entry.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — the display counts down once per second; pausing freezes it at the real value and resuming continues from it; a restart re-derives the deadline from the alarm; the value converges to the background's state at each reconciliation; `TimerDisplay` still computes nothing. Confirmed in a real browser (built extension loaded into Chromium): `00:25:00` → `00:24:52` while running, frozen at `00:24:52` across a 3s pause, then `00:24:50` after resuming.

### I003 — Popup city area enlarged (60 × 20) with the growth engine's floor ceiling raised

- **Problem**: the city canvas was a fixed **40 × 12** grid rendered at `0.75rem` (~360 × 144 px). Enlarging it alone was not enough: `buildHouse` drew its floor count from `randInt(rng, FLOORS_RANGE.min, FLOORS_RANGE.max)` with `FLOORS_RANGE = { min: 1, max: 4 }`, so the tallest possible house was `top(1) + 4 floors + base(2) = 7` rows and the loop below it only *clamped down* to the band — it never scaled up. Measured before the fix: the city saturated at **201 characters, full at tick 201 = 6.7 minutes** of a 25-minute session, so ~73% of every session rendered a frozen city, and at 60 × 20 five of the thirteen house-band rows were permanently empty. Taller grids bought only sky (6% dense) and dead space.
- `lib/city/city-size.ts` (new) — the canonical geometry: `CITY_WIDTH_CHARS = 60`, `CITY_HEIGHT_ROWS = 20`, `CITY_FONT_SIZE_REM = 0.85`. Single source of truth consumed by both the store default and `CityCanvas`'s CSS, so the on-screen box cannot drift from the grid the engine fills. Only the total size lives here; the band split stays derived from the height by `bandOf` in `growth-engine.ts`.
- `store/city.slice.ts` — `DEFAULT_CITY_WIDTH`/`DEFAULT_CITY_HEIGHT` are now re-exports of `CITY_WIDTH_CHARS`/`CITY_HEIGHT_ROWS`, so every existing importer (including all tests) keeps working unchanged. `store/index.ts` needed no edit: it already consumes those names as the injected `width`/`height` dependencies, which is what propagates the new size through the app.
- `lib/city/growth-engine.ts` — `buildHouse` now raises its floor draw's ceiling to the whole remaining budget (`Math.max(FLOORS_RANGE.min, budget)`) instead of the constant, so a tall band is filled with tall houses. `FLOORS_RANGE.max` is re-documented as a *minimum* ceiling — the variety floor for short bands — never a hard cap. The roof and base are still reserved before floors are spent, so completeness is unchanged.
- `components/city/CityCanvas.tsx` — `font-size` interpolates `CITY_FONT_SIZE_REM` instead of the literal `0.75rem`; the canvas already derived its box from `layers.background.width/height` (`ch`/`em`), so no sizing logic changed. `box-sizing` is now **`content-box`** (was `border-box`): under border-box the 1px border ate 2px of the `one ch per column` content width, so the absolutely-positioned layers overflowed and the rightmost column and bottom row were clipped. This was pre-existing (it also clipped at 40 × 12, just less visibly) and is now fixed.
- `entrypoints/popup/App.css` — the comment claiming "the canvas is 40ch wide" was corrected.
- Files created/modified: `lib/city/city-size.ts` (new), `store/city.slice.ts`, `lib/city/growth-engine.ts`, `components/city/CityCanvas.tsx`, `entrypoints/popup/App.css`, `tests/unit/lib/city/growth-engine.test.ts`; deleted `tests/unit/lib/city/dbg7.test.ts` and `tests/unit/lib/city/dbg8.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/city/growth-engine.test.ts` (16 tests, unit — 12 existing + 4 new). The four new **regression guards** are written against the real 60 × 20 size rather than a toy grid: houses exceed the old 7-row ceiling (excluding the pavement row, which is contiguous with the houses and would otherwise make the old maximum look like 8); no permanently empty row remains in the houses band; the city is still growing past tick 201 (expressed in ticks, not wall-clock minutes, so the guard survives a cadence retune); capacity is more than 2× the previous 40 × 12. Each was verified to **fail against the unfixed engine** and pass with the fix. Two long-standing failures were also repaired — neither was a code bug: *"spreads the first characters across the three layers"* used a 4 × 3 grid, but `bandOf` gives the sky a share only from `SKY_MIN_GRID_HEIGHT` (10 rows) up, so the rotation could never reach `background`; and *"stops at capacity"* asserted the fillable count equals total grid capacity, while the band layout only fills structures inside their own band. Deleting `dbg7`/`dbg8` (which asserted nothing, only `console.log`) removed all 8 outstanding lint errors.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the fix is deliberately **size-agnostic** — `budget` is derived from the band, so 40 × 12 still yields 201 characters (unchanged behaviour) while 60 × 20 yields 452. `composeBuilding` and `BUILDING_UNLOCK_SCHEDULE` were left untouched, so `calculate-population` (which reduces over the schedule) is unaffected. `SKY_MIN_GRID_HEIGHT` and `STREET_MAX_SHARE` were also left alone: both are size-relative and behave correctly at H=20 (sky 4, street 3). **Measured after the fix**: 452 characters, saturating at tick 452 = **15.1 min** of a 25-minute session, with all 13 house-band rows used and the tallest house 12 rows. The remaining ~10 minutes still freeze; **72 × 24** would fill at 24.2 min and match the session, left as a follow-up rather than applied here. Post-fix the city renders recognizable buildings with intact roofs rather than scattered glyphs (see issue #18, which no longer matches the code).
- Acceptance criteria: verified — `bun run compile` clean; `bun run test` → **602 passing tests across 69 files** (baseline was 601 with 2 failing); `bun run lint` → **clean, exit 0** (baseline had 8 errors); `bun run build` and `bun run build:firefox` both succeed. Confirmed in a real browser (built extension loaded into Chromium): the City tab's intrinsic size is **512 × 334 px** (was 322 × 172), the canvas renders 20 rows × 60 cells per layer with **no overflow on an idle city** (491/491), no page-level scrollbar, no runtime errors, and the city visibly grows while the session runs (0 → 188 → 382 drawn cells). The export path scales automatically: 496 × 296 px at the new size. The transient canvas `scrollWidth` overshoot seen *mid-animation* comes from `framer-motion`'s exiting cells (M3.T5) and is clipped by the container's `overflow: hidden`; it is pre-existing and independent of the size.

### I004 — Population counter frozen at 0 while the timer runs (live update + restart-safe accumulator)

- **Problem**: `PopulationCounter` showed `0` for the entire session and only jumped to the real value when the end-of-session alarm fired. The pure `calculatePopulation` (M2.T18), the `score.population` field and the counter component were all correct, but the **only** production caller of `setPopulation` was `createSessionCompletion.handleAlarm` (M3.T3c): nothing recomputed the population while the session was running, contradicting the M2.T18 card ("population is a function of elapsed focus time, it keeps evolving"). A second, latent bug was found while tracing: the growth tracker's `focusedMs` accumulator was a closure variable, so it did not survive a service worker restart — after a mid-session restart the accumulator restarted at 0 and even the completion-time population (and the `SessionSummary`) undercounted the session's real focus time.
- `entrypoints/background.ts` — `createGrowthTick.handleTick` now, after each running-only elapsed delta, computes `calculatePopulation(focusedMs / 60_000)` and calls `setPopulation` when the value changed. The write is deliberately skipped while the value is unchanged: population only moves at the unlock thresholds (5'/10'/20'), and `score` is a persisted slice, so an unconditional 2s-cadence write would churn `storage.local` and re-broadcast sync messages for nothing. The population keeps using the same measure as the city (the tracker's focus time), so the two always agree.
- `store/store.types.ts` — `TimerState` gains `focusedMs: number`: the undistracted focus time accumulated by the growth tracker, in ms. Persisted (it is part of the `timer` slice in `partialize`) so the accumulator survives a service worker restart mid-session; reset to 0 by `startTimer` on every new session, kept across pause/resume, kept on `completeTimer` (the summary is derived from it).
- `store/timer.slice.ts` — new `recordFocusedMs(focusedMs)` action: the tracker hands the running total here on each tick. Guards the untrusted boundary (§1.4: non-finite/negative → 0) and skips the write when the value is unchanged (same identity discipline as the other slices). `startTimer`'s fresh-session path sets `focusedMs: 0`; the resume path and `completeTimer` preserve it.
- `entrypoints/background.ts` (accumulator relocation) — the tracker's closure `focusedMs` is gone: the persisted `timer.focusedMs` is now the single source of truth, read and written through the store. This makes the restart case work for free (the store rehydrates) and removes the session-reset wiring (the slice already resets the field on a new session). `getFocusedMs()` (consumed by session completion, M3.T3c) now reads the store. Writing the accumulator on the same tick as `growCity` adds no persist churn: the city write already flushes the store on every tick.
- Files created/modified: `entrypoints/background.ts`, `lib/score/calculate-population.ts`, `lib/timer/tab-distraction-tracker.ts`, `store/store.types.ts`, `store/timer.slice.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/score/calculate-population.test.ts`, `tests/integration/background/growth-tick.test.ts`, `tests/integration/background/session-completion.test.ts`, `tests/integration/background/malus.test.ts`, `tests/integration/messaging/timer-mutation-bus.test.ts`, plus test fixtures that construct a `TimerState` (`tests/unit/lib/timer/countdown.test.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/timer/restore-timer.test.ts`, `tests/unit/components/timer/TimerControls.test.tsx`, `tests/unit/components/timer/use-countdown-estimate.test.tsx`, `tests/unit/components/timer/use-timer-tick-emitter.test.tsx`, `tests/unit/i18n/i18n.test.tsx`, `tests/integration/background/navigation-normalization.test.ts`).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/score/calculate-population.test.ts` (13 tests, unit) — cadence constant alignment with the growth tick; zero before a whole tick; +5 per completed focus tick; whole-ticks-only; 3,750 over a 25-minute session; linearity; monotonicity; purity; −5 per completed distraction tick; the clamp at zero; whole distraction ticks only; non-finite distraction input. `tests/integration/background/growth-tick.test.ts` (+7 tests, integration, "population linked to the timer tick (M2.T18 live update)" block) — +5 per tick while running; no change while paused/idle; no store write (same `score` reference) when the value did not change; the accumulator survives a simulated service worker restart; **the population decreases −5 per tick while a blocked tab is open and the city does not grow**; the distraction debt is paid back before growth resumes. `tests/integration/messaging/timer-mutation-bus.test.ts` (+1 test) — the live population reaches a **read-only popup store** through the M1.T8 sync bridge (+5 after one tick, +10 after two), which is the path the user actually sees. `tests/integration/background/malus.test.ts` (1 test updated) — the M2.T16 "keeps destroying one character per 2s" case now asserts the paused city (the old expectation encoded the pre-penalty behaviour). Each new test was verified red against the unfixed code. The M3.T3c session-completion suite re-run green: the completion path still records the same population (it now reads both persisted accumulators) and does not double-apply.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the population write lives in the background's tracker (not the popup) so the single focus measure stays the source of truth for both the city and the population. The pre-existing M3.T3b limitation (issue #9) still applies unchanged: the city — and therefore the population — only accrues while the popup is open, because the popup is what emits the 2s `TIMER_TICK`. A session whose popup was never open still records population 0, consistent with the empty city it built. **Superseded by I006**, which replaces tick sampling with a persisted wall-clock checkpoint plus a 60s background heartbeat.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — `bun run compile` clean; `bun run lint` clean; `bun run test` → **615 passing tests across 69 files**; `bun run build` and `bun run build:firefox` both succeed. The counter evolves live during a running session (+5 per 2s of focus, −5 per 2s on a blocked site, clamped at zero) and the accumulators are restart-safe.

> **Formula revised (same fix).** The first version of this fix kept the M2.T18 card's *milestone* formula (+5 per unlocked house at 5′, +15 per unlocked floor at 10′/20′, max 35), which made the counter jump once every five minutes — reported by the user as "still broken" because the expected behaviour is a visible, continuous increase. The population rule is now **+5 per completed 2-second focus tick**, i.e. it advances on the same fine-grained clock that grows the city (M2.T12): ~3,750 over a 25-minute session, growing linearly and without an upper bound (the grid cap is purely visual, roadmap M2.T12/M2.T18). `calculatePopulation` therefore takes **milliseconds** of focused time instead of minutes, `POPULATION_PER_HOUSE`/`POPULATION_PER_FLOOR` are replaced by `POPULATION_PER_TICK = 5`, and the dependency on `BUILDING_UNLOCK_SCHEDULE` is gone (the score domain no longer imports the city composer — principle D). The per-tick write-skip is kept: a sub-tick delta still completes no tick, so the store is not written twice for the same value. **Note for review**: this deliberately departs from the literal wording of the M2.T18 card ("+5 per house, +15 per building floor"), per explicit user decision; the roadmap card is left unedited and this entry records the divergence.

> **Distraction penalty (same fix).** The population now also **decreases** while the user is on a blocked site: the same formula inverted, **−5 per completed 2-second distraction tick**, per explicit user decision. `calculatePopulation(focusedMs, distractedMs = 0)` returns `max(0, focusTicks − distractionTicks) × 5` — the result is clamped at zero, since population is an inhabitant count. Crucially, the two measures are **mutually exclusive**: `createGrowthTick` now splits each elapsed stretch into either focus or distraction (via the tab tracker's new `isDistracted()`), so time on a blocked site is no longer *also* counted as focus. This is what makes the net effect −5 per tick instead of a wash, and it means **the city pauses while the user is distracted** — a deliberate behaviour change: the M2.T16 integration test previously asserted "a timer tick grows the city by one character and the malus removes one", which now reads "a timer tick no longer grows the city while the tab is blocked, so the malus removes one net". The distraction total lives in the persisted `timer.distractedMs` (mirroring `focusedMs`), so the penalty survives a restart, and `createSessionCompletion` derives the recorded `SessionSummary.population` from both accumulators. The debt is cumulative: after returning to a safe page the focus ticks must first pay back the accumulated distraction ticks before the counter rises again.
