### I005 — User-selectable focus duration (duration-neutral growth and malus)

- **Request**: the session length was hard-coded to 25 minutes (`DEFAULT_FOCUS_SECONDS = 25 * 60`, and a fixed 2s growth cadence). The user asked for a fixed set of presets — **5, 10, 20, 30, 45, 60, 90, 120 minutes** — where "the more the user wants to focus the slower will be the growth, the less they want to focus the faster will be the growth", with a **duration-neutral** reward: every preset builds the same city, only at a different pace ("the rule changes the pace, not the city size"). The malus must scale with the session tick too, so rewards and penalties run at the same pace, and the preset→cadence data must live in **one** place so adding a choice is a one-line edit.
- `lib/timer/focus-presets.ts` (new) — the single source of truth: `PRESET_MINUTES = [5, 10, 20, 30, 45, 60, 90, 120]`, `TOTAL_CHARACTERS_PER_SESSION = 600`, and the derived `FOCUS_PRESETS` (`minutes`, `durationSeconds`, `tickMs = durationSeconds × 1000 ÷ TOTAL`). `DEFAULT_FOCUS_PRESET` is **30 min** (25 is no longer a preset; the user chose 30 over the 20 recommended by the plan). Pure lookups: `findPresetByDurationSeconds`, `tickMsForDurationSeconds` (an unknown value degrades to the default cadence, §1.4) and `isPresetDurationSeconds` (the untrusted-input guard). Adding a choice is one entry in `PRESET_MINUTES`: the UI options, the cadence and the validation all derive from it, and the i18n label is generated (`'$1 min'`), so no locale edit is needed per preset.
- **Cadence**: `tickMs = durationSeconds × 1000 ÷ 600`, so the user's example holds exactly — 10 min → 1 s, 20 min → 2 s — while 5 min → 500 ms and 120 min → 12 s. Every preset is worth exactly **600 ticks**, hence the same final city. **Measured grid capacity** (60 × 20, to saturation, 9 seeds) is **439–561 characters**, so a typical session fills ~84% of the grid and freezes for the last stretch — the same was already true of the old fixed 25-min default, which saturated at 67% of its 750 ticks.
- `lib/city/growth-engine.ts` — new primitive `growCityByTicks(layers, ticks, seed)` places exactly N characters; the existing `growCity(layers, elapsedMs, seed)` is kept as the elapsed-time wrapper over the legacy `GROWTH_TICK_MS`, which is now documented as legacy (the running app no longer uses it as the production cadence).
- `lib/score/calculate-population.ts` — `calculatePopulation(focusedMs, distractedMs, tickMs)` now takes the session cadence; it stays duration-neutral because every preset yields the same tick total, and a non-finite/non-positive `tickMs` degrades to 0.
- `lib/timer/tab-distraction-tracker.ts` — `getTicks()` → `getAccumulatedMs()`: the tracker no longer owns a cadence (its `tickMs` dependency and the `MALUS_TICK_MS` import are gone), it just reports the multi-tab-weighted blocked-tab time. `lib/score/apply-malus.ts` drops the now-unused `MALUS_TICK_MS`; `applyMalus`/`malusForDistractionTicks` were already tick-based and are otherwise unchanged (M4.T1 still applies, now per session tick).
- `lib/timer/tick-emitter.ts` — the sampling interval is now a fixed `SAMPLE_INTERVAL_MS = 1_000` (`TICK_INTERVAL_MS` is kept as an alias), decoupled from the per-session growth cadence: a missed or late sample only delays growth, it never loses or duplicates characters.
- `entrypoints/background.ts` — `createGrowthTick` accumulates `focusedMs` and derives a **cumulative target** `floor(focusedMs / tickMs)`, growing the city by the delta of whole ticks. Deriving the target from the *total* focus time (rather than the per-tick elapsed delta) is what lets a cadence longer than the sampling interval work without stalling: the leftover fraction of a tick is carried by construction. `createMalusWiring` converts the tracker's accumulated ms to ticks at the same session cadence, and session completion feeds `calculatePopulation` that cadence too.
- `store/store.types.ts` / `store/timer.slice.ts` — `TimerState` gains `durationSeconds`; new `setFocusDuration` (legal only while idle/completed and only for an offered preset) and `requestSetDuration` (a mutation, since the popup is read-only). `resetTimer` returns to the *selected* duration, not a fixed default, and `DEFAULT_FOCUS_SECONDS` now derives from `DEFAULT_FOCUS_PRESET` (30 min).
- `store/city.slice.ts` — the `CityGrowthEngine` port takes **ticks** now; the slice action is `growCityByTicks`.
- `store/index.ts` — injects `growCityByTicks`; adds the **slice-aware `merge`** the store-analysis (CI-2) flagged as missing, so a persisted snapshot that predates `durationSeconds` keeps the fresh default instead of coming back `undefined`, while a slice that was *not* persisted keeps its current reference (no identity churn during the async hydration). No version bump: `merge` alone handles the added field and avoids the migration write-back that would otherwise fire on every read of an old payload.
- `lib/messaging/messages.types.ts` / `lib/messaging/apply-mutation.ts` — a `TIMER_SET_DURATION` mutation, validated against the preset table (integer + `isPresetDurationSeconds`); a non-preset or mid-session value is ignored.
- `components/timer/DurationPicker.tsx` (new) + `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx` + `components/timer/use-countdown-estimate.ts` — the picker is data-driven from `FOCUS_PRESETS`, disabled while running/paused, and requests the change through the background (read-only popup, M1.T9). The countdown estimate now also republishes when the persisted `remainingSeconds` changes while **not** running, so a new choice updates the idle display immediately (previously the effect only depended on `status`/`endsAt`).
- `locales/en.yml` / `locales/it.yml` — `timer.durationLabel` and `timer.durationOption` (`'$1 min'`).
- Files created/modified: `lib/timer/focus-presets.ts` (new), `components/timer/DurationPicker.tsx` (new), `lib/city/growth-engine.ts`, `lib/score/calculate-population.ts`, `lib/score/apply-malus.ts`, `lib/timer/tab-distraction-tracker.ts`, `lib/timer/tick-emitter.ts`, `components/timer/use-countdown-estimate.ts`, `entrypoints/background.ts`, `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx`, `store/store.types.ts`, `store/timer.slice.ts`, `store/city.slice.ts`, `store/index.ts`, `lib/messaging/messages.types.ts`, `lib/messaging/apply-mutation.ts`, `locales/en.yml`, `locales/it.yml`, and the affected test suites.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/timer/focus-presets.test.ts` (9 tests, new, unit) — the offered lengths and order; seconds/cadence per preset; strictly slower growth for longer presets; the character total is duration-neutral; the example mapping (10 min → 1 s, 20 min → 2 s); the 30-min default; preset recognition; the unknown-duration fallback. `tests/unit/lib/city/growth-engine.test.ts` (+5 tests, new `growCityByTicks` block) — exactly N characters; no-op for zero/negative/non-finite; the fractional part is discarded; equivalence with `growCity`; accumulation across calls. `tests/unit/lib/score/calculate-population.test.ts` (rewritten, 16 tests) — the per-tick constant; zero before a whole tick; whole-tick counting; linearity/monotonicity/purity; the distraction penalty and its zero clamp; non-finite input; a non-positive cadence degrades to 0; **duration-neutrality across every preset**; the default preset advances one tick. `tests/unit/lib/timer/tab-distraction-tracker.test.ts` (rewritten, 8 tests) — accrued ms, not ticks; the close/navigate/dispose stops; the multi-tab weighting; startup tabs. `tests/unit/lib/timer/tick-emitter.test.ts` (updated) — the fixed 1s sampling interval, decoupled from the growth cadence. `tests/unit/store/timer-slice.test.ts` (+5 tests) — `setFocusDuration` applies an offered preset, ignores a non-preset, is a no-op while running/paused and when unchanged; `resetTimer` keeps the selected duration. `tests/unit/store/city-slice.test.ts` (updated) — the engine port receives whole ticks. `tests/integration/messaging/timer-mutation-bus.test.ts` (+4 tests) — `TIMER_SET_DURATION` applies and persists; a non-preset is rejected; a change while running is ignored; the change reaches a read-only popup store through the M1.T8 bridge. `tests/integration/store/store.test.ts` (+1 test) — a snapshot that predates `durationSeconds` rehydrates with the default plus its persisted values (the CI-2 guard); the granular selector now expects 30 min. `tests/integration/background/growth-tick.test.ts`, `session-completion.test.ts`, `malus.test.ts`, `tests/unit/background/malus-wiring.test.ts`, `tests/integration/popup/app-composition.test.tsx` (+2 picker tests, plus an explicit per-test reset of the shared module store) and the timer component fixtures were moved to the session cadence (30 min → 3 s).
- Relevant notes/decisions: presets were chosen over a free-form duration (lower complexity; free-form buys detail, not capability). The reward is duration-neutral because there is no larger grid to fill. Growth derives from the **cumulative** `focusedMs` (option a), which is what makes a cadence longer than the sampling interval safe. `TOTAL = 600` (the user's example) and the default preset is **30 min**; a duration may change only while idle/completed.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — `bun run compile` clean; `bun run lint` clean; `bun run test` → **642 passing tests across 70 files** (baseline 615/69). Every preset yields the same final population tick total (asserted directly); the picker changes the idle display and is disabled while running; a v1 snapshot without `durationSeconds` upgrades in place. Not re-confirmed in a live browser in this session.

### I006 — City and population froze when the popup was closed or the browser lost focus

- **Problem**: the alarm-backed countdown kept running after the popup was closed (or the browser was backgrounded), but the city and population stopped advancing. `TIMER_TICK` was emitted only by `components/timer/use-timer-tick-emitter.ts` while the popup was mounted. Worse, the background's tracker measured progress as "time since the previous tick" and re-based that baseline to `now` on every transition and service-worker wake-up, so the gap was not merely delayed — it was discarded. This is the limitation documented in I004, issue #9 and issue #12 ("a session whose popup was never open has `focusedMs = 0`").
- `lib/timer/session-progress.ts` (new) — pure `advanceProgress(timer, nowMs, { distracted, tickMs })`. It reads the persisted `timer.lastAccountedAt` checkpoint, caps the stretch at `endsAt`, splits it into focus or distraction using the current tab observation, and returns the new accumulators, checkpoint, absolute `targetTicks` and population. Non-running timers, a missing checkpoint and non-positive/non-finite deltas are no-ops; an invalid cadence keeps the time but derives no reward.
- `store/store.types.ts` / `store/timer.slice.ts` — `TimerState` gains `lastAccountedAt: number | null`, cleared on pause/reset/completion and when `restoreTimer` reverts a session to paused. New `recordProgress(focusedMs, distractedMs, lastAccountedAt)` persists the two accumulators and the checkpoint in one `set`. The checkpoint is initialized by the background on the running transition (not by the slice), so the accounting and the slice share the background's injected clock.
- `entrypoints/background.ts` — `createGrowthTick` now exposes `account()`: every wake-up credits `now() - lastAccountedAt` at once, grows the city to the absolute tick target and writes the population. It is called by `TIMER_TICK`, the new progress heartbeat, startup reconciliation, `TIMER_PAUSED`/`TIMER_RESET` (before the checkpoint is cleared) and session completion (before the final summary). A 60s one-shot, self-rescheduling `TIMER_PROGRESS_ALARM` (`lib/timer/session.ts`) gives the background its own clock while a session runs and is cleared on every non-running status. Startup awaits the distraction tracker, preserves a persisted checkpoint to catch up a sleep gap, and finalizes a session whose deadline passed while no alarm could fire.
- Files created/modified: `lib/timer/session-progress.ts` (new), `entrypoints/background.ts`, `lib/timer/session.ts`, `lib/timer/restore-timer.ts`, `store/store.types.ts`, `store/timer.slice.ts`, the `TimerState` fixtures, and the background/messaging suites.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/timer/session-progress.test.ts` (new, 9 tests) — non-running/missing-checkpoint/invalid-delta no-ops; full-gap focus credit; distracted stretches booked as penalty; `endsAt` cap; invalid cadence; untrusted accumulator clamping; idempotence. `tests/integration/background/growth-tick.test.ts` (+5 regression tests) — catch-up after the popup stops emitting ticks; catch-up across a worker restart; the trailing stretch before a pause; no credit past the deadline; the progress heartbeat advances the city and re-arms/clears with the timer status. `tests/integration/background/session-completion.test.ts` now asserts that the un-sampled remainder is included in the summary. Run: `bun run test` → **682 passing tests across 73 files**; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` clean.
- Commit: `aa0a20c fix: city and population did not grow/increase when the extension popup was closed`.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the checkpoint is a wall-clock value, not a tick sum, so catch-up is idempotent and a long gap cannot be lost or double-counted. Growth and population remain derived from the same `focusedMs`/`distractedMs` measures. Paused/idle/completed time is never credited because the checkpoint is cleared on those transitions. The whole-gap classification uses the tab state observed at wake-up; without per-interval history, a mixed focus/distraction gap is attributed to that observation (the sampling limitation was already present between ticks). Malus across a worker restart is still a separate known gap.

### I007 — Start stayed clickable after the session completed

- **Problem**: when the countdown reached zero the timer entered `completed`, but `TimerControls` only disabled Start while `running`. The button remained clickable; pressing it requested `TIMER_STARTED`, which fell through `startTimer`'s idle branch with `remainingSeconds = 0` and produced a zero-length session (immediately finalized), so it appeared to do nothing. A completed session is not resumable — only Reset can return it to `idle`.
- `components/timer/TimerControls.tsx` — Start is disabled while `completed` as well as while `running`. Pause was already disabled outside `running` and Reset is enabled outside `idle`, so at completion Reset is the only active control.
- `store/timer.slice.ts` — `startTimer` now early-returns on `completed`, matching the documented invariant and preventing a stale/programmatic start from creating a zero-length session.
- Tests: `tests/unit/components/timer/TimerControls.test.tsx` (+1) — completed leaves Start/Pause disabled and Reset enabled; `tests/unit/store/timer-slice.test.ts` (+1) — `startTimer` on a completed timer keeps the same state and schedules no alarm; `tests/integration/popup/app-composition.test.tsx` (+1) — after the real session alarm fires and the popup syncs, only Reset is active. Run: `bun run test` → **685 passing tests across 73 files**; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` clean.
- Commit: `e7939b6 feat(TimerControls): disable Start button when session is completed; update changelog for I007`.

### I008 — Blocked-site overlay reappeared after choosing "Yes"

- **Problem**: on a blocked site, pressing **Yes** closed the overlay and then immediately re-mounted it, so the confirmation had to be answered twice. `unmount()` itself was correct: the content script re-evaluates on every persisted-store change (`reader.subscribe` → `refreshAndEvaluate` → `evaluate`), and the user's own choice triggers that change — `SITE_BLOCKED_ATTEMPT` with `choice: 'proceed'` starts the malus (M2.T10/M2.T16), the background persists the store, and the content script observes the write. `evaluate` only checked `dismissed`, which is the **No** path (it keeps the encouragement on screen); nothing recorded the **Yes** decision, so the still-blocked/running snapshot mounted a fresh overlay.
- `entrypoints/blocked-overlay.content.tsx` — new per-instance `proceeded` flag, set in `onProceed` before `unmount()` and `reportAttempt(...)`. `evaluate` now early-returns on `dismissed || proceeded`, so no later refresh (store write from the attempt, blocklist edit, background wake-up) can resurrect the overlay for that visit. Lifecycle/detach behaviour is unchanged.
- Files created/modified: `entrypoints/blocked-overlay.content.tsx`, `tests/integration/content/blocked-overlay.test.ts`, `docs/issue/I008-overlay-reappears-after-proceed.md` (new).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/integration/content/blocked-overlay.test.ts` (+1 test, integration with `fakeBrowser`) — after **Yes** the overlay closes, a simulated store refresh (the exact effect of the attempt) does not re-mount it, and the attempt is reported once. Verified red against the unfixed `evaluate` (overlay present after the refresh) and green with the flag. Run: `bun run test` → **686 passing tests across 73 files**; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` clean; both MV3 builds succeed.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the decision is tracked per content-script instance — that is the "visit" the user expects; a new document gets its own instance, as before. **No** keeps its existing meaning (`dismissed` renders the encouragement instead of removing the overlay, because the tab may have no history to return to).
- Acceptance criteria: verified — a single **Yes** dismisses the overlay for the rest of the visit; later store changes do not re-mount it; the **No** encouragement path, shadow-root isolation, idempotence, error logging and teardown tests are unchanged and green. Not re-confirmed in a live browser in this session.
- Commit: `f1c9b87 fix: resolve overlay reappearance issue after confirmation on blocked sites`.

### I009 — Malus not applied to a blocked site already open at session start

- **Problem**: a blocked tab that was already open when the session started accrued no distraction time: after the overlay's **Yes**, only the single immediate character was attempted (and, because the city is empty at session start, that usually removed nothing), and nothing else happened. `tracker.isDistracted()` also stayed `false`, so the growth engine booked the stay as **focus** and the city/population kept growing on the blocked page. A tab opened *after* the start worked because `tabs.onUpdated` registered it.
- Root cause: `TabDistractionTracker` seeds its open-tab set once, at construction, from `queryTabs()`, and otherwise reacts only to `tabs.onUpdated`/`tabs.onRemoved`. The injected `createDistractionPredicate` counts a tab only while `timer.status === 'running'`, and at construction the store is still idle (fresh start) or not yet rehydrated (worker restart). A tab already open on a blocked site never emits `onUpdated` at session start, so it was never registered; `handleTick` then saw zero elapsed distraction ticks and `pump()` did nothing.
- `lib/timer/tab-distraction-tracker.ts` — new `refresh(): Promise<void>` on the tracker: it re-reads the open tabs, samples the elapsed stretch against the previous membership, then replaces the set with the fresh result (so a tab that navigated away stops counting). The initial seed and the refresh share a `syncTabs` helper.
- `entrypoints/background.ts` — the timer-status subscription (`running` branch) calls the new `refreshDistractionTracker()` (logged at the `tab-tracker` boundary, failures contained). Startup awaits one refresh before `growthTick.account()`, so a rehydrated running session classifies the sleep gap with the already-open tabs known.
- Files created/modified: `lib/timer/tab-distraction-tracker.ts`, `entrypoints/background.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/timer/tab-distraction-tracker.test.ts`, `tests/integration/background/malus.test.ts`, `docs/issue/I009-malus-already-open-blocked-tab.md` (new).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/integration/background/malus.test.ts` (+1 test) — a tab that is already open when the session starts still produces a per-tick `MALUS_APPLIED` after **Yes**; verified red before the fix (only the proceed message, no tick message). `tests/unit/lib/timer/tab-distraction-tracker.test.ts` (+2 tests) — `refresh()` registers a tab that was open while the predicate was still false (idle) and credits the following stretch; it drops a tab that navigated away during the re-read while keeping the stretch accrued before it. Run: `bun run test` → **689 passing tests across 73 files**; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` clean; both MV3 builds succeed.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the refresh is deliberately tied to the **running** transition (start, resume, rehydrate), not to every store write — the store changes on every tick, so querying the tabs there would be wasteful. The malus still begins only on the overlay's "Yes" (pre-`proceed` time stays forgiven, M2.T10); the fix does not auto-apply a malus for a tab the user never confirmed. **Known follow-up (not addressed here)**: adding a domain to the blocklist while its tab is already open has the same stale-set shape; it would need a refresh on blocklist/allowlist changes.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — the already-open blocked tab charges the city from the first session tick after **Yes**; the pre-refresh stretch is preserved and a departed tab is dropped; the `onUpdated` path for newly opened sites is unchanged. Not re-confirmed in a live browser in this session.
- Commit: `afc2224 fix: apply malus to blocked sites already open at session start and enhance distraction tracking`.

### I010 — Focus time lost before the first checkpoint (perfect session ended at 599 characters)

- **Problem**: a perfect session ended at 599 of 600 characters (population 6880 instead of 7383). `startTimer`/`resumeTimer` left `lastAccountedAt` empty until the background observed `running`, so the focus time between the start instant and that observation was never credited.
- **Root cause**: the score and population floor the built characters, so one lost millisecond dropped a whole character at the cutoff.
- **Fix**: `store/timer.slice.ts` — the checkpoint is now set to `startedAt` (`startTimer`) and `resumedAt` (`resumeTimer`), the same instant the countdown is armed from, so the whole countdown is accounted for.
- Files created/modified: `store/timer.slice.ts`, `tests/unit/store/timer-slice.test.ts` (+2 tests), `tests/integration/popup/app-composition.test.tsx` (the simulated tick is placed one session tick after the store's own `sessionStartedAt`, not after the test's fixed clock).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/store/timer-slice.test.ts` (+2 tests, unit) — `startTimer` checkpoints the accounting clock at the start instant; resuming checkpoints it at the resume instant. Re-run of `tests/unit/store/timer-slice.test.ts` and `tests/integration/background`: **105 passing tests across 8 files**. Full run: `bun run test` → **873 passing tests across 78 files**; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` clean.
- Relevant notes/decisions: critical logic (§3.4 item 1). The commit was made without a changelog entry; this entry records it after the fact.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — the checkpoint starts at the session start and at each resume, and the timer-slice and background integration suites are green in this run.
- Commit: `4ac0794 fix(timer): checkpoint focus accounting at the session start and resume`.

### I011 — The city is blown out to white by the bloom (buildings almost unrecognizable)

- **Problem**: reported with a screenshot of the full-city page: the whole city is a white glow, the buildings are barely readable and the black ground turns grey-brown. The bloom values of CR6.T6b (strength 0.9, radius 0.5, threshold 0.15) were first guesses, never judged on screen.
- **Root cause**: a city has thousands of thin bright edges and window bands; a bloom with a low threshold and a wide radius adds all their glow together, and the output conversion to sRGB lifts the faint far glow into a grey veil over the ground.
- **Fix**: `components/city/three/post-processing.ts` — strength 0.3, radius 0.2, threshold 0.55. Checked by eye in the real extension (Chromium, the RTX GPU) on a complete city in the popup stage and on the full-city page at the reported size: edges and window bands read as neon outlines with a soft halo, the ground stays dark. Also `components/city/city-panel.css`: the full-city stage is `calc(100vh - 3.8rem)` tall (it was `2.6rem`, which made the page scroll by about 1.2rem).
- Files created/modified: `components/city/three/post-processing.ts`, `components/city/city-panel.css`, `tests/unit/components/city/three/post-processing.test.ts` (+1 test), `DESIGN.md`, `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/changelog.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `post-processing.test.ts` (+1, unit) — the bloom stays under strength 0.4 and radius 0.3 and over threshold 0.45, so a return to the blown-out values fails. Full suite and e2e green.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the look values were first guesses (see CR6.T6b and CR6.T12); this is the first time they were judged on screen, so they may still want taste adjustments (colors, label size, scanline strength). The numeric guard is deliberately loose.
- Acceptance criteria: verified by eye in the built extension on both the popup stage and the full-city page, and by the unit guard.
- Commit: `97aea07 fix(city): tone down the bloom (I011)`.

### I012 — Window flicker too frequent: many floors flash together

- **Problem**: reported while looking at the city: floors flash too often, all together, and the result looks like a bug.
- **Root cause**: the window flicker of CR6.T7 gave each flickering floor a 1.2–4 s cycle and dimmed it for 10% of that cycle. With up to 24 floors, a dip started somewhere in the city about every 0.3 s and the dips overlapped.
- **Fix**: `lib/city/ambient-plan.ts` — each flickering window now has one dip per 10–30 s (`FLICKER_PERIOD_MIN_MS`, `FLICKER_PERIOD_MAX_MS`), its start inside the period is random (`phaseMs` in `[0, periodMs)`), and a dip is a fixed 180 ms (two steps of 90 ms: 0.1 then 0.6) whatever the period, so a longer period never makes a longer dip. The share of flickering floors (10%, at most 24) is unchanged. About 7 times fewer dips across the city, roughly one per second.
- Files created/modified: `lib/city/ambient-plan.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/city/ambient-plan.test.ts` (+2 tests, 1 rewritten), `tests/unit/components/city/three/ambient-layer.test.ts` (the sampled window is now 31 s), `DESIGN.md`, `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/changelog.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `ambient-plan.test.ts` (unit) — a window is mostly lit with short dips; a dip stays at or under 250 ms at a 30 s period; every flickering window of a seeded plan has a period of at least 10 s and a phase inside it. Written first, red before the fix. Full suite green (1443 tests), `tsc` and lint clean. Not seen on screen and not run in e2e.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the period range is a taste value; if it still feels busy, lengthen it (for example 20–60 s) before lowering the share of floors. Reduced motion still turns the flicker off entirely.
- Acceptance criteria: unit tests above.
- Commit: `a49e0fc fix(city): space out the window flicker (I012)`.

### I013 — City and population stay at 0 after a blocked site emptied the city (by design, documented)

- **Report**: starting a session and opening a blocked site in the first seconds empties the city and sets the population to 0; after closing the site both stay at 0 for some seconds before growing again.
- **Analysis**: two causes. (1) A counting bug, fixed separately as I014: the stretch was booked to the tab state at the next heartbeat, which could inflate the charge. (2) The rule: `builtCharacters = max(0, focus characters − escalated malus)` over the session totals, clamped only for display, so a malus larger than the city is a debt that focus pays back before anything grows. Measured in the built extension: early and late distraction with the same totals end at the same count (2 and 3 characters, the difference being 22 ms at a character boundary), so the early user is not penalized more; the freeze is the debt being paid back.
- **Decision**: keep the debt model. It makes the final score depend only on how much focus and distraction a session had, never on their order. A step-by-step count that forgives the excess was implemented and rejected, because it made early distraction cheaper than late distraction.
- **Documentation**: `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md` §5 (the rule, a worked example, why), `docs/features-analysis/score-population.md` §1.1, `README.md` (the Score feature), the comment on `calculateBuiltCharacters`, `.serena/memories/core.md`, `docs/changelog.md`. No code behaviour changed.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: none added; the existing integration test `growth-tick.test.ts` ("pays back the accumulated distraction before growing again") already guards the debt.
- Acceptance criteria: the rule and its reason are written where a contributor (feature docs, code comment) and a user (README) will find them.
- Commit: `9a194f0 docs(score): document the malus debt (I013)`.

### I014 — Distraction booked to the tab state at the next heartbeat, not when it happened

- **Problem**: found while investigating a report that the city and population stay at 0 for a while after closing a blocked site opened at the start of a session. Measured in the built extension (Playwright, real Chromium, 30-minute session, popup closed as it is in real use): a 20 s visit to a blocked site that ended before the 60 s heartbeat was charged **0 ms** of distraction (the whole minute counted as focus, 20 characters built); 44 s of focus followed by 15 s on a blocked site still open at the heartbeat was charged **59 s** of distraction (0 characters, about 41 owed). With the popup open the same runs were accurate to a second.
- **Root cause**: the background accounts only on the popup's 1 s tick, the 60 s heartbeat, pause/reset/added sites and worker wake-ups, and `advanceProgress` books the whole stretch since the last checkpoint to the tab state *at that instant* (`isDistracted()`). Opening or closing a blocked tab did not account, so the charge depended on where the user happened to be when the next accounting ran, and reopening the popup booked the whole gap to the current tab state.
- **Fix**: `lib/timer/tab-distraction-tracker.ts` takes an optional `onBeforeDistractedChange` hook, called just before `isDistracted()` flips (a first blocked tab opens, the last one closes or navigates away, or a refresh changes the state) while it still reports the old state; updates that do not flip it (another blocked tab, a normal page) do not call it. `entrypoints/background.ts` binds it to `growthTick.account`, so each stretch is booked to the state it was really spent in. The formula and the escalation are unchanged.
- Verified in the built extension with the same Playwright probe: the 20 s visit is now charged 20.1 s (6 characters built at the heartbeat instead of 20), the focus before the late visit stays focus (45.6 s focus and 14.4 s distraction instead of 1 s and 59 s), and the popup-open runs are unchanged.
- Files created/modified: `lib/timer/tab-distraction-tracker.ts`, `entrypoints/background.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/timer/tab-distraction-tracker.test.ts` (+3 tests), `tests/integration/background/distraction-accounting.test.ts` (new, 2 tests), `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`, `docs/changelog.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: written first and red before the fix (the integration tests reproduced the measured 0 ms and 0 focus). Tracker (unit): the hook fires before a blocked tab opens and before the last one closes, with the old state still visible; it does not fire when the state stays the same; a refresh that flips the state fires it once. Background (integration, heartbeat only): a blocked visit that ends before the heartbeat is charged 20 s, and focus before a visit still open at the heartbeat stays focus. Full suite green (1448 tests), `tsc` and lint clean, both builds succeed.
- Relevant notes/decisions: this fixes *when* time is counted, not the rule. The rule question raised by the same report (a malus larger than the city leaves a debt that focus must pay back before the city grows, so the city looks frozen) is still open; under the current rule the final count depends only on the session's totals, so early and late distraction cost the same (measured: 2 vs 3 characters, the difference being 22 ms of timing at a character boundary).
- Acceptance criteria: unit and integration tests above, and the Playwright measurement.
- Commit: `17355bb fix(timer): count distraction when it flips (I014)`.

### I015 — The regrowing city flickers red after a loss

- **Problem**: reported: after watching floors being destroyed on a blocked site, closing the site and watching the city grow again shows the malus look (red, flickering) on new blocks, not on every block but seemingly at random.
- **Root cause**: the partly paid top floor was `damaged` whenever `lostCharacters = focus characters − built > 0`. That quantity never returns to 0 once a session has lost anything (focus characters only grow, and built grows with them), so for the rest of the session every partial top floor was drawn with the damaged look (`alarm-red` edges flickering on the 1.7 s crack cycle). A character is worth 0.33–0.5 floor, so a partial floor exists on most characters but not all: hence red on some new blocks and not others.
- **Fix**: damage follows the *current fall*. `lib/city/plan/visible-floors.ts` gains the pure `advanceFall(previous, built)`: a drop of `builtCharacters` adds to the fall, any growth resets it to 0, an unchanged count keeps it, and the first observation starts at 0. `store/city.selectors.ts` keeps that state in the `selectVisibleFloors` cache per plan (volatile view state; a new plan starts a new history) and passes the fall as `lostCharacters` to `visibleFloors`. The selector no longer reads the timer; `calculateLostCharacters` is removed. Every consumer of the selector (3D view, text fallback, summary, export) follows.
- Files created/modified: `lib/city/plan/visible-floors.ts`, `store/city.selectors.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/city/plan/visible-floors.test.ts` (the `calculateLostCharacters` tests replaced by 5 `advanceFall` tests), `tests/unit/store/city-selectors.test.ts` (+2, 1 rewritten), `DESIGN.md`, `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`, `docs/changelog.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: written first and red before the fix (the regrowth test saw `damaged: true` on new characters). Full suite green (1452 tests), `tsc` and lint clean. Not checked by eye in the browser.
- Relevant notes/decisions: damage is no longer persisted across a restart (it was derived from the persisted accumulators); a view opened mid-session starts undamaged until the next drop, because it cannot tell a fall from a regrowth and a red regrowing city is the worse mistake. The glitch-out of removed floors (`floor-motion.ts`) is unchanged.
- Acceptance criteria: unit tests above.
- Commit: `03cd489 fix(city): damage only a falling city (I015)`.

### NEON — Vertical neon signs on some buildings

- **Request**: add vertical flickering neon signs to some buildings, like the isometric preview, adapted to the chosen style. Decisions: replace the small box signs; the whole sign buzzes; a sign appears at once when its building completes; the text fallback stays unchanged.
- **Plan (pure)**: `lib/city/plan/plan-props.ts` places a sign on about a quarter of the ordinary buildings (never landmarks or spires, which have beacons), at most `MAX_SIGNS` = 10 (was 12), only where the ground section on the chosen face is at least `MIN_SIGN_BUILDING_LEVELS` = 4 levels. A sign prop now carries `side` (`x`/`z`: the face at the highest x or z, both turned to the default camera), its middle on that face 75% along it, `level` (lowest floor) and `span` (40–70% of the section, `SIGN_SPAN_SHARE`, at least `MIN_SIGN_SPAN` = 3, fitting inside). `PlanProp` gains the optional `span` and `side`. `colorProps` gives a sign another palette color than its building, so it stands out.
- **Buzz (pure)**: `lib/city/ambient-plan.ts` adds one `SignBuzz` per sign (period 6–18 s, seeded, drawn after cars, flicker and rain, which are unchanged) and `signLevel` (a 240 ms stutter, otherwise lit).
- **Drawing**: new `components/city/three/neon-signs.ts`: all signs as one mesh of flat strips (two rails and a rung on every floor line, 0.24 wide, just off the face), sorted by when their building completes so a draw range shows the finished ones; vertex colors at 1.6× so the bloom makes them glow. `ambient-layer.ts` uses it in place of the box mesh (still four ambient draw calls) and repaints it on the ≤ 30 fps ambient tick; reduced motion holds it steady.
- **Checked by eye** in the built extension on the RTX GPU (complete cities, full-city page, zoomed in). A first version drew the ladder with 1 px lines and was unreadable against the window bands; flat strips with the bloom boost read clearly as neon ladders in a contrasting color. The same check showed the zoomed-in complete city looking washed out, almost white; the last commit before this change looks the same, so it is not caused by the signs and is left as an open point.
- Files created/modified: `lib/city/plan/{limits,city-plan.types,plan-props,assign-colors}.ts`, `lib/city/ambient-plan.ts`, `components/city/three/neon-signs.ts` (new), `components/city/three/ambient-layer.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/city/plan/plan-signs.test.ts` (new, 5 tests), `tests/unit/lib/city/plan/assign-colors.test.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/city/ambient-plan.test.ts` (+2), `tests/unit/components/city/three/ambient-layer.test.ts` (+2, indices), `tests/unit/components/city/three/city-scene.test.ts` (indices), `tests/unit/components/city/three/three-mock.ts` (`DoubleSide`), `tests/unit/lib/city/plan/__snapshots__/scene-data.test.ts.snap`, `DESIGN.md`, `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`, `docs/changelog.md`.

The scene snapshot was updated on purpose: the signs consume the plan's random stream differently, so the palette and colors of a given session id changed once (a session in progress during the update is recolored). e2e: 9 passing on the built extension, still 11 draw calls (budget 12), three.js chunk 146.8 KB gzipped.

### BRIGHT — Lower the buildings' brightness

- **Request**: reduce the buildings' brightness a little more, leaving the neon signs as they are (follow-up to the washed-out complete city noted in the NEON entry).
- **Change**: `lib/city/plan/scene-data.ts` gains `WINDOW_BRIGHTNESS` = 0.75 and `EDGE_BRIGHTNESS` = 0.85; `createScenePlan` writes `windowColors` and `edgeColors` at those shares of the palette colors. Bodies keep `BODY_DIM` (0.12) in the renderer, the bloom is unchanged, and the neon signs (`neon-signs.ts`) have their own colors and are untouched. Dimming the pure scene data keeps every consumer consistent: the meshes, the slot writer (animations, flicker) and the exported frame.
- Checked by eye in the built extension on the RTX GPU (complete city, full-city page, zoomed in): the window bands are no longer near white, the palette colors read again, and the signs stand out more than before.

### PERF22 — City tab loading (open issue #22)

- **Request**: on open issue #22 (the first City frame misses its 200 ms budget), do option 1 (identify the unknown function), option 2 (one WebGL context instead of a probe plus the renderer) and option 3 (show the text city while the 3D loads). Option 3 was then reverted at the developer's request: the `LOADING CITY…` line is the preferred look.
- **Option 1, finding**: an unminified build profiled three times (CDP, click to first frame) named the unknown 110 ms: it was `hasWebGl2()`, about **160 ms** every run. The first WebGL2 context of a page starts the GPU side; the check ran synchronously inside the City panel's first render, so the click froze before anything painted.
- **Option 2**: `components/city/webgl-support.ts` replaces `hasWebGl2` with `probeWebGl2()`, which makes the context with the renderer's attributes (`GL_ATTRIBUTES`) and returns a `ProbedCanvas` (`take()` once, `release()` loses the context only if nobody took it). The renderer draws on it: `CityView3D` prop `probe` → `useCityRenderer` → `CityRendererOptions.canvas` → `createGlContext(host, free, canvas)` (`new WebGLRenderer({ ...GL_ATTRIBUTES, canvas, context })`). One context per view instead of two.
- **What made the difference**: measuring option 3 showed the first 3D frame *later*, a steady ~600 ms: React 19 holds back revealing a `Suspense` boundary for about 300 ms after its fallback appeared, and the old `lazy` + `Suspense` with the loading line paid part of that every time. New `components/city/use-city-view.ts` (`useCityView`) shows the loading line, runs the probe after the first paint (`afterFirstPaint`: the next animation frame, then a task) and loads `CityView3D` with a plain dynamic `import()`; the panel shows the 3D view once the module is there. A chunk that fails to load counts as unavailable (text city with its note) instead of reaching the error boundary. Code splitting is unchanged (the Timer tab never requests the chunk; without WebGL2 it is never requested).
- **Measured** (e2e budget test, the built extension): GPU (`E2E_GPU=1`, RTX 3060) first 3D frame **170–223 ms** in five isolated runs (about 172 or about 220, one frame apart), against **446–543 ms** for HEAD `cde6475` in four runs; software GL 196–219 ms. The 200 ms budget is met about half the time and missed by about 20 ms otherwise. Details in `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md` §8.
- **e2e change**: the live-context check now counts distinct contexts, because the renderer asks the probed canvas for its context again and gets the same one (the instrumented `getContext` saw two calls for one context).
- Files created/modified: `components/city/webgl-support.ts`, `components/city/use-city-view.ts` (new), `components/city/CityPanel.tsx`, `components/city/CityView3D.tsx`, `components/city/use-city-renderer.ts`, `components/city/three/renderer-context.ts`, `components/city/three/city-renderer.ts`, `components/city/three/city-renderer.types.ts`, `tests/unit/components/city/webgl-support.test.ts` (rewritten, 5 tests), `tests/unit/components/city/CityPanel.test.tsx` (+2, 1 rewritten), `tests/unit/components/city/CityView3D.test.tsx` (+1), `tests/unit/components/city/three/city-renderer.test.ts` (+2), `tests/unit/entrypoints/city-page.test.tsx` (mock), `tests/e2e/city-3d.spec.ts` (distinct contexts, budget 250 ms), `docs/roadmap-en.md` (CR6.T12 status note), `DESIGN.md`, `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/project-structure.md`, `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`, `docs/changelog.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: written first and red before the code. Full suite green (1468 tests), `tsc` and lint clean, build succeeds; e2e 9/9 on software GL, and on the GPU everything passes except the first-frame budget when a run lands at about 220 ms.
- Relevant notes/decisions: a slip during profiling put a scratch-only `minify: false` into `wxt.config.ts` and the first post-change e2e numbers were taken on that unminified build; the line was removed and every number above is from minified builds. The developer accepted the result: the first-frame budget is raised from 200 ms to **250 ms** (`tests/e2e/city-3d.spec.ts` `BUDGET.firstFrameMs`, `city-3d.md` §8), which every run meets while a real regression would still fail it. Open issue #22 is resolved.
- Acceptance criteria: the before/after e2e measurements and the unit tests.
- Commit: `2851fb9 perf(city): open the city tab faster (#22)`.

### I016 — Blocked-site overlay text too small; buttons not rounded

- **Problem**: reported with a screenshot of the overlay on YouTube: the title and the description are too small. Requested: title 24px, description 16px, buttons' text size unchanged but with rounded borders consistent with the rest of the design.
- **Root cause**: the overlay's sheet used `rem`. Inside a content script a rem is the *page's* root font size, and YouTube sets it to 10px, so the 1.25rem title was 12.5px and the 0.95rem description 9.5px there.
- **Fix**: `components/common/BlockedOverlay.tsx` — every size in the sheet is now in `px`: the title 24px, the message and the encouragement 16px, and the other values converted at 16px per rem so the panel looks as before on a normal page (480px max width, 16px margin, 24px padding, 8px corners, 32px halo, 12px/20px gaps, 8px 20px button padding). The buttons are pills (`border-radius: 999px`), as every other action; their text size is unchanged.
- Checked by eye in the built extension on a blocked page whose root font size is 10px: the title, description and pill buttons render at the intended sizes.
- Files created/modified: `components/common/BlockedOverlay.tsx`, `tests/unit/components/common/BlockedOverlay.test.tsx` (+3), `DESIGN.md`, `docs/changelog.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: written first and red before the fix: the title rule has 24px, the message and feedback rules 16px, the sheet has no `rem`, and the button rule has the pill radius. Full suite green (1471 tests), `tsc` and lint clean, build succeeds.
- Acceptance criteria: the unit tests and the screenshot.
- Commit: —

### CR2.T1 — Lock the blocklist at the store layer
- `store/blocklist.slice.ts`: adds the pure predicate `isBlocklistEditable(status)` (true only for `idle`) and the `selectBlocklistEditable` selector (structural `BlocklistLockSource`), then applies the guard in `applyBlocklistMutation` — which covers `addSite`, `removeSite`, `assignTag`, `unassignTag` — and explicitly in `applyPreset`, `createTag` and `deleteTag`. A locked action returns before any `set(...)` and before `sendMutation(...)`: the state reference is unchanged, no subscriber is notified and no message is sent.
- The pure mutation helpers (`addBlocklistSite`, `addPresetToBlocklist`, `removeBlocklistSite`, `upsertTag`, `removeTag`, `assignTagToSite`, `unassignTagFromSite`) are untouched and stay status-agnostic: the lock is a policy of the action, not of normalization.
- `store/index.ts` re-exports `isBlocklistEditable`, `selectBlocklistEditable` and the `BlocklistLockSource` type.
- Files created/modified: `store/blocklist.slice.ts`, `store/index.ts`, `tests/unit/store/blocklist-lock.test.ts` (new).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/store/blocklist-lock.test.ts` (30 tests, unit) — predicate truth table for the four statuses, selector read, per-action locked cases for `running`/`paused`/`completed` (same state reference, no notification, no mutation) and per-action `idle` cases. Full run: `bun run test` → **726 passing tests across 75 files**; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` are clean; `bun run build` and `bun run build:firefox` succeed.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the status check runs before validation, so a locked mutation is a silent no-op regardless of the payload. The existing store and component tests are unchanged and stay green because they run in `idle` (the component tests never set a timer status).
- Acceptance criteria: verified — the predicate and selector are exported, all seven mutating actions are guarded, the pure helpers are untouched, the new file passes, and the full gate is green.
- Commit: `e4dd414 feat(CR2.T1): implement blocklist lock to restrict editing during active timer states`.

### CR2.T2 — Reject blocklist mutations in the background and cover the race/startup windows
- `lib/messaging/apply-mutation.ts`: `createMutationHandler(store, logger = createLogger('mutation'))` now refuses the blocklist/tag branch when `store.getState().timer.status !== 'idle'`. The message is dropped before `setState`, so nothing is persisted; a `debug` record carries only `{ type }` (never the payload or a domain). Timer commands (`TIMER_STARTED`, `TIMER_PAUSED`, `TIMER_RESET`, `TIMER_SET_DURATION`) are unaffected because the guard sits inside the default branch.
- `entrypoints/background.ts`: the handler receives the scoped `log.child('mutation')`, so the record reads `[buildit:background:mutation]`.
- Files created/modified: `lib/messaging/apply-mutation.ts`, `entrypoints/background.ts`, `tests/integration/background/blocklist-lock.test.ts` (new), `tests/integration/messaging/mutation-bus.test.ts`, `tests/integration/background/dnr-rules.test.ts`, `tests/integration/background/malus.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/integration/background/blocklist-lock.test.ts` (2 tests, integration) — a mutation received while a restored `running` session is being reconciled is rejected, not persisted, and logged with scope `lock-test:mutation` and `details: [{ type }]` (the record contains no domain); a popup that acts on a stale `idle` status converges to the persisted blocklist once the `running` transition arrives through `attachStoreSync`. `tests/integration/messaging/mutation-bus.test.ts` (+4 tests) — every one of the seven blocklist/tag mutations is dropped for `running`, `paused` and `completed` with no write, and the same mutations are accepted again after `TIMER_RESET`.
- Updated existing integration tests whose premise was "edit the blocklist while running": `dnr-rules.test.ts` now seeds the list before `startTimer` (rules are installed by the running transition) and its "reacts to a blocklist change made while running" case became "keeps the rules stable when a blocklist change is attempted while running"; `malus.test.ts` seeds the blocked domain before starting the session.
- Full run: `bun run test` → **732 passing tests across 76 files**; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` are clean; `bun run build` and `bun run build:firefox` succeed.
- Relevant notes/decisions: rejection is silent for the user (the UI prevents the action anyway) but visible at `debug` level, which distinguishes "the lock worked" from "the message was lost" without logging user data. Sender provenance remains M6.T1's concern; this task only adds the status policy. The race needs no extra mechanism: the `running` transition is itself a storage write, so the M1.T8 sync bridge replaces the popup's optimistic entry with the persisted state.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — the background refuses all seven mutations outside `idle`, timer commands still work, the startup and race windows are covered by integration tests, the debug record is scoped and payload-free, the adjusted DNR/malus tests are green, and the full gate passes.
- Commit: `8289fc1 feat(CR2): implement blocklist lock to prevent mutations during active timer states`.

### CR3.T1 — Completion bands table and `calculateBuiltCharacters`
- `lib/score/completion-bands.ts`: `COMPLETION_BANDS` (`bad` 0 / 5, `good` 0.3 / 10, `great` 0.7 / 20, `excellent` 1 / 0), the `CompletionLevel` type derived from it, `COMPLETION_BONUS_SHARE = 0.07`, `bandCutoffCharacters(fromRatio, total) = ceil(fromRatio × total)` and `calculateCompletion` (ratio clamped to `[0, 1]`).
- `lib/score/calculate-built-characters.ts`: `calculateBuiltCharacters(focusedMs, distractedMs, durationMs, total)` counts characters from the session duration (not from the rounded `tickMs`), caps focus at `total` and never goes below 0; invalid input degrades to 0.

- Tests: covered by CR3.T7.

### CR3.T3 — Tiered `calculatePopulation` and band-based `calculateScore`
- `calculatePopulation(builtCharacters, total)`: marginal rate per band (5 / 10 / 20), plus `round(0.07 × population before the bonus)` when `builtCharacters === total`; for total 600 it gives `179 → 895`, `180 → 900`, `181 → 910`, `420 → 3300`, `421 → 3320`, `599 → 6880`, `600 → 7383`.
- `calculateScore(builtCharacters, total)`: the level of the highest band whose cutoff is reached; invalid input → `bad`, above `total` → `excellent`.
- `POPULATION_PER_TICK` and `GOOD_MAX_RATIO` are removed.
- Files created/modified: `lib/score/calculate-population.ts`, `lib/score/calculate-score.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: covered by CR3.T7.
- Relevant notes/decisions: no hard-coded total; the 180th character earns 5 and the 181st earns 10 (the rate uses the completion before the character is built).
- Acceptance criteria: verified.
- Commit: `50aa6d2 feat(CR3.T3): rewrite population and score on the completion bands`.

- Also fixes an unchecked band lookup in `calculatePopulation` left by CR3.T3 (strict indexed access).

- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: covered by CR3.T8.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the accounting rules (cap at `endsAt`, pause/resume, reconciliation) are unchanged; only the projection changes.
- Acceptance criteria: verified.
- Commit: `33b4f23 feat(CR3.T4): emit builtCharacters from progress accounting`.

### CR3.T5 — Score slice stores `builtCharacters`; persistence rebuilds the score
- `ScoreState` is `{ builtCharacters, level, population }`; `setBuiltCharacters(n)` normalizes the count and derives population and level through `deriveScoreState` (exported). `setDistractionRatio`, `setPopulation` and `selectDistractionRatio` are removed. The initial state is `0` / `bad` / `0`.
- The persist `merge` rebuilds `score` from `saved.score.builtCharacters` and ignores every other saved score field, so a legacy snapshot is repaired.
- Files created/modified: `store/score.slice.ts`, `store/index.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: covered by CR3.T8 and CR3.T9.

- Acceptance criteria: verified.

- The growth tracker passes `durationMs` to `advanceProgress` and writes `progress.builtCharacters` (skipped while unchanged); session completion recomputes the count with `calculateBuiltCharacters` and calls `setBuiltCharacters`, then builds the `SessionSummary` from `score.level` and `score.population` read after the final write.

### CR4.T1 — Analysis: preset sources, toggles, saved presets, v3 migration
- `docs/features-analysis/saved-presets.md` maps every write and read path of `applyPreset`, `createTag`, `deleteTag`, `assignTag`, `unassignTag`, `tagIds` and `customTags` (store, `apply-mutation.ts`, `messages.types.ts`, persistence, snapshot parsing, components, locales, test footprint) and records the decisions CR4.T2–T6 implement.
- Decisions: entries carry `manual: boolean` + `presetIds: string[]` instead of `tagIds`; applied presets are **persisted** (`appliedPresetIds`), not derived; `userPresets` replaces `customTags` and `Preset` loses `tag`; user ids are `user:<time>-<entropy>`; messages `PRESET_TOGGLE` (explicit `applied` target), `PRESET_SAVE`, `PRESET_RENAME`, `PRESET_DELETE` replace `BLOCKLIST_APPLY_PRESET` and `TAG_*`; lock matrix per operation including the CR5 add-only window; persistence `version: 3` with the v2 migration and the accepted `manual` limit; `parseStoreSnapshot` keeps accepting v2 and v3 (domains only).
- Decisions that refine the CR4 text and need the developer's eye: saving a preset captures the whole current list (no "empty and filled later" in v1); saving clears `manual` on captured entries; deleting a preset keeps its sites blocked (they become manual); CR4.T2 and CR4.T3 land in one commit because the type change breaks the tree (revised in CR4.T2: the new model has its own types, so T2 commits on its own).
- Files created/modified: `docs/features-analysis/saved-presets.md` (new), `docs/roadmap-en.md` (CR4 analysis pointer, entry-shape sentence, T2/T3 file lists), `docs/changelog.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.

- Acceptance criteria: verified — every path mapped, entry-source shape, applied-set representation, id scheme, v2→v3 migration with the accepted limit, message set and lock/CR5 behavior decided, tests to change vs keep listed.

### CR4.T2 — Pure preset-toggle and source logic in `lib/blocking/`
- `lib/blocking/preset-toggle.ts`: `togglePreset(state, preset, applied)` with an explicit target state — on adds the missing domains (canonicalized with `normalizeEntry`, invalid ones skipped) and the preset as a source, restoring domains removed with ×; off drops the id from the applied set and from every entry and deletes only entries left with no source. Also `isPresetApplied`, `computePresetCoverage` (`{ covered, total }`) and `dropSource` (shared with the saved-preset module).
- `lib/blocking/saved-presets.ts`: `saveUserPreset` (snapshots the whole list into an applied preset, clears `manual` on captured entries, rejects an empty list, blank/over-24/duplicate names — case-insensitive across built-in and user presets — and a taken id), `renameUserPreset`, `deleteUserPreset` (orphaned entries become manual, sites stay blocked), `generatePresetId` (`user:<time>-<entropy>`), `MAX_PRESET_NAME_LENGTH`.
- `lib/blocking/preset-toggle.types.ts`: `SourcedEntry`, `PresetBlocklistState`, `PresetShape`, `PresetCoverage`.
- Every function is pure, takes the clock/entropy as arguments, returns new arrays and returns the **same state reference** when nothing changes.
- Files created/modified: the three modules above, `tests/unit/lib/blocking/preset-toggle.test.ts` (16 tests) and `tests/unit/lib/blocking/saved-presets.test.ts` (14 tests), plus docs (`docs/features-analysis/saved-presets.md` §7/§9, `docs/roadmap-en.md` CR4.T2/T3 file lists, this changelog).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: 30 unit tests, written first (red) — apply/un-apply/idempotence, manual and shared entries surviving, restore after ×, canonicalization, no input mutation, coverage, name and id rules, rename edge cases, delete keeping sites.
- Relevant notes/decisions: deviation from the card — the new model has its own types instead of editing `store/store.types.ts`, so the tree stays green and T2 commits alone; the tests sit in `tests/unit/lib/blocking/` (the project convention) rather than `tests/unit/blocking/`. CR4.T3 aligns `BlocklistEntry`/`Preset` with these types. The module is split in two to respect the 200-line rule.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — tests first, apply adds domains and source, un-apply removes only source-less entries, manual and shared entries survive, re-apply idempotent, canonicalization through `normalizeEntry` (the `getRegistrableDomain` wrapper), pure functions with explicit return types; enforcement tests untouched. ### CR4.T3 — Store model, slice actions and persistence v3 migration
- Model: `BlocklistEntry` is `{ domain, manual, presetIds }`, `Preset` is `{ id, name, domains }` (no `tag`) and `BlocklistState` is `{ blocklist, appliedPresetIds, userPresets }`, all in `store/store.types.ts`; `Tag` and `customTags` are gone. The temporary `preset-toggle.types.ts` from CR4.T2 was removed (`PresetCoverage` moved into `preset-toggle.ts`).
- Slice (`store/blocklist.slice.ts`, 198 lines, down from 418): `addSite`, `removeSite`, `togglePreset(presetId, applied)`, `savePreset(name)` (id from the injected clock/entropy), `renamePreset`, `deletePreset`, all through one lock-checked helper (no-ops outside `idle`, one `set`, one message, nothing on a no-op). Selectors: `selectBlocklist`, `selectAppliedPresetIds`, `selectUserPresets`, and the factories `selectIsPresetApplied(id)` / `selectPresetCoveredCount(preset)` (primitives, so they never re-render on their own). Site helpers moved to `lib/blocking/blocklist-sites.ts`; adding a domain only a preset listed makes it `manual`.
- Persistence `version: 3`: `partialize` writes the new shape; `migrate` runs `migratePersistedBlocklist` (`lib/blocking/migrate-presets.ts`): v1 → allowlist subtraction → presets, v2 → presets, v3 → sanitize. `migrateToPresets` maps built-in tag ids to those presets, custom tags to `user:<tag id>` presets (applied when they have entries, empty and un-applied otherwise), unknown tags are dropped and an entry left with no preset source becomes `manual`. `merge` uses the new total `sanitizeBlocklistState` (`lib/blocking/blocklist-state.ts`): duplicates collapse, applied ids must name a real preset, entry sources must be applied, a source-less entry becomes manual. `legacy-blocklist.ts` keeps only the v1/v2 reading (`sanitizeV2Blocklist`, `LegacyEntry`).
- Snapshot: `parseStoreSnapshot` (content script / background boot) accepts v1, v2 and v3 payloads and rejects a malformed `manual`/`presetIds` with a `Result`; enforcement (`rules.ts`, `is-domain-blocked.ts`, `apply-rules.ts`) now takes `Pick<BlocklistEntry, 'domain'>`.
- Needed for the tree to compile, beyond the card: `PRESET_TOGGLE/SAVE/RENAME/DELETE` replace `BLOCKLIST_APPLY_PRESET` and `TAG_*` in `messages.types.ts`, and `apply-mutation.ts` handles them (payload checks; the single `idle`-only lock is unchanged); `TagEditor.tsx` and its test are deleted and the eight tag locale keys removed (EN and IT); `PresetPicker` renders toggles with `aria-pressed`; `SiteList` and `entry-sources.ts` read `presetIds`.
- Files created/modified: `store/store.types.ts`, `store/blocklist.slice.ts`, `store/index.ts`, `lib/blocking/{blocklist-sites,blocklist-state,migrate-presets}.ts` (new), `lib/blocking/{legacy-blocklist,store-snapshot,entry-sources,presets,rules,is-domain-blocked,apply-rules,preset-toggle,saved-presets}.ts`, `lib/messaging/{messages.types,apply-mutation}.ts`, `components/blocklist/{PresetPicker,SiteList}.tsx`, `locales/{en,it}.yml`, tests below, docs; follow-up fixes (wrapped comment in `store-snapshot.ts`, test counts here, `docs/project-structure.md` and `docs/testing-matrix.md` no longer list `TagEditor`) landed in a separate commit.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: new `tests/unit/lib/blocking/blocklist-state.test.ts` (12 tests) and `migrate-presets.test.ts` (14 tests: v1/v2/v3 fixtures); rewritten `tests/unit/store/blocklist-preset.test.ts` (togglePreset and saved-preset actions, selectors), `blocklist-lock.test.ts` (truth table for the new actions in `running`/`paused`/`completed` and the idle cases), `tests/integration/store/store.test.ts` (v1 → v3, v2 → v3, v3 passthrough, persisted shape), `tests/integration/messaging/mutation-bus.test.ts` (preset messages, malformed payloads, lock); `tests/unit/lib/blocking/blocklist-sites.test.ts` (moved from the store folder); additions to `store-snapshot.test.ts`, `PresetPicker.test.tsx` and `SiteList.test.tsx`. Removed: `TagEditor.test.tsx`, `blocklist-tags.test.ts`. Enforcement tests only got fixture-shape edits.

A v2 custom tag named like a built-in preset migrates as a second preset with the same name (names are validated only on save/rename).

### CR4.T4 — Preset mutation messages and background handler
- `lib/messaging/blocklist-payload.ts`: `BlocklistMutation` (the site and preset variants of `MutationMessage`) and `isWellFormedBlocklistMutation`, a total runtime check that each payload has the shape its type declares (non-empty `site`/`presetId`/`id`, boolean `applied`, string `name`, payload an object). It checks shape only: whether a site normalizes, a preset exists or a name is free stays with the pure helpers, and a well-formed request they refuse is a silent no-op.
- `lib/messaging/apply-mutation.ts`: the background handler checks the CR2 lock first, then the payload shape; a malformed payload is dropped, not persisted, and logged at `debug` as `blocklist mutation dropped: malformed payload` with `{ type }` only. `applyBlocklistMutation` now takes a validated `BlocklistMutation`, so its inline type checks are gone. Unknown message types never reach it (only `MUTATION_TYPES` are registered).
- `lib/messaging/mutation-sender.ts`: comment updated (presets instead of tags); the sender contract is unchanged.
- Files created/modified: the three above, `tests/unit/lib/messaging/blocklist-payload.test.ts` (new), `tests/integration/messaging/mutation-bus.test.ts`, `tests/integration/background/blocklist-lock.test.ts`, docs.

- Relevant notes/decisions: the message types themselves landed in CR4.T3 (needed to compile), so `messages.types.ts` is untouched here. The CR5 add-only predicate is not wired: every preset message is still `idle`-only, and `PRESET_TOGGLE.applied` is the field CR5.T2 will read.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — discriminated-union messages validated at the boundary, malformed payloads dropped and logged at `debug` without the payload, CR2.T2 lock cases re-expressed for the new types, DNR rules follow the blocklist through `syncRules`. Gate: `bun run compile` clean, project lint clean, 936 tests across 82 files green, both builds succeed, `bun run e2e` → 1 passing test.

### CR5.T1 — `isBlocklistAddAllowed` and the add-only window in the store
- New `store/blocklist-lock.ts` holds the lock rules: `isBlocklistEditable(status)` (`idle` only, moved here from the slice, unchanged) and the new pure `isBlocklistAddAllowed(status)` (`idle`, `running`, `paused`; `false` for `completed`), with `selectBlocklistEditable`, the new `selectBlocklistAddAllowed` and `BlocklistLockSource`. `store/index.ts` exports them from there.
- In `store/blocklist.slice.ts` the shared `applyBlocklistMutation` takes an `adds` flag: `addSite` and `togglePreset(id, true)` use the wider window, everything else (`removeSite`, preset off, save, rename, delete) stays `idle`-only. A no-op is still the same reference, with no notification and no message.
- Files created/modified: `store/blocklist-lock.ts` (new: the slice was at 198 of 200 lines), `store/blocklist.slice.ts`, `store/index.ts`, `tests/unit/store/blocklist-lock.test.ts`, `tests/unit/components/blocklist/SiteList.test.tsx` (one assertion, see below), docs.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/store/blocklist-lock.test.ts` (+11 added, 6 replaced: the truth table of `isBlocklistAddAllowed`, its selector, `addSite` and preset-on applying with exactly one message in `running` and `paused`, a preset-only site gaining `manual`, both refused in `completed`; removal, preset-off and preset management stay refused in all three locked statuses; the `idle` tests are untouched). Written first and seen failing.
- Relevant notes/decisions: deviations from the card — the predicates live in a new file (the 200-line rule) rather than the slice. The old "locked" cases for `addSite` and preset-on in `running`/`paused` encoded the CR2 rule this task amends, so they moved to the new add-only block. One `SiteList` test (`running`/`paused`) no longer asserts that a submitted form leaves the list unchanged, because the store now accepts the add; the field is still disabled until CR5.T3. Until CR5.T2 the background still drops add-direction messages outside `idle`, so a mid-session add from the popup would be undone by the sync bridge; nothing triggers it yet because the popup controls stay disabled.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — the predicate is pure and exported with its selector, the truth tables pass per action and status, the existing `idle` tests are green unmodified. Gate: `bun run compile` clean, project lint clean (only the 5 known impeccable parsing errors, Open issues #21), 965 tests across 83 files green, both builds succeed.
- Commit: `c2b0758 feat(CR5.T1): allow site adds mid-session in store`.

### CR5.T2 — Background accepts add-direction mutations mid-session
- `lib/messaging/blocklist-payload.ts`: new pure, total `isAddDirectionMutation(message)` — `true` for `BLOCKLIST_ADD_SITE` and for a `PRESET_TOGGLE` whose `applied` is exactly `true`; everything else (including a toggle with a missing or non-boolean `applied`) is `false`. It reads the raw payload because the lock is decided before the payload is trusted, so a forged target cannot widen the window.
- `lib/messaging/apply-mutation.ts`: the handler's lock check uses `isBlocklistAddAllowed(status)` for add-direction messages and `isBlocklistEditable(status)` for the rest, then the shape check as before. Rejections keep the CR2.T2 `debug` log (`blocklist mutation ignored while the timer is not idle`, `{ type }` only); `completed` still drops everything.
- `entrypoints/background.ts`: for an add-direction message received while `running`, the growth tracker first credits the elapsed stretch (`growthTick.account()`, still under the old blocklist), the mutation is applied, then the open tabs are re-read (`refreshDistractionTracker()`), so an already-open tab on the new site distracts only from the add onward. Without the credit step the next accounting would have classified the whole gap since the last checkpoint as distraction.
- Files created/modified: `lib/messaging/blocklist-payload.ts`, `lib/messaging/apply-mutation.ts`, `entrypoints/background.ts`, tests below, docs.
- Dependencies added: none.
