### CR6.T1 — Analysis: city plan model, derivation, renderer, budgets, migration, docs inventory
- `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md` records the decisions CR6.T2a–T13 implement: a reference description of the new model; the current read and write paths of `city.layers`, `city.buildings`, `city.themeId`, `growCityByTicks`, `applyMalus`/`applyMalusToCity`, `malusForDistractionTicks`, `MALUS_APPLIED`, `seedFromSession`, `generateSessionId`, `getBuildingColor`, `selectThemeId`, `buildCityImage` and `calculateBuiltCharacters`; the `CityPlan` shape with fixed limits (12 × 10 ground, 24–40 lots, 120–180 total floors, 1–3 landmarks) and the variation sets; the visible-floor formula; option A; persistence v4; the three.js decisions; the performance budgets; the deletion list for CR6.T9a; and the documentation inventory (living vs historical, with the owning task).
- Findings: `city.buildings` and `selectCityBuildings` are never filled or read (dead); `getBuildingColor` is called with layer names, not domains; `MALUS_APPLIED` has no production listener; the drawing and the score disagree today (30-minute session, 60 s on a blocked site: score −20, drawing −45).
- Option A, with a worked table: the escalation enters `calculateBuiltCharacters` over the cumulative `distractedMs` (stage 1 equals today's linear penalty). In a 30-minute session 60 s on a blocked site costs 44 characters instead of 20; a distraction of 10% of the session moves a score from `great` to `good`, 20% from `good` to `bad`.
- Files created/modified: `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md` (new), `docs/roadmap-en.md` (CR6.T1 status; CR6.T4 and CR6.T5b criteria amended), `.serena/memories/core.md`, `docs/changelog.md`.
- Dependencies added: none (three.js is approved for CR6.T6a; `0.187.0` at the time of writing, exact-pinned there).
- Tests: none — analysis only, no production code.
- Relevant notes/decisions: two points need the developer's confirmation (`city-3d.md` §11): the cumulative `distractedMs` drops the "proceed" baseline and the extra character at "Open anyway"; and the partly paid top floor is *under construction* while nothing was lost and *damaged* only after a loss (the CR6.T4 card said always damaged, which would flash red during normal growth).
- Acceptance criteria: verified — every item of the card is covered in `city-3d.md` (§1 reference description, §2 paths, §3 plan, §4 floors, §5 malus, §6 migration, §7 rendering, §8 budgets, §9 deletions, §10 inventory).
- Commit: `f6185f7 docs(CR6.T1): analyze the three.js city model`.

### CR6.T2a — Plan generator, part 1: seeded road layouts and lots
- `generateCityPlan(seed, options?)` (`lib/city/plan/generate-city-plan.ts`) returns a `GroundPlan`: seed, layout id, offset, the 12 × 10 bounds, road segments and lots. `planRoads` picks one of four layouts (`grid`, `crossroads`, `comb`, `ring`, data in `road-layouts.ts`) and a rigid offset of ±1 cell on each axis, clipped to the ground; `packLots` covers every free cell with a lot (footprints 1×1, 2×1, 1×2, 2×2 by weight, falling back to the largest that fits), so roads and lots tile the ground exactly; `packWithinBounds` retries up to 12 times until the count is inside `MIN_LOTS`–`MAX_LOTS` and keeps the closest attempt. `createRng` (mulberry32), `nextInt` and `pickWeighted` in `rng.ts`; constants in `limits.ts`. Layouts and footprints can be passed as options.
- Files created/modified: `lib/city/plan/{city-plan.types,generate-city-plan,plan-lots,road-layouts,rng,limits}.ts` (new), `tests/unit/lib/city/plan/{plan-lots,rng}.test.ts` (new), `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md` (ground plan as built), `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `docs/roadmap-en.md`, `docs/changelog.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `plan-lots.test.ts` (15 tests) and `rng.test.ts` (8 tests), unit — determinism, no `Math.random`, 1,000-seed invariants (inside the frame, no overlap, exact tiling, one connected road network, lot bounds, ids), 100-seed variation (layouts, offsets, 2×2 counts, footprint kinds, distinct plans) and a custom layout array.
- Relevant notes/decisions: measured over 1,000 seeds the packing gives 32–54 lots, so the bound is **30–48** instead of the 24–40 in the analysis; the total-floors bound of 120–180 does not fit that many buildings and is re-derived in CR6.T2b (about 200–300). `-0` offsets are normalized so equal inputs give deep-equal plans.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — tests first, same seed deep-equal, invariants for 1,000 seeds, measurable variation, a layout is an array entry, files ≤ 200 lines (largest 110) and functions ≤ 40 lines.
- Commit: `6c499e2 feat(CR6.T2a): add seeded roads and lots plan`.

### CR6.T2b — Plan generator, part 2: archetypes, heights, landmarks, build order, names
- `generateCityPlan` returns a `CityPlan`: the ground plan plus `buildings`, `floors` (build order), `props` and `totalFloors`. `placeBuildings` leaves up to 10% of the lots as parks, picks 1–3 landmark lots, and draws a weighted archetype and a height for each other lot from six archetypes (`tower`, `slab`, `stepped`, `twin`, `spire`, `lowBlock`; data in `building-archetypes.ts` with a pure section builder each). `fitTotalFloors` moves regular buildings one level at a time until the total is inside **200–300** and swaps archetypes when heights alone cannot reach it. Landmarks get unique generated names (`tower-names.ts`). `orderFloors` shuffles the buildings and lists each bottom-up. `placeProps` adds lamps (≤ 40), signs (≤ 12) and beacons (≤ 4). `rng.ts` gains `shuffled`.
- Files created/modified: `lib/city/plan/{building-archetypes,tower-names,plan-buildings,build-order,plan-props,fit-total-floors}.ts` (new), `lib/city/plan/{city-plan.types,limits,rng,generate-city-plan}.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/city/plan/generate-city-plan.test.ts` (new), `tests/unit/lib/city/plan/rng.test.ts`, `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `docs/roadmap-en.md`, `docs/changelog.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `generate-city-plan.test.ts` (20 tests, unit) and 2 new `shuffled` tests — determinism, no `Math.random`, 1,000-seed invariants (total floors, parks, ranges, sections inside lots, landmarks and names, exact floor listing and order, props), 100-seed variation, custom archetypes and names, and both directions of the archetype swap.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the total-floors bound moved from 120–180 to **200–300** (2–3 characters per floor) because 30–48 lots cannot share 120–180 floors; the roadmap's "about 4 characters per floor" is corrected. Two helper files (`fit-total-floors.ts`, `plan-props.ts`) bring the task to eight production files, each under 200 lines. Colors were left to CR6.T3. (This entry was added with CR6.T3: it was missing from the CR6.T2b commit.)
- Acceptance criteria: verified — same seed deep-equal, invariants over 1,000 seeds (heights, bounds, landmark, build order), measurable variation over 100 seeds, an archetype or a name is an array entry.
- Commit: `d5a31d2 feat(CR6.T2b): add buildings and build order`.

### CR6.T3 — Session palettes and color assignment
- `generateCityPlan` now draws one of six palettes (`city-palettes.ts`: Neon City, Sunset Strip, Arctic Dawn, Toxic Grid, Synthwave, Amber Terminal; data, six distinct neon colors each plus window, lamp and beacon accents) and an integer hue shift in [−25, 25] from the seed, and returns `paletteId`, `hueShift`, `PlanBuilding.color`/`windowColor` and `PlanProp.color`. `chooseStyle` and `colorBuildings` (`assign-colors.ts`) give every building a random color not used by an already colored neighbour that shares an edge with its lot; `colorProps` gives lamps, signs (their building's color) and beacons theirs. `color-math.ts`: hex↔HSL, `shiftHue`, WCAG `contrastRatio`. `environment-colors.ts`: the fixed floor, grid, rain and HUD colors and `MIN_COLOR_CONTRAST` (4.5).
- Files created/modified: `lib/city/plan/{city-palettes,assign-colors,color-math,environment-colors}.ts` (new), `lib/city/plan/{city-plan.types,limits,generate-city-plan,plan-buildings,build-order,plan-props}.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/city/plan/{assign-colors,color-math}.test.ts` (new), `DESIGN.md` (environment tokens, session palettes, One Beam exception), `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `docs/roadmap-en.md`, `docs/changelog.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `assign-colors.test.ts` (14 tests) and `color-math.test.ts` (5 tests), unit — palette data, readability of every palette color at every hue shift, fixed environment, determinism, 1,000-seed invariants (palette, shift, shifted palette colors, contrast, no shared color across an edge, props, signs), 100-seed variation, custom palettes, no use of the domain-hash palette in the plan folder.
- Relevant notes/decisions: CR6.T1 had not set the minimum luminance the card refers to; it is set here as a **4.5:1 contrast ratio on the black floor**. Adjacency means sharing a side, not facing across a road or touching at a corner. The `.impeccable/design.json` sidecar of DESIGN.md was not regenerated; it is not read by code. The type and helper signatures of the plan folder changed beyond the card's file list (`BuildingShape`, `PropShape`).
- Acceptance criteria: verified — palette and shift from the seed, same seed same colors, no adjacent building shares a color, every color readable on the floor, environment colors constant, domain-hash coloring unused by the plan code, DESIGN.md and the analysis updated.
- Commit: `e28402f feat(CR6.T3): add session palettes and colors`.

### CR6.T4 — Visible-floor derivation, damaged top floor and pure scene data
- `visibleFloors(plan, builtCharacters, total, lostCharacters)` (`lib/city/plan/visible-floors.ts`) returns `{ complete, partialFraction, damaged }` from `built / total × plan.totalFloors`: nothing at 0 or on invalid input, every floor complete at the total, monotonic, newest floors removed first. The partly paid top floor is damaged only when `lostCharacters > 0`, so a growing city never flashes red; one lost character always changes the result (a character is worth 0.33–0.5 floor). `calculateLostCharacters(focusedMs, durationMs, built, total)` is `max(0, focus − built)` from the timer accumulators.
- `createScenePlan(plan)` and `createSceneFrame(scene, plan, visible)` (`scene-data.ts`, `box-geometry.ts`): static typed arrays for every floor in build order (matrices, body and window colors, 12 edge segments per floor, landmark labels) built once per plan, and a cheap per-update frame with the instance and edge counts, the partial floor with its own scaled matrix and edges plus the damaged marker, and the visible labels. `summarizePlan` (`plan-summary.ts`) gives buildings standing, floors built of total, the tallest building and a 0–10 milestone for screen readers.
- `lib/score/calculate-built-characters.ts` exports `calculateFocusCharacters` (the focus part, extracted so the lost-character count does not duplicate it); `calculateBuiltCharacters` behaves exactly as before.
- Files created/modified: `lib/city/plan/{visible-floors,scene-data,box-geometry,plan-summary}.ts` (new), `lib/city/plan/{limits,color-math}.ts`, `lib/score/calculate-built-characters.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/city/plan/{visible-floors,scene-data,plan-summary}.test.ts` and `__snapshots__/scene-data.test.ts.snap` (new), `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `docs/roadmap-en.md`, `docs/changelog.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `visible-floors.test.ts` (14), `scene-data.test.ts` (13) and `plan-summary.test.ts` (6), unit; the existing built-characters tests stay green unchanged — formula, monotonicity, newest-first, single-character change, damaged rules, invalid input, lost characters, snapshot for a fixed seed, array sizes, ground and height bounds over 200 seeds, frame counts, partial floor, labels, summary milestones, purity (no clock, randomness, DOM or three.js in the plan folder).
- Relevant notes/decisions: the scene data is split into a static per-plan part and a per-update frame instead of the single array set sized for the maximum that the analysis first described, because the static part never needs to change while a session grows. Four production files (`box-geometry.ts` beyond the card's list) plus the score export.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — formula and damaged rules, a snapshot for a fixed seed, instance and edge counts equal to the visible floors, pure (no `Date.now()`, browser or three.js imports), docs updated.
- Commit: `6d2ddd3 feat(CR6.T4): derive visible floors and scene data`.

### CR6.T5a — Store and persistence v4: derived city instead of layers
- Persist **version 4**: `partialize` writes `city: { sessionId }` only; `migrateCity` (`lib/city/migrate-city.ts`) turns a v1–v3 city into it, dropping `layers`, `buildings` and `themeId`, and rejects a malformed city with a `Result` (the store logs `city migration rejected` with the key and version only, falls back to no session and migrates the rest normally). `merge` applies only the sanitized session id (`sanitizeSessionId`). A session in progress at the upgrade keeps its timer, score and id, so its plan is derived from the id.
- `store/city.selectors.ts`: `selectCityPlan` (the plan of the current session, generated once per session id) and `selectVisibleFloors` (from the plan, `score.builtCharacters` and the lost characters of the timer), both memoized so a component re-renders only when the city changes; exported from `@/store`. `seedFromSession` moved to `lib/city/plan/seed.ts`; `growth-engine.ts` re-exports it until CR6.T9a.
- **Transitional (expand/contract):** the ASCII `layers`, `buildings` and `themeId` stay in `CityState`, in memory and unpersisted, so the background's growth and malus (CR6.T5b) and their tests keep working until CR6.T9a deletes them. Consequences: the popup rehydrates from storage, so its old ASCII City view is blank until CR6.T8a draws the plan, and a worker restart loses the redrawn ASCII characters (the score and population still count all of them).
- Files created/modified: `lib/city/migrate-city.ts`, `lib/city/plan/seed.ts`, `store/city.selectors.ts` (new), `store/index.ts`, `store/city.slice.ts`, `lib/city/growth-engine.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/city/migrate-city.test.ts`, `tests/unit/store/city-selectors.test.ts`, `tests/integration/store/city-persistence.test.ts` (new), `tests/integration/store/{store,sync-storage,read-only-store}.test.ts`, `tests/integration/background/growth-tick.test.ts`, `CLAUDE.md` (§1.3), `docs/store-analysis.md`, `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `docs/roadmap-en.md`, `docs/changelog.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `migrate-city.test.ts` (12), `city-selectors.test.ts` (8) and `city-persistence.test.ts` (9) — v1–v3 fixtures, malformed input, in-progress session, v4 payload, the selectors' memoization and damaged flag. Existing tests changed only where version 4 or the unpersisted layers show: `store.test.ts` (two payload version expectations 3 → 4), `sync-storage.test.ts` and `read-only-store.test.ts` (their payload helper writes the current version, otherwise the migration re-persists), `growth-tick.test.ts` (after a worker restart the ASCII layers hold the 4 catch-up characters, not 5). Blocklist, timer and history tests are unchanged.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the card says the slice state is reduced to the session id; keeping the ASCII fields in memory until CR6.T5b/T9a is a deliberate deviation so that every commit compiles and passes. `store/index.ts` was already over 200 lines (now about 375).
- Acceptance criteria: verified — `resetCityForNewSession` unchanged (runs only on idle → running), v4 with v2 and v3 fixtures, malformed data rejected with a `Result`, granular memoized selectors, plan computed once per session id, blocklist/timer/history tests green and unmodified, docs updated.
- Commit: `1cd6298 feat(CR6.T5a): persist city as session id only`.

### CR6.T5b — Background and score: progress-only growth tick, escalating malus in `builtCharacters`
- **Option A.** `lib/score/malus-escalation.ts` (new) holds `MALUS_ESCALATION_SCHEDULE` and `malusForDistractionTicks` (M4.T1, moved out of `apply-malus.ts`); `calculateBuiltCharacters` now subtracts `malusForDistractionTicks(floor(distractedMs × total / durationMs))` instead of one character per tick. The first stage equals the old linear cost, so only stays beyond five ticks are harsher: in a 30-minute session 60 s on a blocked site costs 44 characters (not 20) and 5 minutes cost 284. The score, tiers, population and the city's floors lose the same amount. All of `distractedMs` is charged: the seconds before "Open anyway" are no longer forgiven, and the extra character at the choice is gone.
- **Progress-only background.** `advanceProgress` (`lib/timer/session-progress.ts`) lost its `tickMs` option and its `targetTicks` result. The background's accounting step (`createGrowthTick`) records the accumulators and `builtCharacters`; it no longer calls `growCityByTicks`. `createMalusWiring({ store })` no longer applies anything and no longer takes the distraction tracker: it subscribes to the store and, once "Open anyway" was chosen, sends `MALUS_APPLIED { domain, charactersRemoved }` when an accounting step that added distraction time lowers `builtCharacters` (the drop); a drop with no new distraction time (a reset) sends nothing and a new session forgets the choice. `countOccupied` and the tracker's `handleTick` calls are gone.
- Files created/modified: `lib/score/malus-escalation.ts` (new), `lib/score/{apply-malus,calculate-built-characters}.ts`, `lib/timer/session-progress.ts`, `entrypoints/background.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/score/{malus-escalation(new),apply-malus,calculate-built-characters}.test.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/timer/session-progress.test.ts`, `tests/unit/background/malus-wiring.test.ts` (rewritten), `tests/unit/store/timer-slice.test.ts`, `tests/integration/background/{malus,growth-tick,session-completion}.test.ts`, `tests/integration/messaging/timer-mutation-bus.test.ts`, `tests/integration/popup/app-composition.test.tsx`, `CLAUDE.md` ("two clocks" note), `README.md`, `docs/features-analysis/{score-population,city-3d}.md`, `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `docs/roadmap-en.md`, `docs/changelog.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: written first for the score (`calculate-built-characters.test.ts` adds the worked example, long-vs-short stays, monotonicity, the same share at every preset and the 1/5/10/20/33% band table; `malus-escalation.test.ts` is the M4.T1 suite moved). CR3 expectations that moved are named for the escalation: `calculate-built-characters` ("subtracts whole distraction characters" became "charges distraction on the escalation schedule"), and the `session-completion` tier table (a 60-tick distraction that was `great`/4500 is now `good`/2860; 180 and 270 ticks that were `good`/`bad` now leave nothing, `bad`/0; a 20-tick case stays `great`/5620). ASCII-layer assertions in `growth-tick`, `timer-mutation-bus` and `app-composition` now assert `score.builtCharacters`, one per session tick as before. The wiring unit test and the malus integration test were rewritten for the report-only wiring. Population, score-band and history tests are otherwise unchanged.
- Relevant notes/decisions: both open points of CR6.T1 §11 were accepted by the developer (cumulative `distractedMs`, no "proceed" baseline). The ASCII `growCityByTicks`/`applyMalusToCity` store actions, `applyMalus` and their tests stay, unused by the background, until CR6.T9a.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — tests first for the score, a long stay costs more by the schedule, the result stays an integer in `[0, total]`, an undistracted session reaches 100%, the background never writes city geometry, `MALUS_APPLIED` reaches the bus with the drop, CR3 expectations updated with the reason in the test names, docs updated.

### CR6.T6a — Add three.js; renderer lifecycle and pure render scheduler
- `three` and `@types/three` **0.187.0**, exact versions (no caret). `lib/city/render-scheduler.ts` (pure): a frame is drawn only for a pending `floors`, `camera` or `resize` change or a due ambient frame (≤ 30 fps, none under reduced motion); a hidden view draws nothing and keeps its changes pending; `needsLoop` is false when idle or hidden, so no frame callback is requested for a still city. `lib/city/camera-framing.ts` (pure): the fixed isometric framing and the orbit limits (no pan; a quarter turn either side; zoom 0.8–1.8). `components/city/three/renderer-context.ts`: the `WebGLRenderer` (`antialias: false`, `powerPreference: 'low-power'`, `preserveDrawingBuffer: false`, pixel ratio ≤ 1.5), the orthographic camera, the scene, `OrbitControls` without pan or damping, `applyFraming`, `disposeScene`. `components/city/three/city-renderer.ts`: `createCityRenderer` returns a `Result` (an error, never a throw, when WebGL cannot start) with `resize`, `invalidate`, `setVisible`, `setReducedMotion`, `registerDisposable`, `renderCount` and an idempotent `dispose()` that cancels the frame, removes the listeners, frees the scene, the controls, the renderer and the context; `webglcontextlost` is reported through `onContextLost`. It also follows `visibilitychange`. No `chrome.*`/`browser.*` and no store access (a test scans the folder).
- Files created/modified: `package.json`, `bun.lock`, `lib/city/{render-scheduler,camera-framing}.ts`, `components/city/three/{city-renderer,renderer-context}.ts` (new), `tests/unit/lib/city/{render-scheduler,camera-framing}.test.ts`, `tests/unit/components/city/three/city-renderer.test.ts` (new), `CLAUDE.md` (stack line, §1.4 dependency rule), `docs/project-structure.md`, `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `docs/roadmap-en.md`, `docs/changelog.md`, `.serena/memories/tech_stack.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`.
- Dependencies added: `three` `0.187.0` and `@types/three` `0.187.0` (dev), exact — the CR6 exception to the minimal-dependency rule (§1.4), recorded in CR6.T1.
- Tests: `render-scheduler.test.ts` (19), `camera-framing.test.ts` (9) and `city-renderer.test.ts` (27), unit with `three` and `OrbitControls` mocked and a manual frame clock — every frame reason, "nothing changed → no frame", ambient limits, hidden and reduced-motion behavior, pixel-ratio cap, renderer options, orbit limits, context loss, disposal and listener removal, and the no-extension-API scan.
- Relevant notes/decisions: nothing imports the renderer yet (the lazy `CityView3D` is CR6.T8a), so the build and bundle are unchanged; the chunk-size budget is checked by CR6.T12. Two helper files were added beyond the card's list. The scene is empty; CR6.T6b draws the meshes, bloom and CRT pass through `registerDisposable`.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — frames only on floor change, camera move, resize or a due ambient frame; pixel ratio ≤ 1.5, low-power, no MSAA; `dispose()` frees everything and context loss reaches the caller; no browser APIs or store in the renderer; docs updated.

### CR6.T6b — City meshes from scene data, bloom and CRT pass
- `createCityMeshes({ scene, plan })` (`components/city/three/city-meshes.ts`) draws the city in **six draw calls**: ground, grid and roads, the floor bodies and the window bands as two `InstancedMesh`es over one shared box, and all edges as one `LineSegments` with vertex colors. Every floor of the plan is uploaded once; `applyFrame(frame)` sets the instance counts and the edge draw range, rewrites the single partly paid floor (its own scaled box, no lit windows, half-bright; red edges when damaged) and restores the previous slot, and uploads nothing for an unchanged frame. The edge buffers are copies, so the scene data is never written. Bodies are dark (12% of the building color) so the neon edges and the window bands carry the color.
- `lib/city/plan/scene-geometry.ts` (pure): `windowBandMatrices`, `roadVertices`, `scaleColors`; `DAMAGED_EDGE_COLOR` in `environment-colors.ts`. `post-processing.ts`: `EffectComposer` with `RenderPass`, `UnrealBloomPass` at half the view size, the CRT `ShaderPass` and `OutputPass`; `crt-pass.ts`: the shader (scanlines, vignette, a 1.2% phosphor floor lift) as plain data, switchable without other changes. `createCityRenderer` gained `effects`, `setScene`, `setFrame` and `setCrt`; the scheduler a fourth change reason, `look`; disposal frees the meshes, the composer and its passes.
- Files created/modified: `lib/city/plan/scene-geometry.ts`, `components/city/three/{city-meshes,post-processing,crt-pass}.ts` (new), `components/city/three/city-renderer.ts`, `lib/city/plan/environment-colors.ts`, `lib/city/render-scheduler.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/city/plan/scene-geometry.test.ts`, `tests/unit/components/city/three/{city-meshes,post-processing,crt-pass}.test.ts` (new), `tests/unit/components/city/three/city-renderer.test.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/city/render-scheduler.test.ts`, `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `docs/roadmap-en.md`, `docs/changelog.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`.
- Dependencies added: none (the `three/addons/postprocessing/*` modules ship with `three`).
- Tests: `scene-geometry.test.ts` (9), `crt-pass.test.ts` (5), `city-meshes.test.ts` (16) and `post-processing.test.ts` (8), plus 10 new cases in `city-renderer.test.ts` and one in `render-scheduler.test.ts`; unit with three.js and its addons mocked and typed (no `any`).
- Relevant notes/decisions: bloom half resolution means half of the view size in CSS pixels. Nothing renders on screen until the lazy view (CR6.T8a), so the look (bloom 0.9/0.5/0.15, colors, CRT strength) is a first guess to be tuned by eye then; the draw-call count (6 of the 12 allowed) and the chunk size are checked in CR6.T12. The meshes file was split into helpers to respect the 40-line rule and sits at exactly 200 lines.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — bodies and windows are two instanced meshes, edges one line mesh updated only when the visible floors change, instance counts equal the scene data, bloom at half resolution, the CRT pass can be disabled without other changes.

### CR6.T7 — Ambient layer, floor animations and damaged-floor effect
- Pure, in `lib/city/`: `ambient-plan.ts` (`createAmbientPlan(plan)`, seeded from `plan.seed`: up to 12 car routes along the roads, up to 24 flickering floors, 120 rain drops; `carPosition`, `rainHeight`, `flickerLevel`, `beaconLevel`) and `floor-motion.ts` (the timeline: a new floor drops in 360 ms, a removed floor glitches out in red for 320 ms, the damaged top floor's edges flicker on a 1.7 s cycle; at most 12 floors animate per change; nothing animates on first paint; every animation ends at rest). The scheduler gained an `animating` input and an `animation` frame reason (frames at the display rate while a floor animation runs, none under reduced motion or while hidden).
- Three.js, in `components/city/three/`: `floor-slots.ts` (extracted from `city-meshes.ts`; draws one slot from its static data or from a `SlotLook`: partial floor, shift, glitch, glow; window-band brightness), `floor-animations.ts`, `ambient-layer.ts` + `ambient-motion.ts` (lamps and beacons as one `Points`, signs, rain and cars: four draw calls), `city-labels.ts` (every landmark's `> NAME` drawn once into one texture, quads of one mesh, turned to face the camera), `city-scene.ts` (composes meshes, animations, ambient layer and labels; the renderer's single entry to the city, with `setFrame(frame, now)`, `update(now)`, `orient`, `setReducedMotion`). `city-renderer.ts` now builds a `CityScene`, updates it just before each draw and turns the labels with `cameraAxes`; `labelQuad` in `lib/city/plan/scene-geometry.ts`. The ambient layer gates itself at 30 updates per second; under reduced motion there is no ambient motion or animation (growth, removals and damage still show as static changes); lamps, signs and static beacons are scenery and stay.

### CR6.T8a — Popup City tab: lazy 3D view and accessible summary
- The City tab shows `CityPanel` (`components/city/CityPanel.tsx`, not lazy) instead of `CityCanvas`: a `.city-stage` (full tab width, 17rem tall, `role="img"`), a `Suspense` fallback that keeps its size, the HUD line (`> 120/240 FLOORS`, `aria-hidden`) and a visually hidden `role="status"` summary (floors built of total, buildings standing, tallest building; EN and IT, plural-neutral wording) that changes at milestones (10% steps) and when a new city starts. `React.lazy(() => import('./CityView3D'))` is the only way in: `wxt build` emits a separate `CityView3D` chunk (598 KB, 148 KB gzipped, budget 180) and `WebGLRenderer` appears in no popup, background or content-script bundle.
- `CityView3D.tsx` (default export) reads `selectCityPlan` and `selectVisibleFloors`, memoizes `createScenePlan`, and feeds `renderer.setScene` (plan changes) and `renderer.setFrame` (floors change). `use-city-renderer.ts` owns the lifecycle: renderer created only when there is a plan, with bloom, CRT and the ambient layer; `ResizeObserver` → `resize`, `IntersectionObserver` → `setVisible`, `prefers-reduced-motion` and its changes → `setReducedMotion`, `dispose` on unmount; WebGL failure or a lost context shows a quiet message until CR6.T8b adds the fallback. `use-city-summary.ts` derives the live and the announced summaries. The popup is 32rem wide (it took its width from the 60-column grid before); orbiting stays optional and nothing in the stage takes focus, so there is no keyboard trap.

### CR6.T10 — Export the city image from the 3D view
- `renderer.captureFrame(width, height, format)` (`components/city/three/capture-frame.ts`, wired in `city-renderer.ts`) draws one frame at the requested size and reads it in the same call, so `preserveDrawingBuffer` stays off: pixel ratio 1, canvas, camera and composer fitted without touching the on-screen box, `toDataURL` (JPEG at 0.92), then the view, the ratio and a redraw are restored in a `finally`. It is a `Result` boundary: sizes outside 1–4096 px, a lost context, an unreadable canvas or an empty data URL are errors, never exceptions; it does not count as a rendered frame of the view. The renderer's types moved to `city-renderer.types.ts` (the renderer was at the 200-line limit).
- `exportCityImage(rows, format, capture)` (`lib/city/export-image.ts`) uses the capture at `EXPORT_WIDTH × EXPORT_HEIGHT` (1600 × 900) when given one; with none, or when it fails or throws, it falls back to the text city SVG (the M3.T8 path). `components/city/city-capture.ts` joins the lazy view and the non-lazy button: `CityView3D` registers its renderer's capture while it runs and removes it on unmount (only if still its own), `ExportCityButton` reads it at click time and passes it to the injected exporter (now `(rows, format, capture)`). `JPEG_QUALITY` is exported from `rasterize-image.ts`.
- Files created/modified: `components/city/three/{capture-frame,city-renderer.types}.ts` (new), `components/city/three/city-renderer.ts`, `components/city/city-capture.ts` (new), `components/city/{CityView3D,ExportCityButton}.tsx`, `lib/city/{export-image,rasterize-image}.ts`, `tests/unit/components/city/three/capture-frame.test.ts` and `tests/unit/components/city/city-capture.test.ts` (new), `tests/unit/components/city/three/city-renderer.test.ts`, `tests/unit/components/city/{CityView3D,ExportCityButton}.test.tsx`, `tests/unit/lib/city/export-image.test.ts`, `README.md` (export), `DESIGN.md` (exported image), `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `docs/project-structure.md`, `docs/testing-matrix.md`, `docs/roadmap-en.md`, `docs/changelog.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: 32 new: `capture-frame` (14), `city-capture` (3), 5 in `city-renderer`, 7 in `export-image`, 1 in `ExportCityButton`, 2 in `CityView3D`; unit and component, with three.js mocked and a fake capture host. Full suite: **1407 passing tests across 114 files**; `bun run compile` and ESLint clean; `wxt build` still keeps `WebGLRenderer` out of the popup chunk.
- Relevant notes/decisions: a failed capture falls back to the text city instead of showing an error, which is the declared fallback; the user gets a picture either way. The 3D image has bloom, the CRT pass, the camera as the user left it and the tower labels; the HUD line and the summary are DOM and are not in it. The capture was never run against a real GPU (happy-dom has none): real pixel output, the 1600 × 900 render time and a tainted-canvas case are for CR6.T12's e2e check.
- Acceptance criteria: verified in unit tests — one frame rendered at the chosen size and read in the same call with `preserveDrawingBuffer` off; the export uses the text city without WebGL; errors stay inside the existing export boundary (a `Result`, logged and shown by `ExportCityButton` as before). Docs: README (export), DESIGN.md (exported image).

### CR6.T11 — *(Optional)* Full-city extension page
- `entrypoints/city/` (`index.html`, `main.tsx`, `App.tsx`, `style.css`) builds `city.html`, an unlisted page: the same city as the popup's City tab on a stage that fills the window, with a free camera (all the way round, polar 0.15–1.5 rad, zoom 0.5–4, pan: `FREE_ORBIT_LIMITS` in `lib/city/camera-framing.ts`, selected by `createGlContext(host, free)` through a new `freeCamera` renderer option, threaded through `useCityRenderer`, `CityView3D` and `CityPanel`'s new `full` prop). It reads the persisted state like the popup (`attachStoreSync` on the read-only `useAppStore`) and updates live; it is read-only by construction (§1.3); its ambient layer pauses with the tab through the renderer's existing `visibilitychange` handling. The manifest and the permissions are unchanged; `wxt build` emits `city.html` for Chrome and Firefox MV3, and `WebGLRenderer` still appears only in the `CityView3D` chunk.
- The popup's City tab has a "View full city" pill (`OpenCityPageButton`) that calls `openExtensionPage('/city.html')` (`lib/browser/open-extension-page.ts`: injectable `getUrl`/`createTab`, a `Result`, so no component touches a browser API). The stage styles moved from `entrypoints/popup/App.css` into `components/city/city-panel.css`, imported by `CityPanel`, so the popup and the page share them. EN and IT: `city.openFull`, `city.openError`, `cityPage.title`, `cityPage.hint`.
- Files created/modified: `entrypoints/city/{index.html,main.tsx,App.tsx,style.css}` (new), `lib/browser/open-extension-page.ts` (new), `components/city/{OpenCityPageButton.tsx,city-panel.css}` (new), `components/city/{CityPanel,CityView3D}.tsx`, `components/city/use-city-renderer.ts`, `components/city/three/{city-renderer,city-renderer.types,renderer-context}.ts`, `lib/city/camera-framing.ts`, `entrypoints/popup/{App.tsx,App.css}`, `locales/{en,it}.yml`, tests (`tests/unit/entrypoints/city-page.test.tsx`, `tests/unit/lib/browser/open-extension-page.test.ts`, `tests/unit/components/city/OpenCityPageButton.test.tsx` new; `CityPanel`, `CityView3D`, `city-renderer` and `camera-framing` tests extended), `README.md`, `DESIGN.md` (page layout), `docs/{project-structure,testing-matrix,roadmap-en,changelog}.md`, `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: 19 new: `city-page` (5), `open-extension-page` (3), `OpenCityPageButton` (3), 2 in `camera-framing`, 2 in `city-renderer`, 1 in `CityView3D`, 3 in `CityPanel`. Full suite: **1426 passing tests across 117 files**; `bun run compile` and ESLint clean; `wxt build` and `wxt build -b firefox` both succeed.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the optional card was taken at the developer's request. The build now has a shared 530 KB chunk (store, React) between the popup (174 KB, it was 703 KB) and the page. The page has no keyboard alternative to the pointer camera (OrbitControls' `listenToKeyEvents` was left out) and was never opened in a real browser: live updates across tabs and the hidden-tab pause are for CR6.T12's e2e.
- Acceptance criteria: verified in unit tests and builds — reads the same state through the same selectors and updates live, read-only (a test scans the page's sources for writes); the ambient layer pauses with the hidden tab (the renderer's scheduler, already tested in CR6.T6a/T7); Chrome and Firefox builds emit the page. Docs: README, `docs/project-structure.md`, DESIGN.md (page layout).

### CR6.T12 — Performance verification and e2e
- `tests/e2e/city-3d.spec.ts` (Chromium, the built extension, a fresh profile per test, SwiftShader by default, `E2E_GPU=1` for the real GPU; `tests/e2e/setup.ts` gained the GL flags and `extensionIdOf`) proves: the Timer tab never requests the three.js chunk and the City tab does, with a canvas and no console errors; reduced motion with nothing growing keeps the render counter flat; the ambient layer never exceeds 30 fps; ten tab switches leave one live WebGL context (none off the City tab); two sessions give different plan fingerprints and a reopened one the same; a capture on a real GPU is a 1600 × 900 PNG and JPEG with lit pixels; the full-city page draws and stops when told it is hidden; and the budgets are measured and written to `test-results/city-3d-metrics.json`. Debug-only hooks behind `?debug=render`: `components/city/three/debug-stats.ts` (`data-render-count`, `data-render-ms`, `data-draw-calls`, `data-created-at`, `data-first-frame-at`; a `debug` renderer option) and `components/city/use-city-debug.ts` (`data-plan-hash`, `window.__buildItCapture`).
- **Measured (whole city standing; RTX 3060 Laptop GPU / SwiftShader):** three.js chunk 146.4 KB gzipped (budget 180) — within; draw calls 11 (budget 12) — within; ambient 78 / 62–73 frames in 3 s (≤ 30 fps) — within; renders per second flat with reduced motion — within; about 1 ms of main-thread time per render; one live context — within; **first City frame 489–527 ms on the GPU and 516–736 ms in software, against a 200 ms budget: OVER BUDGET, reported.** A CPU profile shows about 270 ms of idle waiting (chunk, GPU process, next frame), about 110 ms in one unidentified function of the shared chunk and only about 60 ms of JavaScript in the three.js chunk; nothing was changed for it here (options are in `city-3d.md` §8).
- **A real bug the e2e found, fixed here:** leaving the City tab crashed the popup (`TypeError: t?.dispose is not a function`, shown by the error boundary) since CR6.T8a. `disposeScene` (`renderer-context.ts`, CR6.T6a) called `dispose` on every property of a material, and a rendered material has a `_listeners` object whose `dispose` key is an array. It now calls only what is a function. Regression test on real three.js objects (`renderer-context.test.ts`, 3 tests), red before the fix and green after; the mocked unit tests of CR6.T6a–T8a could not see it.
- Files created/modified: `tests/e2e/{city-3d.spec.ts (new),setup.ts}`, `components/city/three/{debug-stats.ts (new),city-renderer.ts,city-renderer.types.ts,renderer-context.ts}`, `components/city/{use-city-debug.ts (new),use-city-renderer.ts,CityView3D.tsx}`, `tests/unit/components/city/three/{debug-stats,renderer-context}.test.ts` (new), `tests/unit/components/city/three/city-renderer.test.ts`, `tests/unit/components/city/CityView3D.test.tsx`, `README.md` (e2e command), `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md` (measured numbers), `docs/{testing-matrix,roadmap-en,changelog}.md`, `.serena/memories/core.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: unit +14 across 2 new files (`debug-stats` 5, `renderer-context` 3, 3 in `city-renderer`, 3 in `CityView3D`): **1440 passing tests across 119 files**; `bun run compile` and ESLint (including `tests/e2e`) clean. e2e: **9 passing** (the 8 new plus the existing placeholder), run twice with no flake; `bun run build` first.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the two values over budget or not met as written are reported, not hidden: the first-frame time (above), and the hidden-tab check, which the card words as "pauses when the tab is hidden" — this display has no window manager so Chromium never reports a background tab hidden, and the e2e instead sets `document.hidden` and dispatches `visibilitychange`, which exercises the renderer's real behavior but not the browser's own visibility. The e2e's time budget fails the run only on a real GPU (`E2E_GPU=1`); software GL records an `over-budget` annotation.
- Acceptance criteria: partial — all checks pass and the numbers are recorded, but the first-frame budget is missed (about 2.5×; resolved afterwards by PERF22, see its entry) and the hidden-tab behavior is verified by emulation. Docs: `city-3d.md` §8 and the e2e row of the testing matrix.

## PR reviews

### PR6.T2 — `calculateScore` treats a non-finite count as `'bad'`

- `calculateScore(builtCharacters, total)` now returns `'bad'` for any non-finite `builtCharacters` (`NaN`, `+Infinity`, `-Infinity`): the guard is `!Number.isFinite(builtCharacters)` in place of `Number.isNaN`. A finite count above `total` is still clamped to `'excellent'`, and the invalid-`total` check is unchanged. The doc comment is rewritten to match. `calculatePopulation` is not touched, so both functions now treat a non-finite count as invalid input.
- Files created/modified: `lib/score/calculate-score.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/score/calculate-score.test.ts`, `docs/changelog.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/unit/lib/score/calculate-score.test.ts` — `+Infinity` moved from the "clamps a count above the total" test into the invalid-count cases. Written red first (the new case failed with `'excellent'`), then green after the change. Run: `bun run test -- tests/unit/lib/score tests/unit/store/score-slice.test.ts` → **133 passing tests across 6 files**; full `bun run test` → **874 passing tests across 78 files**; `bun run compile` clean.
- Relevant notes/decisions: this fixes the contract only. Per the PR6.T2 card, a non-finite count cannot reach the function today because `setBuiltCharacters` normalizes the value first. The CR3.T3 and CR3.T7 cards that describe `+Infinity` → `excellent` are history and are not edited.
- Acceptance criteria: verified — `NaN`, `±Infinity` → `'bad'`; `900` → `'excellent'`; all CR3.T3 reference boundaries unchanged and green.
- Commit: `6a86fd5 fix(score): treat a non-finite built count as bad`.

### PR10.T2 — Extract the overlay host watcher into `lib/browser/host-watcher.ts` (F4)

- New `createHostWatcher({ targets, isLost, onLost }): { disconnect }` in `lib/browser/host-watcher.ts` (27 lines). One `MutationObserver` observes every node in `targets` (`childList`, `subtree`) and calls `onLost()` only when `isLost()` is true; `disconnect()` stops it and is safe to call twice. `startBlockedOverlay` now builds its watcher with it: `targets: [document.documentElement]`, `isLost: () => ctx.isValid && host() === undefined`, `onLost: unmount(); evaluate();`. `entrypoints/blocked-overlay.content.tsx` no longer references `MutationObserver`. **No behavior change.**
- Files created/modified: `lib/browser/host-watcher.ts` (new), `tests/unit/lib/browser/host-watcher.test.ts` (new), `entrypoints/blocked-overlay.content.tsx`, `docs/changelog.md`.

Written red first (the module did not exist). `blocked-overlay.test.ts` and `blocked-overlay-hardening.test.ts` pass unchanged. `bun run test` → **2123 passing tests across 142 files**; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` clean.

### PR10.T3 — Recognize the overlay host by a private registry, not by its id (F1)

The doc comment of `startBlockedOverlay` now says the host is found among the elements the module created.

### PR10.T4 — Remount the overlay when the page empties its shadow root (F2)

- `watchHost(shadow)` now passes `targets: [document.documentElement, shadow]` to `createHostWatcher`, and `isLost` is `ctx.isValid && (host() === undefined || shadow.querySelector('.blocked-overlay') === null)`. Emptying the open shadow root (`host.shadowRoot.replaceChildren()`) therefore counts as losing the overlay: the watcher unmounts and `evaluate` mounts a fresh one. React's own renders keep `.blocked-overlay`, so they do not trigger it. The shadow root stays `mode: 'open'` (decision D1). `unmount` now wraps `root.unmount()` in a `try`/`catch` that logs with `logger.error` and carries on, so the host is still removed when the page already took React's nodes away.
- Files created/modified: `entrypoints/blocked-overlay.content.tsx`, `tests/integration/content/blocked-overlay-hardening.test.ts`, `docs/changelog.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `blocked-overlay-hardening.test.ts` (+2, integration, happy-dom), in *"the page cannot get rid of the overlay"* — mounts again when the page empties the shadow root (exactly one host with a shadow root, showing `facebook.com`); does not remount on its own renders (after a trusted "Back to work" click the same host stays, shows the encouragement, one `sendAttempt` call). The first was written red and failed; the second passed before the fix and guards against a false remount. happy-dom does observe a `ShadowRoot`. `bun run test` → **2128 passing tests across 142 files**; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` clean.
- Relevant notes/decisions: only a missing `.blocked-overlay` counts as a loss. Hiding or restyling the overlay from script stays out of scope (M6.T4 card). The entrypoint file is now 324 lines. The catch branch (`root.unmount()` throwing) has no test of its own (not checked whether the happy-dom scenario reaches it). Builds and e2e were not run in this task.
- Acceptance criteria: verified.
- Commit: `43b27f6 fix(overlay): remount on emptied shadow root`.

### PR10.T5 — Pure predicate for extension-page senders (F3)

- New `isExtensionPageSender(sender, extensionOrigin): boolean` in `lib/messaging/extension-page-sender.ts`. It returns `true` only when `sender.tab` is `undefined`, `sender.url` is a string that starts with `extensionOrigin`, and `extensionOrigin` is non-empty and ends with `/` (an empty prefix matches every URL; a prefix without the slash also matches `chrome-extension://<id>.evil/`). The origin is a parameter, so the module never calls `browser.*`. A doc comment gives the why: content scripts share a renderer process with the page, so `sender.id` alone does not exclude them. No production caller uses it yet (PR10.T5b wires it).
- Files created/modified: `lib/messaging/extension-page-sender.ts` (new), `tests/unit/lib/messaging/extension-page-sender.test.ts` (new), `docs/changelog.md`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `extension-page-sender.test.ts` (+8, unit) — popup URL without a tab accepted; the same URL with a tab, a content-script sender, a sender with no `url`, another extension id, a look-alike prefix, an empty origin and an origin without a trailing `/` all rejected. Written red first (the module did not exist). `bun run test` → **2136 passing tests across 143 files**; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` clean.
- Relevant notes/decisions: by the card, an extension page shown in a tab is rejected as well (it has a `sender.tab`). Builds and e2e were not run in this task.
- Acceptance criteria: verified.
- Commit: `4c5a415 feat(messaging): add extension page sender check`. Amended by PR10.T5b: the `sender.tab` condition was dropped (decision D6), so an extension page shown in a tab is accepted.

### PR10.T5b — Accept mutations only from extension pages (F3)

- In `startBackground`, every `MUTATION_TYPES` receiver now checks `isExtensionPageSender(sender, extensionOrigin)` first (`extensionOrigin` is `browser.runtime.getURL('/')`, computed once). A mutation from any other sender is ignored before `startup.run`, with `busLog.warn('ignored a mutation from outside an extension page', { type })` (the type only, never a payload field). The passive `MESSAGE_TYPES` (`TIMER_TICK`, `SITE_BLOCKED_ATTEMPT`) are not affected, so the overlay's attempt from a tab is still accepted.
- **Decision D6 changed PR10.T5.** The review and the first version of the predicate also required "no `sender.tab`". With it, 7 e2e tests failed: the e2e suite opens `popup.html` with `page.goto`, so the popup has a tab. Confirmed by removing the condition, rebuilding and re-running the sound specs. The predicate now checks only the URL prefix (and the origin shape). A content script's `sender.url` is the web page's, so it is still rejected; an extension page shown in a tab is extension code and is accepted.
- `installExtensionSender` (test helper) now delivers `{ id, url: getURL('/popup.html') }`, like the popup, so the existing tests keep sending mutations through `sendMessage`. `deliverAs` and `sendFromTab` are unchanged.
- Files created/modified: `entrypoints/background.ts`, `lib/messaging/extension-page-sender.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/messaging/extension-page-sender.test.ts`, `tests/helpers/fake-extension-sender.ts`, `tests/integration/messaging/message-hardening.test.ts`, `tests/integration/messaging/bus.test.ts` (its sender assertion now expects the popup `url`), `docs/pr-review/pr-10-review.md` (D6 and the T5/T5b cards), `docs/changelog.md`. That is more than the 4 files of the card, accepted by the user because of D6 and the helper side effect.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `message-hardening.test.ts` (+3, integration) — a mutation from a content script (a tab with the page's URL) and one from a sender with no `url` are rejected, the timer stays `idle` and the warn record carries `{ type: 'TIMER_STARTED' }` only (two `describe.each` rows); an extension page shown in a tab is accepted. Written red first (three failed, including the tab case before D6 turned it into an acceptance). In `extension-page-sender.test.ts` the "shown in a tab" case now expects `true`. `bun run test` → **2139 passing tests across 143 files**; `bun run compile`, `bun run lint`, `bun run build`, `bun run build:firefox` and `bun run e2e` (13 tests) are clean. `malus.test.ts` passes unchanged.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the Firefox popup's sender was **not checked in a real browser** (the e2e suite is Chromium-only). If Firefox gives the popup a `sender.url` outside the extension origin, its mutations would be ignored; to check by hand before release.
- Acceptance criteria: verified.

## Open issues and points to clarify

1. **Roadmap §4 open point** not to be anticipated (YAGNI): behavior beyond grid capacity. *(The exact `distractionRatio` formula is resolved by CR3: the ratio path was removed and the score derives from `builtCharacters`.)*
2. **Resolved (M3.T5)** — `framer-motion` was not installed; it is now a runtime dependency (`^14.0.0`) used by `CityCell.tsx` and mocked in `tests/setup.ts`.
3. **`store-analysis.md` note (M2.T1)** — the analysis predicted that adding action functions to a slice would break `PersistedState = Omit<AppState, keyof UiSlice>`; the type is now declared explicitly (`store/index.ts`), so a future action cannot leak into storage.
4. **Manifest permissions are not auto-detected by WXT** — they must be declared in `wxt.config.ts` (`storage`/`alarms` added in the M2.T1 fix, `declarativeNetRequest` in M2.T7, `host_permissions: ['<all_urls>']` in M2.T8, `tabs` in M2.T10). `fakeBrowser` does **not** mask a missing permission the way a hand-written mock would: it exposes the API *namespaces* but leaves every method unimplemented (each throws `MockNotImplementedError`), so a background test that reaches an unimplemented API fails at that boundary rather than silently succeeding. What the suite still cannot verify is the manifest itself — `fakeBrowser` never reads it, so a permission declared in code but missing from `wxt.config.ts` stays invisible to vitest; only a real-browser run (dev/e2e) can catch that. (Corrected during the DNR test-noise fix: `declarativeNetRequest` is now backed by the shared in-memory fake in `tests/helpers/fake-declarative-net-request.ts`, installed from `tests/setup.ts`.)
5. **Resolved (M6.T3) — `urlFilter` escaping.** `buildDnrRules` now only interpolates domains that pass `isCanonicalDomain` (`[a-z0-9.-]`); anything else is dropped, since `urlFilter` has no escape for `*`/`|`/`^`. See the M6.T3 entry.
6. **Resolved (M3.T3b) — timer commands from the popup are now routed to the background.** The popup's `useAppStore` is read-only (M1.T9), so `TimerControls` (M2.T3) used to call `startTimer()`/`pauseTimer()`/`resetTimer()` on its own in-memory copy and schedule an alarm on its own provider, while the background — which owns the timer, the alarms and the DNR rules (M2.T7) — never learned about it. M3.T3b added `TIMER_STARTED`/`TIMER_PAUSED`/`TIMER_RESET` to `MutationMessage` (with empty payloads: the background mints the session id) and handles them in the background's mutation path (`lib/messaging/apply-mutation.ts`), so the transition is applied to the background's timer slice and the alarm lives on the background's provider. `TimerControls` now invokes the `request*` actions, which only send the mutation; the popup converges through the M1.T8 bridge.
7. **Overlay reachability is bounded by the content-script injection rules (M2.T8)** — with `main_frame` no longer blocked (see the M2.T8 entry), the top-level navigation gate is the overlay itself. Pages where content scripts cannot be injected (Chrome Web Store, the PDF viewer, other extensions' pages, and, in Firefox, some restricted URLs) therefore cannot be gated at all: the user reaches them with no overlay and no malus. This is inherent to the content-script approach (it is why M3.T6's manual cross-browser pass matters) and is accepted rather than worked around; a hard-block alternative would need an extension interstitial page with a one-time allow, which is a different design. Also inherent: at `document_idle` the page paints briefly before the overlay covers it.
8. **Resolved (M6.T1) — `SITE_BLOCKED_ATTEMPT` and the sender.** The runtime guards, the `sender.id` check on every receiver and the binding of the overlay's answer to `sender.tab.url` are in place; no payload `tabId` is read anywhere.
9. **Resolved (M3.T3b) — the popup now emits the growth ticks.** The background consumes `TIMER_TICK` and grows the city through the session-seeded engine (M2.T15), but nothing *sent* it: `TimerDisplay`/`TimerControls` (M2.T3) did not run the fine-grained 2s timer and `App.tsx` (M3.T4) was not composed. M3.T3b added `lib/timer/tick-emitter.ts` (a dumb periodic clock with an injected scheduler) and `components/timer/use-timer-tick-emitter.ts` (starts it while `status === 'running'`, stops it on status change/unmount), called from `App.tsx`. **Ownership decision**: the popup owns the emitter — it is the context active while the user watches the city, and the only one that can be woken on a 2s cadence (an MV3 worker is suspended between events and `browser.alarms` cannot tick faster than 60s, M1.T5). **Remaining limitation**: the city therefore only grows while the popup is open; the countdown is unaffected (persisted by the background's alarm, M2.T2). A background-owned emitter would need a different mechanism (e.g. an offscreen document) and is not scheduled by any card. **Resolved (I006)**: the background now owns a wall-clock checkpoint (`timer.lastAccountedAt`) and a 60s progress alarm, so growth catches up without the popup; no offscreen document is needed because the reward is derived from elapsed time rather than sampled ticks.
10. **Obsolete since CR6.T9a (the ASCII city, its growth engine and `city.buildings` were removed; the 3D city is derived from the plan and `builtCharacters`)** — ~~**`city.buildings` is still never populated; the malus targets the globally topmost cell (M2.T16)** — M2.T16 wired the malus (M2.T10) to the store and the tab distraction tracker to the background (resolving the former issue #9), but no task writes `city.buildings` (`resetCityForNewSession` keeps it `{}`) and the growth engine (M2.T12) grows by focus time, not by domain: a domain → building locator is therefore not derivable without changing the growth engine. `applyMalus`'s documented rule (remove the topmost occupied cell) is used as-is; per-domain destruction and the per-cell domain color anticipated by `CityCanvas` (M2.T14) remain deferred. A separate task would be needed to attach a domain identity to each building.~~
11. **Resolved — five roadmap detail cards were missing.** M2.T13b, M2.T18, M2.T19, M3.T7 and M3.T8 appeared only in the §1.2 summary table, while §1.5/§1.6 jumped over them: the tasks had no authoritative acceptance criteria, and M2.T13b/M2.T18/M2.T19 had been implemented by deriving the contract from the summary row plus neighbouring notes. **Root cause**: the gap predates the agent work — the original Italian `docs/roadmap.md` (removed in `bcd8189`) already had these five rows without cards, so the English translation preserved an upstream omission rather than introducing it; no commit ever contained a card for them (`git log -S "#### M2.T18"` is empty, and the same holds for M3.T7/M3.T8). **Fix**: cards added for all five, in numeric order (M2.T13b between M2.T13/M2.T14; M2.T18/M2.T19 between M2.T17/M2.T20; M3.T7/M3.T8 between M3.T6/M3.T9); the summary table and the card set now match (58 ↔ 58); two stale cross-references were corrected (M4.T4's dependency pointed at M3.T7 and now points at M3.T4; M1.T1's `SessionSummary` note now names M3.T7 as the consumer). The roadmap file itself has no §3.3 test/task mapping section — that table lives in the agent instructions (`.github/copilot-instructions.md`, mirrored under `.continue/rules/`) and is **still out of sync**: it has no row for M2.T13b/M2.T18/M2.T19/M3.T8, and its M3.T7 row still reads `M3.T7 (mobile layout)` — a task that exists nowhere in the roadmap, since M3.T7 is `SessionHistory`. Tracked as issue #16.
12. **Resolved (M3.T3c, live update added in I004) — population upstream wiring.** `calculatePopulation` (pure) and `PopulationCounter`/`setPopulation` (store + UI) existed, but nothing computed a session's focused minutes and called `setPopulation`. M3.T3c derives the population from the focus time the growth tracker (M2.T15) accumulates — the tracker now also exposes `getFocusedMs()`, reusing the same running-only baseline as growth, so the population always agrees with the city that was actually built — and writes it with `setPopulation` at session completion. **I004** additionally wires the same computation into every 2s tick, so the counter evolves live during the session instead of jumping at completion, and relocates the accumulator into the persisted `timer.focusedMs` so it survives a service worker restart. **Formula change (I004)**: `calculatePopulation` now takes **milliseconds** and adds **+5 per completed 2-second tick**, replacing the M2.T18 card's milestone rule (+5 per house at 5′, +15 per floor at 10′/20′, max 35) — the milestone version made the counter jump only once every five minutes, which the user reported as not working. **Distraction penalty (I004)**: the same formula inverted, **−5 per completed 2-second tick** while a blocked site is open, with focus time no longer accruing during those stretches (so the net effect is a decrease, and the city pauses); the total is clamped at zero. The roadmap card still describes the old rule and is intentionally left unedited; this entry is the record of the divergence. **Remaining limitation**: a session whose popup was never open has `focusedMs = 0` and therefore population `0`, consistent with the empty city it built (the M3.T3b emitter limitation, issue #9). **Resolved (I006)**: a running session's progress is now credited from the persisted wall-clock checkpoint on every background wake-up, so the city and population no longer depend on the popup.
13. **Resolved (M3.T3c) — session history now has a runtime producer.** The `sessionHistory` slice, its pure helper and persistence existed, and M3.T7 added the display UI (`SessionHistory`), but nothing called `recordSession`: the `SESSION_ENDED` message (M1.T1) was never emitted and its background listener was the shared placeholder. M3.T3c replaced that placeholder with `createSessionCompletion`, which fires on the session alarm, computes the population, transitions the timer to `'completed'` and records the `SessionSummary` through `recordSession` before emitting `SESSION_ENDED`. **Note**: the emitted message still has no consumer — M3.T4 is the intended one.
14. **Resolved (M3.T3), superseded by CR1 — M2.T20 UI feedback** — `components/blocklist/SiteList.tsx` (M3.T3) rendered the explicit feedback: a duplicate add showed "`<domain>` is already in the `<list>`", and adding a domain present in the opposite list was reported as "Moved `<domain>` from the `<other list>`", backed by the M2.T20 store-level mutual exclusion. **Superseded by CR1.T1/CR1.T2/CR1.T5a (allowlist removal)**: the allowlist UI, its locale keys and the move feedback are removed, the mutation protocol no longer has a `list` discriminator, and the store no longer models a second list at all — M2.T20 is obsolete.
15. **Resolved (M3.T3) — entry-rejection feedback transport** — as anticipated, `SiteList` validates the input on the popup side with `normalizeEntry` before dispatching, so an unparseable input or a bare public suffix shows `INVALID_URL_MESSAGE` ("Enter a valid URL") and is never sent; the fire-and-forget `BLOCKLIST_ADD_SITE` limitation is thus avoided without adding a mutation ack.
16. **The test/task mapping table in the agent instructions is out of sync with the roadmap** — `.github/copilot-instructions.md` §3.3 (mirrored in `.continue/rules/`) lists neither M2.T13b, M2.T18, M2.T19 nor M3.T8, and its `M3.T7 (mobile layout)` row refers to a task that does not exist in the roadmap (M3.T7 is `SessionHistory`; no mobile-layout task is defined anywhere). This is the same drift documented in issue #11 and is left untouched because the instructions are the agent's own contract rather than project documentation — updating them is a separate decision.
17. **Resolved (M3.T4) — `App.tsx` is now translated.** The i18n infrastructure, `locales/en.yml` and every implemented popup/overlay component were internationalized, but `entrypoints/popup/App.tsx` was still the WXT starter, so its placeholder copy was not routed through `i18n.t`. M3.T4 replaced the starter with the real composition and every string in `App.tsx`/`PopupTabs.tsx` now resolves through `i18n.t(...)`, with the new `tabs` keys added to both `locales/en.yml` and `locales/it.yml` (the M3.T10 drift test stays green). This closes the remaining work of M3.T9's "all popup strings" criterion.
18. **Obsolete since CR6.T9a (the ASCII city, its growth engine and `city.buildings` were removed; the 3D city is derived from the plan and `builtCharacters`)** — ~~**The drawn city is scattered characters, not recognizable buildings (I002, open — description now stale)** — the growth engine picks each character by global tick index (`glyphFor` flattens the composed building and takes `characters.charAt(tick % characters.length)`) while `nextFreeSlot` picks the cell independently, so the character and its position are unrelated and the composer's building never materializes. The three layers are also absolutely stacked at the same coordinates, so the scattered glyphs superimpose into noise. No remaining task owns this: M2.T12b only adds decoration on top of the structure (and is itself unwired), and M4.T2 (sprite-sheet renderer) is skipped for now. Tracked in `docs/issue/I002-scattered-city-draw.md`. **Update (I003)**: `glyphFor` no longer exists in `lib/city/growth-engine.ts` — the function was replaced by the lot-based placement strategy (`planLayer`/`nextFreeCellInLot`/`glyphAt`) in `3c03dea`, which anchors each structure in its own lot and walks it bottom-up, so the described character/position mismatch is gone and the built extension now renders recognizable buildings with intact roofs. The issue file and this entry therefore describe code that is no longer there; what survives of the *symptom* is the layer stacking, which `CityCanvas` compensates for by fading the layers by depth (0.4/0.75/1) rather than overlaying them at the same coordinates. Left open pending a decision to re-verify and close it.~~
19. **Obsolete since CR6.T9a (the ASCII city, its growth engine and `city.buildings` were removed; the 3D city is derived from the plan and `builtCharacters`)** — ~~**The city still freezes for the last ~10 minutes of a session (I003)** — with the floor ceiling raised and the grid at 60 × 20 the city saturates at **15.1 minutes** of the default 25-minute session (measured), down from 6.7 minutes before. **72 × 24** would fill at 24.2 minutes and match the session length, and still fits the popup cap (~530 px at `0.85rem`). Left as a follow-up rather than applied: the size is a single constant pair in `lib/city/city-size.ts`, so changing it is a one-line edit plus a re-baseline of the saturation figures in the I003 entry.~~
20. **Obsolete since CR6.T9a (the ASCII city, its growth engine and `city.buildings` were removed; the 3D city is derived from the plan and `builtCharacters`)** — ~~**`GROWTH_TICK_MS` is temporarily set to `20` in the working tree (not committed)** — the production value is `2_000` (one character per 2 seconds, roadmap M2.T12). The `20` in the current working tree is a local experiment to make the city draw faster; it makes 10 existing integration/unit tests fail, because `lib/timer/tick-emitter.ts` derives `TICK_INTERVAL_MS` from this constant and those tests assert the 2s cadence by name (verified: the whole suite is green again at `2_000`). The comment above the constant still says "one character of city per 2 seconds", which contradicts the value. **Must be reverted before committing.**~~

21. **Resolved (CR6.T13) — `bun run lint` exited non-zero on installed skill scripts.** ESLint reported 5 parsing errors in `.claude/skills/impeccable/scripts/*.js` (skill install commit `1895c37`); `.claude/**` (and the generated `test-results/**`, `playwright-report/**`) are now in the ESLint ignores and `bun run lint` exits 0.

22. **Resolved (PERF22) — the first City frame missed its budget (CR6.T12)** — opening the City tab takes about 490–527 ms to the first frame on an NVIDIA RTX 3060 Laptop GPU and 516–736 ms on software GL, against the 200 ms budget of `docs/features-analysis/city-3d.md` §8. About 440 ms of it is before the renderer exists and 50 ms after; a CPU profile shows mostly main-thread idle (chunk, GPU process, next frame), one unidentified 110 ms function in the shared chunk and only about 60 ms of JavaScript in the three.js chunk. Options (none done): one WebGL2 context instead of a probe plus the renderer, build the scene before the first frame, warm the chunk (conflicts with "the Timer tab never requests it"). To be decided by the developer, or accepted by raising the budget; the e2e fails on this only with `E2E_GPU=1`. **Update (2026-11-17, PERF22):** the unidentified 110 ms was the WebGL2 probe `hasWebGl2()` (about 160 ms, synchronous, before any paint), and the largest cost was React 19 holding the `Suspense` reveal for about 300 ms after the loading fallback. The probe's context is now reused by the renderer, the probe runs after the first paint, and the chunk loads with a plain dynamic `import()`: the e2e first 3D frame on the GPU is **170–223 ms (was 446–543 ms)**, so the 200 ms budget is met about half the time and missed by about 20 ms otherwise. The developer accepted this and the budget was raised to 250 ms (e2e constant and `city-3d.md` §8): resolved.

23. **Resolved (M5.T13) — Firefox autoplay of the session sounds.** The Firefox background is an event page that plays through `AudioContext` directly (M5.T13), and Firefox may block audio in a page that has had no user gesture, so it was unknown whether the start and end sounds are audible there. The Chrome side was verified in M5.T20 (the offscreen page's `AudioContext` runs and builds the sound). The Firefox side was checked by hand by the project owner on the Firefox build (`bun run build:firefox`, loaded in Firefox, a session started): the result was reported as OK, so no workaround was needed and none was added.

### PR5.T1 — Gate background mutations on the startup hydration (F1)
- The background holds every cross-context mutation until its startup work has settled (the `ready` of `startBackground`: rehydration, tracker seed, timer restore, the catch-up account and the rules sync), then runs the queued ones in arrival order. The gate is `createStartupGate` (`lib/messaging/startup-gate.ts`): it opens once, runs each queued task in isolation (a failing one is logged and the rest still run), and also opens when the startup fails, so no message waits forever.
- The CR5 `addsSites` pre-step and the `TIMER_PAUSED`/`TIMER_RESET` `growthTick.account()` call now run inside the gated task, on the hydrated state. The mutation handler itself is unchanged; the passive `TIMER_TICK` and `SITE_BLOCKED_ATTEMPT` listeners are not gated.
- Files created/modified: `lib/messaging/startup-gate.ts` (new), `entrypoints/background.ts` (the mutation body extracted to `applyMutation`, the gate opened after `ready`), `tests/integration/background/blocklist-lock.test.ts` (describe `blocklist lock before the startup hydration (PR5.T1)`: the red test is green as written, plus four new cases; the CR2 case is renamed "rejects a mutation received after a restored running session is reconciled"), `tests/unit/lib/messaging/startup-gate.test.ts` (new), `docs/pr-review/pr-5-review.md` (progress and status).
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/integration/background/blocklist-lock.test.ts` (21 tests in the file, 5 of them in the PR5.T1 describe: the early remove is rejected, storage never holds `idle`, the early add is kept; an early add while running credits the stretch since the checkpoint; an early pause applies to the hydrated session; an idle add then remove leaves an empty list; an idle edit during an in-flight sync rehydration is applied; a failed startup read releases the queued add); `tests/unit/lib/messaging/startup-gate.test.ts` (5 tests, unit: runs at once when created open, holds then runs in arrival order, opens on rejection, a task queued while draining keeps the order, a throwing task is logged and does not stop the ones behind it). Full run: `bun run test` → **1493 passing tests** across 122 files; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` clean.
- Relevant notes/decisions: the gate is closed only while the store's own startup hydration is pending. `persist.hasHydrated()` is read once, when the gate is created, to pick the initial state; it is never checked again, because sync rehydrations reset it. A store already hydrated when `startBackground` starts (an injected one, as the M1.T10 mutation-bus tests use) starts open, so those tests keep their synchronous path and stay unchanged. The sync-rehydration case uses a direct write to `storage.local`, like the M1.T8 sync tests.
- Acceptance criteria: verified against the PR5.T1 card (amended M1.T10/CR2.T2/CR5.T2 rules): the red test is green as written; the early ADD, pause, idle add-then-remove, sync-rehydration and failed-startup cases are covered; the M1.T10, CR2, CR5, M2.T2 and the other suites in the full run are green unchanged.
- Commit: `9c07f5d fix(PR5): gate mutations until startup ends`.

### PR5.T2 — Explicit blocklist branch and exhaustive switch (F2, F3)
- `lib/messaging/messages.types.ts` declares the two mutation families as data: `BLOCKLIST_MUTATION_TYPES` (the six blocklist and `PRESET_*` types) and `TIMER_MUTATION_TYPES` (the four timer commands). `MUTATION_TYPES` is now composed from them, so the lists cannot drift. It also exports `BlocklistMutationMessage` and the guard `isBlocklistMutation(message)`.
- `createMutationHandler` declares its return type and is about 30 lines. The blocklist branch (CR2/CR5 lock, malformed-payload drop, `applyBlocklistMutation`, identity check) moved into the private `handleBlocklistMutation`. The lock no longer lives in a catch-all `default:`: the handler first asks `isBlocklistMutation`, then switches over the four timer commands and ends with `message satisfies never`, so a mutation type that is added but not handled fails `bun run compile`. Checked by removing the `TIMER_RESET` case: `tsc` reports `TimerResetMessage` does not satisfy `never`.
- No behavior change for any delivered message. The only difference is for a type outside the registry, which used to fall into the lock branch and is now ignored; the bus never delivers one.
- Files created/modified: `lib/messaging/messages.types.ts`, `lib/messaging/apply-mutation.ts` (180 lines), `lib/messaging/blocklist-payload.ts` (`BlocklistMutation` is now an alias of `BlocklistMutationMessage`, so the six names exist once), `tests/integration/messaging/mutation-bus.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: `tests/integration/messaging/mutation-bus.test.ts` (+12 tests, integration, describe `mutation type registry (PR5.T2)`): the six blocklist types, `MUTATION_TYPES` equals them plus the four timer commands with no duplicate, and `isBlocklistMutation` classifies each of the ten types. Written red first (the exports did not exist). Every existing mutation-bus, CR2, CR4.T4 and CR5.T2 test passes unchanged. Full run: `bun run test` → **1505 passing tests** across 122 files; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` clean.
- Acceptance criteria: verified against the PR5.T2 card.
- Commit: — (to be committed).

Each file spies on `fakeBrowser.runtime.sendMessage` (the module-level store sends mutations through the bus) and records the message types.
- Files modified: `tests/unit/components/blocklist/SiteList.test.tsx`, `PresetPicker.test.tsx`, `SavedPresets.test.tsx` (the last one tests the saved-preset controls of `PresetPicker`).

Positive controls keep the spy honest: an add while running sends exactly one `BLOCKLIST_ADD_SITE`, switching an unapplied preset on sends exactly one `PRESET_TOGGLE`. Full run: `bun run test` → **1509 passing tests** across 122 files; `bun run compile` and `bun run lint` clean.

### PR7.T3 — Require the `user:` prefix on saved preset ids
- `isUserPresetId(id)` (the prefix with something after it) is added to `lib/blocking/saved-presets.ts` and used by the `PRESET_SAVE` check in `lib/messaging/blocklist-payload.ts` and by `saveUserPreset`, so a forged message can no longer save a preset under an id like `work`.
- Files created/modified: `lib/blocking/saved-presets.ts`, `lib/messaging/blocklist-payload.ts`, and the tests `tests/unit/lib/blocking/saved-presets.test.ts`, `tests/unit/lib/messaging/blocklist-payload.test.ts`, `tests/integration/messaging/mutation-bus.test.ts`.
- Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: +10 (helper cases, the message check, `saveUserPreset`, the bus dropping a forged id), written red first.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. Commit: `f510f87 fix(presets): require the user prefix on saved ids`.

### PR7.T4 — Add a reset for the visible-floors cache
- `resetVisibleCache()` in `store/city.selectors.ts` clears the module-level cache that holds the fall history (I015), so a test starts without the history of an earlier one. Production behavior is unchanged; the reset is not exported from the `store` barrel.
- Files created/modified: `store/city.selectors.ts`, `tests/unit/store/city-selectors.test.ts`. Dependencies added: none.
- Tests: +1, written red first; the selector tests now reset before each test.
- Acceptance criteria: verified. Commit: `091a691 test(city): add a reset for the floors cache`.

## How to re-run the checks

**Important**: execute `bun run build` for Firefox as well as for Chrome

```bash
bun run test          # vitest (unit + integration + component)
bun run compile       # tsc --noEmit
bun run lint          # clean (M0.T2 issue resolved)
bun run build:firefox # Firefox build (WXT)
bun run build         # Chrome build (WXT)
```
